# Finished proposals

Proposals from [BIBLE.md](BIBLE.md)'s "Next up" list that became stories,
oldest first within each shelf, with the numbers each story was built on.
Read them before proposing, so a new story does not repeat a setting or a
calculation. `node bible_done.mjs` moves new Done entries here.

## Hearth & Horizon

- *(Done: Not Enough Ones, Saint Isidore the Farmer (invented), Wexley (invented), Iowa, 10 Feb to 2 Mar 2053; text only.)* Benford first-digit screen misapplied to a small parish: 500 payments ($123,180.62), 200 of them nine fixed amounts ($45 x90 organist/plowing/mowing, $62 x52 bulletin, $410 x26, $2,140 x12, $89.99 x12, $2,960 x4, $68.50 x4), so ones 20.0% vs 30.1, fours 30.2% vs 9.7, MAD 0.0638 against Nigrini's 0.015; the 300 variable lines give MAD 0.0203 but chi-square 11.6 on 8 df (p 0.17), and a true Benford sample of 300 exceeds 0.015 about 34% of the time (500: 7%); the duplicate test finds a $1,184 boiler invoice paid by check (5 Nov 2052) and again by online bill pay (19 Nov); a dean's remark, a resignation, a public correction (CCC 2477, rash judgment and detraction).
- *(Done: The North Slope, Podhora Svätého Jána, Orava, Slovakia, 22 to 25 Dec 2052; text only.)* Snow load is depth times density: a 55 cm core of 1.21 kg in 4.32 L is 280 kg/m3, so 154 kg/m2 at the stake against a 1.6 kN/m2 (163 kg/m2) design figure; a drone map shows the unseen lee (north) slope at 71 cm = 199 kg/m2 (209 with 10 mm rain) and the visible south slope at 38 cm = 106; the brigade cuts 35 cm off the north side only (16.5 t, about 59 m3) to balance both near 105; sexton Jozef Hudak, captain Peter Mikus, Fr Marian, assistant Vaha.
- *(Done: Three Cents a Degree, Saint Tegfan's, Llanfachreth-y-Glyn, Gwynedd, 2048; text only.)* Flue pipes follow the speed of sound, reeds do not: 36 cents flat at 8 C, 24 at 12, 18 at 14 (3.0 cents a degree); the organist's late husband's notebook rule, tune at the Mass's heat; the cellist tunes to the organ, the reeds stay off, heating on at six.
- *(Done: The Eclipse Turn, Juniper Wells, Utah, 12 August 2045; text only.)* Solar farm, import cap, five pivot pumps and a lent water turn; times from the Salt Lake City table (edge of the path, totality 1 min 26 s).
- *(Done: The Mark on the Pillar, Saltmoor St Brannock, Somerset Levels, 2051; text only.)* A flood agent, a gauge record that cannot tell one in a hundred from one in a thousand, a 1607 mark in a church pillar, and a school raised on a plinth.
- *(Done: More Roof, Santa Rosa Yucuyaa, Oaxaca, 2051; text only.)* A rainwater tank that cannot fill in a dry year: a bigger tank buys nothing, more roof buys everything; the ration is the stick reading on 1 November divided by the days left, painted on the wall.
- *(Done: The Grandmother's Sorghum, Ntambala, Tanzania, 2048; text only.)* A mission seed store's germination test (38 of 50, then 117 of 150) cannot say whether a landrace sorghum has crossed the 85%-of-initial regeneration line; the clerk stops testing, sows the regeneration plot first, lends seed at a thicker rate and records the keeper's forgotten silica gel.
- *(Done: The Coat on the Bell, Saint-Aubin-du-Bois, Mayenne, 2051; text only.)* An Angelus bell 60 m from a new estate: 95 dB at 10 m falls to 79.4 at the glass and 54.4 in the room (25 dB window, school meter good to about 2), 9.5 dB over the 45 dB night figure; a chamois pad on the clapper reads 84 at 10 m (43.4 in the room), so the six o'clock bell wears a coat and noon and evening stay bare.
- *(Done: Thirty Seconds and Thirty Minutes, Cnoc Bhríde, Donegal, 2048; text only.)* Flash-to-bang at three seconds a kilometre gives a storm's speed (14 km to 9 km in ten minutes, 30 km/h); a 50-minute median descent cannot beat an 18-minute storm for everyone, so a rule written in April sorts people to a rod-protected chapel, a barn, vehicles or the path, and the assistant says it cannot tell whether the day needed it.
- *(Done: Ten and a Half Seconds, San Pelino di Valle, Abruzzo, 2053; text only.)* Earthquake early warning: P 6 km/s, S 3.5 km/s, alert about 6 s after origin, so 10.6 s of lead at 58 km, 5.7 s at 41 km and a blind zone of about 21 km; the school's rule changes from evacuate to under the desk (slowest class 8.4 s).
- *(Done: Eight to a Well, Lupaso, Lake Nyasa, Tanzania, 2047; text only.)* 200 lab wells for 483 children: the 31 stream children (about 35 in 100 last year) are tested singly, 449 others in 55 pools of 8 and one of 9 (about 148 wells planned against 184 for uniform pools of 6; 163 used, 20 children found); pooled sensitivity 33 of 40 (82%) so a clear pool is never written as clear.
- *(Done: The Hundred and Twenty, Saint Finbarr's parish hall (invented), outside Cork, Sun 7 Feb 2049; text only.)* Survey margins and who answers: 66 of 120 replies from 400 households = 55%, margin 8.9 points (7.5 with finite-population factor 0.838), so 47.5 to 62.5; over-70 households are 160 of 400 (40%) but 28 of 120 replies (23%), 8 of 28 (28.6%) for a later Mass against 58 of 92 (63.0%), so reweighted 49.3% (margin about 9); the committee counts heads on 8 Sundays from Lent into Easter (clickers 212 at ten, 131 at 11:30) and knocks on 40 doors; assistant Fógra, Maeve Kiely, Idris Walsh, Dónal, Fr Cathal.
- *(Done: The Sun at Three Degrees, Selk Rim and Camp Nine, Titan, January 2193; text only.)* Human-powered flight needs about a fortieth of Earth's power (g 1.352, air about 5.2 to 5.4 kg/m3) but a tandem carrying 2.14 times the weight needs 3.1 times the power, so the priest cannot fly; a printed aluminium housing (about 0.4% contraction to 94 K) seizes a steel bearing (about 0.2%) by about 0.23 mm across a 120 mm bore; the Sun climbs about 0.85 degrees an hour at 7 N in northern winter, so the 2181 three-degree rule waits about 3.5 hours past sunrise; the sacristan, an extraordinary minister, brings Viaticum (canon 911 §2) and the priest arrives after the death; perfect contrition (CCC 1452) and no confession by radio; Fr Tomasz Wrona, Nkiruka Obi, Marguerite Lindqvist, Hamid Sadeghi, Henrik Aalto, Paulo Ferreira, Dr Ruth Abernethy, the system Tern.
- *(Done: The Ground Under the Drift, Saints Cyril and Methodius cemetery (invented), Spring Coulee (invented), Walsh County, North Dakota, 18 to 23 January 2049; text only.)* Winter grave in frozen clay: Stefan X = sqrt(2 k n F / L) with k 1.5 W/m/K, L about 125 MJ/m3 (375 kg ice), invented air index 620 C-days; n 0.9 on the ploughed lane gives 1.08 m (auger 108 cm), the shelterbelt drift (1.4 m) holds n near 0.22, auger 52 cm; a 4 m2, 1.6 kW blanket at 70% needs 147 h at the gate, 66 h under the drift; the assistant had given the whole cemetery the plough route's surface, and its first number made the son skip the Saturday; new rule: an auger at the plot before any family hears a date; sexton Frank Sevcik and his plot-book margins, Karen Dahl, Mike Hruby on the phone from Prudhoe Bay, Fr Thomas Okafor, the township assistant Hollis.
- *(Done: Zero Is the Sap, Pellerin Hill (invented), Caledonia County, Vermont, Tue 14 to Tue 21 Mar 2051; text only.)* Maple syrup drawn at water's boiling point + 7.5 F (Vermont minimum 66.9 Brix, from memory); water's boiling point from Clausius-Clapeyron (L 40.66 kJ/mol, about 0.5 F per 10 hPa) at 457 m: SLP 991 / 1,013 / 1,028 / 998 hPa give 208.1 / 209.2 / 209.9 / 208.4 F; 8 Brix RO concentrate adds about 0.2 F (ideal sucrose); a new probe keeps the old probe's +1.2 F controller offset, so draws are really 6.3 F above water and about 65.7 Brix at an illustrative 1.25 Brix per F; the old rule (read the first boil every morning) checks air and instrument together; 27 gal drum re-boiled, 13 of 22 stand quarts sold, 7 come back; Saint Joseph moved to Mon 20 Mar because 19 Mar 2051 is the Fifth Sunday of Lent (Easter 2 Apr).
- *(Done: The Rule for After Rain, Kestrel Bay (invented), Tillamook County, Oregon, Mon 6 to Thu 23 May 2047; text only.)* Oyster larvae need aragonite saturation (Omega) about 1.5 for the first shell; a 3.4-inch May storm makes the bay about 23% river water (S 25, TA 1,792, DIC 1,683): pH 7.98 looks normal but Omega is 1.32, while the assistant's constant TA 2,210 at S 32.5 reads the same pH as 1.89; 122 M eggs give 17% normal D-larvae against about 65%; Gran titration 1.79 mL of 0.1 N in 100 g = 1,790; soda ash 110 umol/kg (about 71 g per 6,000 L tank) lifts Omega to 2.5, Wednesday's 1.74 needs 70 (45 g); the manager declines a relief-fund claim for a loss the hatchery could have seen (about $38,000, invented) and the growers' shortfall goes to the small leases first; constants Lueker 2000, Dickson 1990, Mucci 1983; Rogation Monday 20 May, Ascension 23 May.
- *(Done: The Stones in the Nave, Hrafnvík (invented), south coast of the Reykjanes peninsula, Iceland, Sun 16 Feb to Easter Sun 5 Apr 2048; text only.)* Freezing a lava margin with seawater: lava at 2,400 kg/m3 and 1,150 C cooled to 750 C plus 400 kJ/kg latent heat needs about 2.0 GJ/m3, seawater from 6 C to steam takes 2.65 GJ/m3, so 0.76 m3 of water per m3 ideal and about 3.0 at an assumed quarter efficiency; a 60 m margin 4 m thick moving 2 m/h is 480 m3/h, needing about 406 L/s against 300 (two 50 L/s fire engines, two 100 L/s fish-farm bore pumps), so 44 m held; supply halving every 18 h makes 300 L/s enough after about 7.8 h, with about 470 m3 (155 m2 at 3 m) unheld; at a fifth efficiency 35 m, 13.6 h, 1,220 m3; the gap goes to the 1881-1958 north-west churchyard corner, not the 1909 timber church, and the 26 stones wait in the nave until Easter, then stand on the rock by survey coordinate; Rodel Bautista, Guðmundur Ólafsson, séra Hildur Magnúsdóttir, Arnar Þórsson, Kristín Hallsdóttir, the model Varða (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: Three for Every One, Bukit Kenari and Lorong Kenari (invented), Maumere, Flores, 7 Dec 2048 to Fri 19 Mar 2049; text only.)* Wolbachia replacement threshold = fitness cost / CI strength = 0.20 (assumed 20% cost, complete CI, perfect maternal transmission); lane 4 of 22 = 18% (Wilson 7 to 39%) drifts to 17.6% alone, and an edge block at 65% with 30% of adults from the lane goes 62.1, 59.7, 57.6; 31 of 43 uncovered tempayan hold larvae; male-only releases at 3:1 give q = 0.795 and the lane goes to 46.1% then 83.6% (34.9 and 62.2 if released males are half as competitive), offspring 38% of normal; about 1 female per 100 sorted, so about 3 biting females a week; readings 9/22 (41%), 19/25 (76%), 24/26 (92%, Wilson 76 to 98); card in the baby's room 9, 7, 6, 4, 3, 1; 14 dengue cases against 31 the year before, which the assistant refuses to attribute; Theresia 'Mama Tres' Da Cunha, Pak Kornelis Parera (RT, Wuring 1992), Fransiska, Mikael Wara, Dewi Hartono (Yogyakarta), Romo Boni, assistant Hitung.
- *(Done: The White Clay, Hallig Lüttmoor (invented), North Frisian Wadden Sea, 26 Nov 2057 to 12 Oct 2058; text only.)* A raised summer dike cuts Landunter days from 5 to 2 a winter but starves the marsh of flood sediment; invented rates (storm 1.2 m x 300 mg/L x 0.4 = 144 g/m2 = 0.19 mm; moderate 0.4 m x 250 x 0.6 = 60 g/m2 = 0.08 mm at 0.75 g/cm3) give 0.96 mm/yr behind the dike, 3.36 on the open fringe (30 moderate floods), 2.88 with October-to-March gates (24 of them), 0.38 raised; against an assumed 5.5 mm/yr rise, 40-year losses about 18, 9, 10 and 20 cm; Landunter days 5 to 29 with gates; feldspar marker horizons (14 months: 1.34 vs 4.46 mm); the flood assistant Pegel owns that its measure counted floods as harm; Biike 21 Feb, Chair of Peter 22 Feb 2058 (Matthew 16, 'the mud is the harvest'); Marta Ketelsen (from Opole), Knud Petersen, Tatje Hansen, teacher Jannik Paulsen, Dr. Imke Rörden, Fr Tomasz Wrona.
- *(Done: The Straw Coat, Tsangra (invented), above the Indus west of Leh, Ladakh, 1 Mar to Mon 4 Jun 2046; text only.)* Ice stupa 18 m high, r 12 m (rope 75.4 m; clinometer 21.4 deg at 42 m + 1.5 m eye): 2,714 m3 ice, 816 m2 skin (0.30 m2/m3 against 0.24 for the planned 25 m, r 14.4 m, 5,429 m3); 0.9 water yield; invented melt 3 to 10 cm w.e./day/m2 from 1 Mar to 31 May, area as volume^(2/3), south half 3/4 of melt, 20 cm dry straw passes 1/10; bare: 1,199 m3 before 10 Apr and 1,191 to 5 Jun against a need of 1,600; coated 5 Mar and uncovered over 40 days: 507 and 1,595, about 340 left on 4 Jun (bare about 50); straw 408 m2 x 0.2 m = 82 m3, about 3.3 t, 87 kg for each of 38 households; Easter 25 Mar 2046, 4 Mar Laetare (Year C, Luke 15); Sister Lissy Mathew, churpon Tsering Angchuk, Abi Yangchen, Padma Wangchuk, Stanzin Dolkar, Lobzang, the AI Tally.
- *(Done: The Last Dark Noon, Chapel of the Visitation, Stillpoint Station, the sub-Charon point of Pluto (invented), 30 Jan to Thu 9 Mar 2237; text only.)* Charon (r 606 km, a 19,591 km, Pluto r 1,188 km, from memory) stands at the zenith 18,403 km away, 3.77 deg wide (7.3 Moons, 53x area); day 6.387 d so the Sun moves 2.35 deg/h and a central noon eclipse lasts ~96 min; near the perihelion equinox (obliquity 122.5, e 0.2488) the Sun's noon declination moves ~2.1 deg/yr = 0.037 deg per Pluto day, so a season of ~1.8 yr and ~103 noon eclipses (about 5 yr at the aphelion season ~124 yr later); last totals 41, 37, 32, 26, 18 min; no. 103 is a 12.6 min partial with the Sun's centre 2.3 km inside the smooth limb against a 2.9 km solar radius (about 6% uncovered), so limb mountains make beads; limb speed 210 m/s; Sun 1.08 arcmin, 1/880 of Earth's light at 29.7 AU; full Charon ~1/6 of a full Moon at albedo 0.35; Oculus 24 places, 6-min turns, 26 turns = 624 places for 412 people, 318 ask for the last; lottery fails, the almanac declines to use the door log to find who never came, self-report finds 141; priest declines a Mass built on the Crucifixion darkness and says the Angelus at local noon; widower who never went with his wife (61 marks on a ration card) makes the 62nd; Tran Van Loc, Mai, Ama Boateng, Rosa Quispe Mamani, Delphine Aubert, Fr Ignatius Mwale, station scheduling AI Roster (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: Two Dates on the Ceiling, Saint-Arbogast's church and the Wolfsmatt Chaleur heat cooperative (both invented), northern Alsace, 30 Sep to Christmas Eve 2047; text only; twenty-year strand.)* Induced seismicity from deep geothermal stimulation: 452 events M>=0 from 3,000 m3, b = 1 (Gutenberg-Richter), events proportional to volume; 7,000 m3 more gives 1,054.7 events, P(M>=2.5) 96.4%, P(M>=3) 65.2%, P(M>=3.5) 28.4% (Poisson); soft plan 4,000 m3 at an assumed 1/3 gives 47.0%, 18.2%, 6.2%; the post-stop tail 16, 9, 6 a week extrapolated geometrically (0.55) leaves 7.3 events, ~2% of a 2.5, plus a guessed 3% for circulation; 2.6 vs 2.4 is 10^0.3 = 2x the energy, 3.5 vs 2.6 is 10^1.35 = 22x; 18 kg/s x 4.2 x 80 K = 6.0 MW vs 13.4 MW at 40; Basel 2006 (largest shock after the cut) from memory; traffic light 2.0/2.5 invented; the AI's unread log warning is the honesty turn; sacristan Odile Schaeffer, seismologist Emre Aydın, Fr Patrice Ngoy, mayor Claire Wendling, Madame Yıldız, monitoring AI Hark.
- *(Done: The Last Sun Comes in Two Pieces, chapel of Saint Simeon, Southfold Depot (invented), 85 deg S on Miranda's Uranus-facing meridian, Jan to Feb 2429; text only.)* Miranda R 235.8 km, g 0.079 m/s2 (120 kg suited weighs like 1 kg), day 33.9 h, tidally locked; Uranus 22.8 deg across, fixed due north 5 deg up; 98 deg tilt gives ~40-year polar day and night; near the end of the southern day the Sun is up only around noon, and noon is behind Uranus (~2 h eclipse), so the almanac's last sunrise (11 Feb, 2 m eye, horizon only) is invisible and the last visible sun is 4 Feb in two ~5 min pieces; horizon dip sqrt(2h/R) is 0.24 deg at 2 m, 0.41 at 6 m, 0.94 at 32 m against a Sun sinking ~0.0116 deg/day, so a 6 m berm gives pieces to 20 Feb and a 32 m mast to 7 Apr; disc 100 arcsec crosses Uranus's limb in ~9 s; Sun at 19.2 AU is 1/370 of Earth's light, ~1,100 full moons, same surface brightness; eccentricity and Miranda's 4 deg inclination ignored (dates illustrative). Nunc dimittis (Douay-Rheims and Vulgate), Candlemas, Exsultet *qui nescit occasum*. Rosa Achterberg (84), great-grandson Tomás Achterberg-Lindqvist (10), Fr Ignatius Mwale, depot head Hugo Brandt, Dr Saanvi Rao, assistant Gonzalo, skimmer *Long Reach*.
- *(Done: The Stone That Comes Back, chapel of Saint Christopher at Overhead (the sub-Mars point) beside Stickney Yard, Phobos (invented), Oct to 2 Nov 2141; text only.)* Phobos mean g 0.0058 m/s2 (a 36 kg child weighs like 21 g on Earth), escape 11.3 m/s on average but a few m/s at the sub-Mars point (Mars's tide ~0.002 m/s2, Hill radius 16.6 km); orbit 2,137 m/s, 7 h 39 min; Mars 42.4 deg wide (81 Moons), full at local midnight, eclipsing the Sun at noon for up to 54 min; a 15 m/s throw straight at Mars drops ~66 km, runs ~263 km ahead and returns after one orbit (two-body integration: ~4 km off), a throw along the orbit laps back in ~15 days (23 at 10 m/s); Phobos falls ~2 m a century and ends in 30 to 50 Myr (from memory); a dying tug pilot's wish to have his ashes thrown into orbit meets *Ad resurgendum cum Christo* (2016, paraphrased) and the yard's no-ejecta rule; the first tagged stone is lost, the second returns at 7 h 39 min 3.1 km away; ashes buried in blessed regolith facing Mars; Anton Brask, Signe and Ide Brask, Fr Paulo Tuilagi, yard master Hamid Rahimi, yard AI Sounding (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Voice That Slides Off the Dial, radio club at Saint Cuthman's, Steyning, Sussex (invented) and ring station Corona Minor, LEO 420 km, Sun 9 Oct 2101; text only.)* v = sqrt(398,600/6,791) = 7.66 km/s; line-of-sight 7.08 km/s at 10 deg elevation (v R cos e / (R+h)); Doppler 145.8 MHz x 7.08/299,792 = 3.44 kHz each way, 6.9 kHz swing over a 6.4 min pass; 12.5 kHz narrow-FM channel clips at the ends; remedy is tracking-program retuning at both ends; Earth rotation ignored, regulation and the Rosary left to regulator, priest and bishop; all figures illustrative.
- *(Done: The Tide That Is Fifty Minutes Late, Saint Ebba's chapel on Skerry Holm (invented), near Berwick-upon-Tweed, Sat 14 Jun 2053; text only.)* Lunar day 24 h 50.5 min so a tide is 50.5 min later daily, 353 min (5 h 53) a week, high waters 12.42 h apart; invented causeway rule closed 2 h before to 3 h after HW; from HW 09:30 on 15 Jun, Sundays are 15:23, 08:51, 14:44, 08:12, 14:06, so 11:00 Mass is crossable on 22 Jun and 6 Jul (14 min spare after Mass) but not 20 Jul (closes 12:06); pattern repeats every 14.77 days (not 14), drifting about 39 min per fortnight; spring-neap, wind and pressure ignored, real tables govern, Mass times left to priest and bishop (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Procession That Weighs More One Way, Saint Brendan's chapel, Kestrel Wheel (invented), low Earth orbit, June 2118; text only.)* Rotating wheel r = 200 m at 1 g: omega = sqrt(g/r) = 0.2215 rad/s, 2.11 rpm, rim 44.3 m/s; walking 1.5 m/s spinward weighs (45.8/44.3)^2 = 1.069, against 0.934 (first order 2 omega v / g = 6.8%); head 1.7 m up sees 9.73 m/s2 (0.85% less); a 1.2 m drop takes 0.495 s and drifts omega g t^3 / 3 = 8.8 cm antispinward; a 56 m wheel needs 4 rpm, 3% head-to-foot, 13% walking; fallen-Host practice left to the priest, wheel, people and altar rail invented (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: Twelve Kilos of Bell, Saint Elmo's chapel, Halcyon Reach (invented), floating station at 50 km on Venus, Sat 14 Feb 2201; text only.)* Breathable air as lifting gas: CO2 1.628 kg/m3 vs air 1.071 at 1.07 bar and 348 K (from memory), lift 0.556 kg/m3; a 60 kg bell needs 107.9 m3 of cell (5.9 m sphere) against a 52 kg margin less a 40 kg storm reserve, leaving 12 kg (21.6 m3); similar-bell scaling gives a 9.5 kg bell a pitch ratio 1.85 (10.6 semitones) above a 60 kg one; the cost is the children's recorders and nine paper books; 94 h cloud-level day from memory.
- *(Done: The Moon That Rises in the West, Saint Hildegard's chapel, Meridian Hollow on the Martian equator (invented), Sat 2 Mar 2147; text only.)* Phobos (7.654 h, 9,376 km) against a 24.623 h day: synodic period 11.11 h, 2.217 risings a sol, rises in the west, times never repeat (00:00, 11:07, 22:13, 08:42, 19:48, 06:17, 17:24, 03:53), so an Angelus bell by Phobos fails; visible arc 2 x arccos(3396/9376) = 137.5 degrees = 4.2 h, 38% of the time above the horizon; 12.8 arcmin wide (Moon 31); orbital data from memory, chapel and people invented (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Lamp That Is Dark Before Dawn, Chapel of Saint Lucy, Sabine Base, Mare Tranquillitatis (invented base), Sat 15 Sep 2096; text only.)* Lunar night = half of 29.53 d = 14.77 d = 354.4 h; 4.0 W (3.8 lamp + 0.2 controller) needs 1,418 Wh against an invented June test of 1,360 Wh, so the lamp dies at 340 h, 14.4 h before sunrise (30 Sep 10:10 vs 1 Oct 00:34); a late cut would need most of the last week off, an early one 0.16 W; planning 12 h in hand (366.4 h) gives 3.71 W in all, 3.51 W lamp (7.6% down, about 3% seen), 45 Wh left at dawn; electric lamp left to the bishop, battery, floor and eye response from memory or invented (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Charter That Is Seventy Years Late, Saint Oswin's church (invented), near Hexham, Northumberland, Sat 9 Jan 2055; text only.)* Radiocarbon on a parchment charter dated 1189: fraction 0.9118 +/- 0.0034 gives -8033 ln F = 742 +/- 30 BP (second sample 0.9115 = 744), so mid-thirteenth century, about 70 years after the claimed date; the test dates the animal, not the writing, so honest copy, forgery or wrong date stay open; 5% modern glue would read about 45 years young (true about 787 BP, around 1163) and is why a second, untouched sample matters; calibrated years deliberately not quoted; all figures illustrative.
- *(Done: The Pendulum That Is Short of a Turn, Saint Albert's chapel, Cobalt Terrace Settlement, Arcadia Planitia, Mars (invented settlement), Sat 23 Nov 2143; text only.)* Foucault pendulum at 47 N on Mars: plane turns 360 x sin(lat) per sidereal day (24.6229 h), 10.69 deg/h, full turn 33.67 h = 1.365 sols, 263.7 deg (96.3 short) per sol; pegs every 15 deg fall every 84 min; same line every 16.83 h; 6 m wire at 3.721 m/s2 swings in 7.98 s (4.91 s on Earth); Mars day lengths and Paris comparison from memory, settlement, people and times invented (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Hill That Adds Forty-Five Minutes, Saint Aldhelm's village and Saint Cuthman's chapel (invented), near Cerne Abbas, Dorset, Sat 15 Aug 2054; text only.)* Naismith's rule from memory: 9 km at 5 km/h = 108 min, +45 min for 450 m of climb = 153 min; a party of 14 at 3.5 km/h = 154 + 45 + 20 stops = 219 min (3 h 39); latest start 07:21 for Mass at 11:00, so leave at 07:00 (21 min spare); a 08:30 start arrives 12:09; all figures illustrative.
- *(Done: The Bell That Is Five Seconds Late, Saint Wystan's church and Saint Michael's hill chapel (invented), near Church Stretton, Shropshire, Sat 21 Mar 2054; text only.)* Speed of sound 331.3 x sqrt(1+T/273.15): 337.3 m/s at 10 C, so 1,800 m takes 5.34 s; both bells at 4.00 s lag 1.34 s (a limp); shifting one start only moves the lag to the other end (6.68 s); each end hears the other midway when delay is an odd number of half beats, beat 2 x 5.336 / 3 = 3.56 s; -5 C is 0.15 s off, 20 C 0.09 s, 5 m/s wind 5.26 / 5.42 s; formula from memory, bells, distance, forecast and people invented (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Cabinet That Is Ten Degrees Colder, Saint Fillan's church (invented), near Crieff, Perthshire, Sat 20 Dec 2053; text only.)* AED porch cabinet: 0.7 W/K loss; 10 W heater holds 10 C at -4 C but only 2.3 C at -12 C (15.4 W needed); 20 W thermostat 77% duty, 370 Wh/day, 33 kWh/90 d (~10 pounds); power cut 10 to 5 C in 18 min bare, 2.0 h with 4 L water (to 0 C: 43 min vs 4.7 h); thermostat reads the air, logger goes on the back wall; all figures illustrative.
- *(Done: The Rainbow That Is Below the Horizon, Saint Swithun's fête field (invented), near Alresford, Hampshire, Sat 15 Jul 2056; text only.)* A bow's top stands 42 degrees minus sun altitude: at 3 pm sun 53 degrees so the top is 11 degrees below the horizon (about 93 m away seen from an 18 m tower); noon sun about 59.5 on 15 Jul, 61 at midsummer, 14.5 at midwinter (winter noon bow top 27 degrees); sun 30 degrees about 17:45 BST gives top 12, sun 20 about 18:50 gives 22; secondary about 51 degrees, Alexander's band; Swithun's forty days a legend; angles and times from memory, place and people invented (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Comet That Comes Twice, Saint Aidan's hall (invented), near Bamburgh, Northumberland, Sat 13 Mar 2060; text only.)* Kepler for Halley: 0.586 and 35.1 AU give a = 17.84 AU, T = 17.84^1.5 = 75.4 y (real 74 to 79, planets); 9 Feb 1986 + 76 y = 9 Feb 2062, 196 days after the predicted 28 Jul 2061 perihelion, which is not the best viewing date; speeds 54.6 km/s at perihelion, 0.9 at aphelion (2023); Alan 11 in 1986, 86 in 2061, Nuala 83 in 2134; Giotto's comet in Padua only supposed to be Halley's; dates and orbit from memory, club and people invented (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Light the Sea Takes, Saint Maelrubha's chapel (invented), near Applecross, Wester Ross, Sat 4 Oct 2053; text only.)* Tower lamp at 38 m: horizon sqrt(2Rh) 22.0 km, plus 5.05 km (2 m eye) = 27.0 km (14.6 nmi) or 8.74 km (6 m) = 30.7 km (16.6 nmi), so the label's '20 miles' is past the curve; Allard's law at 2e-7 lux, T 0.74 clear / 0.5 haze: 500 cd 8.0 nmi (4.9 haze), 5,000 cd 12.7 (7.2), 50,000 cd 18.0 (9.6); a private light needs the lighthouse authority's consent (from memory); thresholds and T assumed, chapel, lamp and people invented (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: Sixteen Sunrises and One Bell, cupola of the ring station Corona Minor (invented), low Earth orbit at 420 km, Fri 13 Mar 2099; text only.)* Period 2 pi sqrt(6791^3/398600) = 5,569 s = 92.8 min, 15.5 sunrises a day; shadow half-angle arcsin(6371/6791) = 69.7 deg, night 38.7% = 36.0 min, light 56.9 (zero-beta case, no night at high beta); sunrise pattern shifts 47.6 min a day, 31 orbits = 2,877.5 min (2.5 short of 2 days); a sunrise inside a 3-minute Benedictus is 3.2% (1 in 31); Hours kept by the ship's clock with the sunrise after the bell as a sign, liturgical-day rulings left to a bishop; station and people invented (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Water That Does Not Stay Water, Chapel of Saint Frances of Rome, Dune Gate Station, Arabia Terra, Mars (invented), Tue 9 Mar 2162; text only.)* Triple point 611.657 Pa vs surface 610 Pa, saturation pressure 2.3 kPa at 20 C (memory): a 20 C sprinkle boils; cooling 1 kg to 0 C is 83.6 kJ plus 334 freezing = 417.6 kJ, 2,500 x = 417.6 (1-x) gives x = 0.143, so 14% vapour and 86% ice (single energy balance, sublimation and droplet kinetics ignored); blessing moves to the 1 atm garage bay, sealed vials in cabs, ice as matter left to a bishop; station and people invented (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Window That the Sky Does Not Need, Chapel of Saint Odilia, Hollow Ridge Settlement, Terra Sirenum, Mars (invented), Thu 8 Dec 2157; text only.)* Column mass p/g: 610/3.721 = 16.4 g/cm2 on Mars against 1,033 on Earth (63x); 2.5 m regolith at an assumed 1.5 g/cm3 = 375 g/cm2 = 22.9 atmospheres of Mars; surface dose about 0.67 mSv/day = 245 mSv/yr (cruise about 2.7x), 2 h/day outside = 20.4 mSv/yr, all from memory; regolith attenuation deliberately not computed (secondaries, not exponential); a dog-leg light pipe with a dosimeter, scatter left to the radiation office; settlement and people invented (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Water That Boils at Nought, Chapel of the Epiphany, Low Mercy Settlement, Hellas Planitia, Mars (invented), Tue 6 Jan 2161; text only.)* Antoine boiling point: 0.1 C at 610 Pa (triple point 611.657 Pa), 9.1 C at an assumed 1,150 Pa; 200 g at 16 C boils off 2.3 g (1.15%) at Hellas pressure and stops at 9 C, but at 610 Pa 13.8% (27.6 g, about 5.7 m3 of vapour) must leave to freeze the rest; water to ice expands 9.05%, so an 85% filled 250 mL sealed brass phial (212 mL becomes 231) survives a -60 C night where an open clay stoup cracks; stoup moves inside, phial on the porch rail, use left to a priest; Hellas pressure from memory, place and people invented (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Bell That the Air Forgets, Chapel of the Annunciation, Ember Flats Settlement, Isidis Planitia, Mars (invented), Thu 25 Mar 2151; text only.)* Speed of sound sqrt(gamma R T) with 1.29, 188.9 and an assumed 210 K is 226 m/s (340 on Earth), 100 m in 0.44 s against 0.29; density 610/(188.9 x 210) = 0.0154 kg/m3 (about 80 times thinner), impedance rho c 3.48 against 417, so the same swing of bronze is about 21 dB quieter, 50 dB at 30 m against 70, before CO2 absorption (not computed) and a helmet; the bell moves inside and a contact microphone feeds the field crews' radio, or a helmet rests on the airlock door; the Angelus hours and doctrine left to a bishop; settlement and people invented (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Earth That Is Not Lit, Chapel of the Holy Cross, Vesper Cut Base, 30 E 20 N on the Moon (invented), Holy Week 2124; text only.)* Earth 1.90 degrees wide (3.67 Moon diameters, 13.5x area); albedo ratio 0.37/0.12 gives a full Earth 41x a full Moon, about 10 lux on a 0.25 lux Moon, 8 lux on a table at altitude 54.5 degrees; full Moon on Good Friday (invented) puts Earth at phase angle 155.6 degrees two days before, 4.4% lit, Lambert brightness 0.0080 of full, about 0.08 lux, with the Sun near noon at the base, so Tenebrae's dark is made with shutters; 15 days later full Earth in lunar night; albedos, 0.25 lux and the daily brightness swing from memory, base and people invented (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Candles That Are Not Lit, Chapel of the Presentation, Lantern Ring (invented), low Earth orbit, Mon 2 Feb 2099; text only.)* Paraffin C25H52 at an assumed 6 g/h: 3.45 g O2 and 3.12 g CO2 per g, so 20.7 g O2 and 18.7 g CO2 an hour and 77 W a candle (0.59 and 0.45 of a person at rest); 40 candles for 20 min = 80 g wax, 276 g O2 (2.7% of a 12-person day), 250 g CO2 = 0.139 m3 = about 2,300 ppm in 60 m3 sealed, 3.1 kW; people alone add about 1,560 ppm; microgravity flame spherical and self-extinguishing without airflow (from memory); blessing of unlit candles left to a bishop; station and people invented (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Shadow That Moves a Degree in Two Hours, Chapel of Saint Bede, Equator Cross Station, lunar equator (invented), Sat 1 Aug 2111; text only.)* Lunar solar day 29.53 d = 708.7 h, so the Sun moves 0.508 degrees an hour, 12.19 a day; 2 m upright rod at the equator (tilt 1.54 degrees ignored, 5 cm at noon) throws a tip at h x tan H: 0.43, 0.91, 1.48, 2.28, 3.60, 6.60, 24.5 m after 1 to 7 days, sunset at 7.38 d (14.77 d light, 354 h dark); 1.8 cm of shadow movement an hour; an equatorial dial (rod to the pole) gives even 12.19 degree marks, a calendar not a clock; Moon values from memory, station and people invented (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Star That Is Not Quite North, Saint Finnian's chapel (invented), near Strangford, Co. Down, Sat 14 Oct 2102; text only.)* Polaris circles the pole at about 0.45 degrees (2102, from memory): at latitude 54.4 the greatest azimuth is asin(sin 0.45 / cos 54.4) = 0.77 degrees, 24 cm over an 18 m chapel (50 cm for an invented 1.6 degree compass error); sighting at the two greatest elongations 11 h 58 min apart (dawn and the next dusk) and taking the midpoint cancels it (extremes 1.51 m apart at 56 m); pole drift 50.3 arcsec/yr x sin 23.44 = 0.00556 deg/yr gives 0.72 degrees in 2000 and 0.71 in 2200; elongation times not computed, site and people invented (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Transfer That Takes Two Hundred and Fifty-Nine Days, Saint Raphael's corner, transit ship Wayfarer's Lamp (invented), Fri 17 Mar 2147; text only.)* Hohmann 1 to 1.524 AU: a = 1.262 AU, T = 1.418 y, half = 258.9 d; Mars moves 135.7 deg meanwhile so must lead Earth 44.3 deg at departure; Earth gains 0.4616 deg/d, so a 10-day slip is 4.6 deg off and 30 days 13.8 deg (delta-v cost not computed); 1 Jul 2146 departure, Ash Wednesday 1 Mar 2147 (day 243), landing 17 Mar (day 259), Easter 16 Apr 2147; the Mass does not need the arrival to match the feast; circular orbits, ship, people and schedule invented (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Christmas the Sun Sits On, Saint Stephen's chapel, Ferrous Gate Settlement, Arabia Terra, Mars (invented), Fri 25 Dec 2144; text only.)* Solar conjunction: light time 499 s/AU, 2.524 AU = 21.0 min one way at conjunction vs 0.524 AU = 4.4 min at opposition; relative rate 0.4616 deg/d gives Sun-Earth-Mars angle 2 deg at 7.2 d, 3 deg at 10.8 d, 5 deg at 17.9 d either side (blackout 14 to 36 days); synodic period 779.93 d; relay Mass fails, Mass itself does not need a link, so send the carol and receive the blessing early as recordings; thresholds from memory, circular orbits, chapel, dates and people invented (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Names in the Hat, Saint Nicholas's hall (invented), Clonbrack, Co. Roscommon, Sun 11 Dec 2050; text only.)* Gift-exchange draw: nobody-holds-self 176,214,841/479,001,600 = 36.79% (-> 1/e), so 63.2% of simultaneous draws have a match; redraw-own-slip hat strands the last person 6.51% (1 in 15); 3 couples excluded: 100,511,168 = 20.98% (1 in 4.8); a given swap 0.76%, about 39% some swap; ring method 11! = 39,916,800; hall, people and customs invented (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Air the Congregation Breathes, Chapel of Saint Joseph of Cupertino, Tessera Station, low Earth orbit (invented), Sun 3 Oct 2088; text only.)* CO2 in a shut chapel: 80 people x 15 L/h x 70 min = 1.4 m3 into 150 m3 (9,300 ppm if sealed); loop 200 m3/h gives 3,000 to 7,730 ppm at dismissal, past 5,000 at 19 min; 600 m3/h holds 4,980; in microgravity breath hangs round the face so the room average understates a still pew (invented 650 ppm gap); spare fan 38 W; breath rates and limits from memory, station and people invented (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Lamp That Outlasts Its Light, Saint Hubert's chapel, Hubert Station, Ganymede (invented), Sat 19 Mar 2112; text only.)* Pu-238 half-life 87.7 y: 0.674 of the heat left after 50 y (2,000 W thermal becomes 1,347), but 110 W electric reads 34 W: 2.35%/yr fall = 0.79 decay + 1.56 inferred converter ageing; 20 W needed (15 W lamp + 5 W electronics) at 22.6 y (about 2134), bare lamp 34.8 y (2147); waste heat 1,313 W is the lasting asset (1,063 W in 30 y, 907 in 50, 611 in 100); half-life is the fuel's clock, not the lamp's; heat-loop reuse left to engineer and regulator; unit, ageing curve and people invented, values from memory (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Rest That Is One Point Three Seconds, Malapert Station chapel, Mons Malapert near the lunar south pole (invented station), Sat 14 Nov 2093; text only.)* Earth-Moon light time 384,400 km / 299,792 km/s = 1.282 s one way, 2.564 s round trip (1.21 s at perigee, 1.35 at apogee, from memory); waiting to hear the other choir stop leaves the finisher 2.6 s of silence per handover (9 handovers = 23.1 s); a shared clock only moves the delay: silence heard minus overlap heard is always 2.564 s, so the even split is a 1.282 s rest and a 1.282 s overlap (muted speaker); radio slack, verse length, station and people invented, liturgical status of a linked prayer left to a bishop (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Butt That Held Two Hundred, Saint Ninian's hall (invented), near Peebles, Scottish Borders, Sat 22 July 2051; text only.)* Rainwater: 1 mm on 1 m2 = 1 L; 45 m2 plan area x 0.8 runoff = 36 L/mm, so a 200 L butt fills at 5.6 mm and a 20 mm day overflows 520 L; 40 m2 of beds at 10 L/m2/week = 400 L/week, so the butt lasts 3.5 days and a 1,000 L tank 17.5; storage, not catchment, is the limit; 1.06 t on 1.2 m2 = 8.7 kPa, ask a surveyor; runoff coefficient, rainfall and crop need assumed (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Sack by the Lychgate, Saint Bega's churchyard (invented), near Keswick, Cumbria, Sat 17 Jan 2054; text only.)* De-icing salt: freezing-point drop = 2 x 1.86 x molality, 31.4 g salt per kg water per degree (94 g/kg at -6, 8.6% brine); ice 0.917 kg/m2 per mm, so 0.5 mm needs 43 g/m2 at -6 and 3 mm 259 g; 60 m2 path needs 2.6 kg for a thin film, the 25 kg sack is 417 g/m2 (9.6x); weigh a 0.1 m2 scrape to find thickness (38 g = 0.41 mm, 210 g = 2.3 mm, so chip it); salt slow below about -9 and stone damage quoted from memory, readings and people invented (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Wave That Takes Fifteen Hours, Saint Marianne's (invented), Hāmākua coast, Hawai'i, Sat 6 to Sun 7 Jun 2054; text only.)* Tsunami speed sqrt(g d): 198.1 m/s = 713 km/h at 4,000 m, so 10,500 km takes 14.7 h (19:40 quake, 10:23 arrival); 113 km/h at 100 m, 36 km/h at 10 m; Green's law 0.5 m offshore becomes 2.2 m at 10 m depth; 30 min period = 357 km wavelength, the third wave is the largest; 1,200 m uphill walk 40 min at 0.5 m/s, so move at 07:00; Mass moved to the hall at 08:00; heights, gauge readings, people and place invented (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Wine That Leaves in October, Arcadia Station, Mars, Wed 26 Sep 2142; text only.)* Hohmann transfer 1 to 1.524 AU: a = 1.262 AU, 259 days, Mars must lead Earth by 44.3 degrees, windows 779.9 days apart; departure Wed 3 Oct 2142 arrives 19 Jun 2143, the next leaves 21 Nov 2144 and arrives 7 Aug 2145; 4 bottles (3,000 mL) at 15 mL a Mass last 28.6 weeks at 7 Masses a week, 40.0 at 5 against 37 weeks of transit; Fr Anselm drops Tuesday and Thursday Masses; orbits idealised, Mass-rule points from memory, people and cargo invented (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Sun That Rises Three Times, Saint Giuseppe Piazzi's chapel, Piazzi Dome on Ceres (invented), Third Sunday of Advent 2164; text only.)* Ceres solar day 9.076 h (rotation 9.074 h, 4.6 yr orbit): 2.644 sunrises per 24 h, first sunrise slips 3.2 h a day, 37 sunrises = 14 clock days within 11 min; 10:00 Mass in daylight 7 days of 14 (sun up 4.54 h of each day near the equator, tilt about 4 degrees, from memory); 168 h week = 18.51 Ceres days; sunlight 177.7 W/m2 (153 to 209 over the orbit), 13% of Earth's; 24.2 h body clock from memory; dome, people and farm lamp schedule invented (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Hole That Was Wider, Saint Dwynwen's churchyard (invented), near Newborough, Anglesey, Sat 4 Oct 2053; text only.)* Post-hole concrete: 25 cm x 60 cm hole = 29.45 L, minus a 10 cm post's 6 L = 23.45 L, x10 = 234.5 L = 23.45 bags at an invented 10 L a bag, +10% = 25.8, buy 26; a 30 cm hole = 42.4 L, 36.4 L of concrete, 364 L, 41 bags with 10% (15 more, 300 kg, about 90 pounds at an invented 6 pounds); concrete ratio 1.55 against area ratio 1.44; bag yield, price and one-third-burial rule quoted from memory or invented (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Ladder That Reaches the Gutter, Saint Cuthman's hall (invented), near Steyning, West Sussex, Sat 25 Oct 2053; text only.)* Ladder angle: 4-to-1 is atan 4 = 75.96 degrees; 4.6 m ladder reaches 4.46 m with foot 1.12 m out, 34 cm short of a 4.8 m gutter; foot 0.6 m gives 4.56 m at 82.5 degrees, 1.2 m 4.44 m at 74.9, 2.0 m 4.14 m at 64.2; no angle reaches 4.8 m; minimum length sqrt(4.8^2+1.2^2) = 4.95 m, hire about 6 m; 4-to-1 rule and rung advice quoted from memory, ladder and people invented (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Jar in the Vestry Cupboard, Saint Milburga's vestry (invented), near Much Wenlock, Shropshire, Sat 13 Dec 2053; text only.)* Weighing coins: 6,400 g could be £17.98 (all 1p) to over £1,000, so sort first; 1p/2p worth 0.281 p/g and 5p/10p 1.538 p/g whatever the mix; piles 2,600 g copper £7.30, 1,950 g small silver £30.00, 850 g 20p (170 coins) £34.00, 1,000 g 50p (125 coins) £62.50, total £133.80; scale checked on 20 twenties (100 g); coin masses from memory, piles invented (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Pipe That Goes Flat in December, Saint Dunstan's church (invented), near Glastonbury, Somerset, Sat 12 Dec 2054; text only.)* Flue pitch follows sqrt(T): 16 C to 6 C is 0.9826 = -30.5 cents (about 3 per kelvin), A 440 becomes 432.3 against an unmoved reed (7.7 Hz beat); reeds retuned at 6 C sit 4.6 Hz flat once the church reaches 12 C (flues 436.9); tune reeds at the service temperature; reed behaviour and tuner practice from memory, temperatures, church and people invented (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Candle That Says Four Hours, Saint Senan's church (invented), near Kilrush, Co. Clare, Sat 14 Mar 2054; text only.)* Vigil tealights, 21:00 to 07:00 (10 h), 48 cups, label 'up to 4 h': round up per cup, not on the total (3 per cup = 144, not 120); test burns 3 h 55 (aisle) and 3 h 10 (door, draught), changed at about 90%: aisle 3 lights (00:30, 04:00), door 4 (00:00, 03:00, 06:00); 40x3 + 8x4 = 152, +5% = 160, 7 boxes of 25 = 175 (23 spare); one test candle per place, times and price invented, label from memory (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Thermometer That Read Low, Saint Kea's hall (invented), near Truro, Cornwall, Sat 21 Jun 2053; text only.)* Jam setting point: boiling-water check reads 98.5 (1.5 low), so true 105 shows as 103.5; 3 kg strawberries (90% water, from memory) + 3 kg sugar = 6 kg, 3.3 kg solids (55%); at 65% finished 5.08 kg (0.92 kg boiled off, 14 jars of 340 g), at 70% 4.71 kg (0.37 kg, one jar, less); heating 1.44 MJ + evaporation 2.09 MJ at a guessed 1.5 kW is 16 + 23 min; cold-saucer wrinkle test agrees at 103.5; setting point, solids and boiling points from memory, probe and pan invented (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Forty Tickets, Saint Dympna's hall (invented), near Dundalk, Co. Louth, Sat 8 Nov 2053; text only.)* Raffle odds: 40 of 400 tickets, 6 prizes: 40x6/400 = 0.6 prizes on average, but P(at least one) = 1 - C(360,6)/C(400,6) = 47.1% (with replacement 46.9%); 100 tickets 82.4%, 200 98.5%, 157 tickets (EUR 235.50) 95.1%; 10 tickets 14.2%; EUR 600 of tickets against about EUR 65 of prizes; ticket price, prizes and draw invented, local raffle law not covered (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Sack That Makes Less, Saint Ernan's hall yard (invented), near Strabane, Co. Tyrone, Sat 14 Jun 2053; text only.)* Ready-mix yield: 2.4 x 1.2 x 0.1 = 288 L; label 12 L a 25 kg bag gives 24; test box 60x30x6 cm = 10.8 L + 0.4 L spare = 11.2 L (7% under), 25.7 bags; 10 mm over-dig on 2.88 m2 = 28.8 L = 2.6 bags, 28.3, order 30 (750 kg); 2.5 L water a bag = 65 L; water ratio and caustic burns from memory, box, readings and pour invented (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Rate That Was Flat, Saint Cadoc's hall (invented), near Usk, Monmouthshire, Sat 15 Feb 2053; text only.)* Flat rate vs APR: £12,000 over 5 years at 4.5% flat = £2,700 interest, £14,700, £245 a month, which is 0.691% a month = 8.29% nominal / 8.62% compounded; credit union 7.9% APR gives £241.18 a month, £14,471, £229 cheaper; 4.5% on the reducing balance would be £223.72, £13,423; balance after 24 payments £7,785 (flat deal) vs £7,739, 36 x 245 = £8,820 incl. £1,035 interest; early-settlement rebate method and no-charge credit union assumed (all figures illustrative, not advice).
- *(Done: The Road That Shuts at Twenty to Six, Saint Wilfrid's chapel on an invented tidal island (invented), off Northumberland, Sat 9 Aug 2053; text only.)* Causeway window by the rule of twelfths: range 4.8 m (0.8 to 5.6), twelfth 0.4 m, heights 1.2/2.0/3.2 m after each hour (62 min), road at 2.4 m floods 145 min after low water (cosine curve 146), 15:10 + 2:25 = 17:35, opens 12:45, window 4 h 50 not the board's 6 h (18:10); 1.2 m in 62 min is 1.9 cm/min; 1.8 km at 2.5 km/h = 43 min, hour margin gives leave by 15:52, Mass at 14:30; rule of twelfths, surge effect and pace quoted from memory, heights and road level invented (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Soup That Did Not Take Twelve Hours, Saint Mochta's hall (invented), near Louth, Co. Louth, Sat 20 Jan 2052; text only.)* Scaling a 4-bowl soup to 60: 60 x 0.3 L +10% = 20 L, factor 16.7 not 15 (8.3 kg veg); simmer stays 45 min (not 11 h 15); heating 20 kg x 4.18 x 80 K = 1.86 kWh, at 2.8 kW x 75% = 53 min; pot 30 L is two thirds full; salt 5 g/L = 100 g, start at 70 and taste; specific heat, efficiency, simmer time and fill limit from memory, hob ratings invented (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Seed That Was Two Years Old, Saint Tudwal's churchyard (invented), near Abersoch, Gwynedd, Sat 23 Sep 2051; text only.)* Reseeding 26 x 4.5 = 117 m2: pure live seed = purity x germination, 0.98 x 0.85 = 0.833, so a 35 g/m2 label rate is 29.15 g live, 3,411 g over the strip; a 2 kg half bag (97%, tin 85%, packed 2049) tested on plates at 31/50 + 28/50 = 59% (margin about +/-9.6) holds 1,145 g live, so 2,721 g of new seed, blend 4,721 g = 40.3 g/m2, 363 g per 9 m2 section; at 49% the shortfall is 233 g, covered by a 5 kg tin's 2,279 g spare; co-op sack (92%, 70%) is 4.97 per kg live vs 5.16; germination loss with age, rates, prices and test length quoted from memory or invented (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Cut Tiles at the Wall, Saint Colman's vestry (invented), near Gort, Co. Galway, Sat 9 Sep 2051; text only.)* Tiling 3.45 x 2.62 m with 300 mm tiles: area says 101, but 11.5 x 8.73 tiles means centring gives 225 and 260 mm cuts (corner start gives 150 and 220), 12 x 9 = 108 positions, 38 cuts, offcuts unusable; 10 boxes of 11 leaves 2 spare against about 3 expected breaks (1 in 12, invented), so 11 boxes (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Posts and the Gaps, Saint Ninian's glebe (invented), near Whithorn, Wigtownshire, Sat 16 Apr 2050; text only.)* Fence posts: 27 m at 1.8 m is 15 gaps = 16 posts; 15 m is 8.33, so 9 gaps of 1.667 m = 10 posts; shared corner makes 25, not the shortcut's 24 (42/1.8 = 23.3); 3.6 m rails: 8 + 5 = 13 a line, 26 for two, plus one for staggering; span, rail length and burial depth quoted from memory (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Two on the Eighth, Saint Cronan's hall (invented), near Roscrea, Co. Tipperary, Sun 14 Mar 2049; text only.)* Birthday problem: 30 people, P(all different) = 0.294, so 70.6% that two share (23 gives 50.7%); 435 pairs at 1/365 is 1.19 expected; a match with one named person is 7.6% (29 others); birth bunching quoted from memory, no size (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Slowest Walker Sets the Hour, Saint Teilo's chapel (invented), Black Mountains near Crickhowell, Powys, Sat 11 Oct 2053; text only.)* Naismith (5 km/h + 1 h per 600 m, from memory): 16 km and 750 m is 267 min, 4 h 27; median walker (invented 4.4 km/h, 520 m/h) 5 h 04, +45 min stops = 5 h 49; slowest (3.5, 400) 387 min + 45 = 7 h 12, so an 11:00 start reaches the chapel at 18:12, after the 17:00 Mass and about sunset (18:10, from memory); 08:30 start arrives 15:42, 78 min margin; rain cuts the climb to 360 m/h (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Censer That Swings Slow, Saint Hedwig's chapel, Calder Habitat (invented), L5, Holy Saturday 9 Apr 2191; text only.)* Spin gravity: 0.38 g at 200 m is 1.30 rpm; thurible 0.6 m swings in 2.52 s (1.55 on Earth), 23 cm chain matches Earth; dropped snuffer from 1.8 m takes 0.99 s and lands 16 cm anti-spinward; aisle along the axis avoids Coriolis; detectors stay on, half a tablet, fire officer's hand on the panel; no CO figure invented (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Wood That Reads Twenty-Eight, Saint Aidan's hall (invented), near Alston, Cumbria, Sat 2 Nov 2052; text only.)* Firewood moisture: a pin meter reads 28 on the oven-dry basis = 21.9% of the wet log, not the trade's 'under 20%'; kitchen-oven slice 152 g to 118 g = 22.4% wet / 28.8% dry basis; 600 kg is 134 kg water, 3.88 kWh/kg net (5.19 dry, 0.68 per kg water, both from memory), 1,630 kWh into the room at an assumed 70% against 1,690 at 20%; the tarpaulined pile reads 52 = 34.2% and gives 3.19 kWh/kg, 18% less. Invented figures: the meter, the slice, the efficiency.
- *(Done: The Bag That Covers Thirteen, Saint Piran's churchyard path (invented), near Perranporth, Cornwall, Sat 13 Sep 2053; text only.)* Gravel by the label: a 850 kg bag "covers 13 m2" at 40 mm; a 10 L bucket weighs 15.5 kg so 1.55 t/m3, and 850/1.55 = 0.548 m3 is 13.7 m2 at 40 mm but 10.97 at 50 mm; path 10.5 x 1.2 = 12.6 m2 x 0.05 = 0.63 m3 = 0.977 t, +10% settling (guess) = 1.074 t, so one bulk bag plus ten 25 kg bags (1,100 kg); barrow 80 kg, 14 loads predicted, 15 actual; 1.5 to 1.75 t/m3 and the label depth quoted from memory (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: Seven Rolls for a Vestry, Saint Edburga's vestry (invented), near Bicester, Oxfordshire, Sat 18 Oct 2053; text only.)* Wallpaper: 15.6 m x 2.4 / (10.05 x 0.53) = 7.03 rolls by area; by drops, walls 4.2 and 3.6 m give 8 + 7 each twice = 30 drops; plain 2.5 m drops (4.02 per roll) need 8 rolls; a 64 cm straight-match repeat makes each drop 2.56 m, 3 per roll (2.37 m left, 19 cm short of a fourth), so 10 rolls, +1 spare from one batch = 11; 21 drops would leave 4.5 m bare; roll size and batch-number advice quoted from memory (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Tin That Said Twenty-Five, Saint Fillan's churchyard (invented), near Killin, Perthshire, Sat 7 Jun 2053; text only.)* Two-stroke mix: 100 ml oil in 2.5 L petrol is 25:1 (4% of petrol; 40 ml/L) against the manual's 40:1 (2.5%, 25 ml/L) and 50:1 (2%, 20 ml/L); 100 ml x 40 = 4 L, so add 1.5 L petrol; oil adds about 2.5% to the volume; burn about 0.5 L/h guessed (4 to 5 h = 2 to 2.5 L; first tank 51 min); rich-running effects and the one-month mix limit quoted from memory (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Bunting That Would Not Go Straight, Saint Kentigern's Green (invented), near Penrith, Cumbria, Sat 5 Jun 2049; text only.)* Bunting sag: length = S + 8h^2/(3S), 14 m at 0.6 m sag is 14.069, 9 m at 0.4 is 9.047, 11 m at 0.5 is 11.061, total 34.18 of a 35 m roll; end pull = wS/(8h) at an assumed 30 g/m: 12 N at 0.6 m, 24 at 0.3, 48 at 0.15, 144 (14.7 kgf) at 0.05; cord rating and knot loss quoted from memory, ask the churchwarden about the lych-gate (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Label Says One to Five, Saint Nectan's field (invented), near Hartland, Devon, Sat 29 Jul 2051; text only.)* Cordial dilution: 150 x 1.5 x 0.2 L = 45 L; 1+5 is 1/6 = 7.5 L, 1+4 is 1/5 = 9 L (20% vs 16.7%); 3 kg of ice melting into 15 L makes 18 L, so mix at 1/5 to finish at 1/6 (1/6 mixed ends 1/7.2); the label reading and food-grade ice quoted from memory, melt guessed (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: Fourteen Watt-Hours Short, Saint Aidan's Way pilgrim ride (invented), near Alston, Cumbria, Sat 10 Aug 2052; text only.)* E-bike energy for 64 km and 1,100 m with 104 kg all in: climb 104 x 9.81 x 1100 = 312 Wh, rolling (0.8%, from memory) 145 Wh, drag (CdA 0.6, 5 m/s, guessed) 160 Wh, total 617 Wh at the wheel; rider 80 W for 4.5 h = 350 Wh, motor 267 Wh at 80% = 334 Wh from a battery of 400 x 80% = 320, so 14 Wh (4%) short, inside the error; fix: 90 min charge at a 72 W charger = about 92 Wh (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Cake That Serves Twenty-Eight, Saint Alban's hall (invented), near Harpenden, Hertfordshire, Sat 22 Jun 2052; text only.)* Cake portions: 30 cm round = 707 cm2, label 5x5 = 28.3; 60 guests = 11.8 cm2 each; ring cut (5 cm rings, 2.5 cm slices at the mid-circumference) gives 31 + 18 + 6 = 55 at 12.5 cm2; spare 20 cm tin (314 cm2) gives 18 + 6 = 24, total 79 for 63; a 35 cm tin is 962 cm2 (+36% for +17% width); sponge 0.5 g/cm3 from memory, 47 g guessed, 52 g weighed (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Verge That Looked Thin, Saint Cedd's churchyard (invented), near Bradwell-on-Sea, Essex, Sat 4 Nov 2051; text only.)* Daffodil bulbs by the label: 36 m2 verge at 2 m2 a bag is 18 bags, 900 bulbs, 25 a m2, 20 cm apart (a naturalised drift); bedding density about 12.25 cm (from memory) is 2,400 bulbs, 48 bags; at an invented 4 pounds a bag that is 72 vs 192; compromise 30 bags, 1,500 bulbs, 15.5 cm; depth 3x bulb height = 15 cm (from memory); 7 cm planter holes 577 cm3 each, 0.87 m3 of soil; 25 s a hole guessed, corrected to 30 by the clay, 12.5 h, 1.25 h each for ten; 1,497 planted (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Bird That Would Not Be Hurried, Saint Wite's hall (invented), near Charmouth, Dorset, Wed 17 to Sat 20 Dec 2053; text only.)* Thawing a 10 kg turkey: time goes with width squared, width with the cube root of weight, so 2x the weight is 1.26^2 = 1.59x the time; 48 h for a 5 kg bird gives 76 h for 10 kg (not the label's 100 h); hall fridge glass-of-water reading 7.5 C (not the dial's 3) gives 41 h but a warm surface, 3.9 C after a notch gives 78 h; 66 h available, so probe at 54 h (-2.5 C), finish in cold water (about 1 h/kg from memory, 3 to 5 h), +1.5 C at 5 h 10 min; warm bath 25 to 30 C for 4 h is 8 doublings at an assumed 30 min, 256x; fridge 5 C limit and water rule quoted from memory (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Square That Came Out Small, Saint Ebba's hall (invented), near Coldingham, Berwickshire, Sat 12 Nov 2050; text only.)* Knitting gauge: 40 stitches at 24 per 10 cm is 16.7 cm, at 18 is 22.2; a 30 cm square needs 60 / 72 / 54 stitches (rows 84 / 96 / 78) at gauges 20 / 24 / 18; washed swatch 6 g per 100 cm2, 3 x 4 squares plus 5 cm border (100 x 130) is about 780 g, 8 skeins of 100 g, one dye lot; wool relaxing in the wash quoted from memory (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Pan on the Scale, Saint Finbarr's hall (invented), near Bantry, Co. Cork, Sat 16 Jul 2050; text only.)* Jam by weight: 1 kg fruit at an assumed 8% sugar plus 800 g sugar is 1,800 g with 880 g sugar; 65% needs 880/0.65 = 1,354 g so 446 g of water must go (60% would be 1,467 g); a 2,100 g pan reads 3,900 at the start and 3,454 at the target; thermometer reads 99 in boiling water so the target is 104 not 105; cold-plate wrinkle test; 65%, 105 C and 8% quoted from memory (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Lamp Before Dawn, Saint Ita's glebe (invented), near Abbeyfeale, Co. Limerick, Fri 18 Nov 2050; text only.)* Hens and day length: about 8.5 h of light on 18 Nov, 7 h 40 min at the solstice; 14 h needs 5.5 to 6 h 20 min of morning lamp (12 W, 76 Wh a day), but the keeper and assistant cap it at 13 h until February because the welfare question is open.
- *(Done: The Urn at the End of the Reel, Saint Kenelm's field (invented), near Winchcombe, Gloucestershire, Sat 15 July 2051; text only.)* Extension-cable voltage drop: 3 kW urn = 13 A, 17.6 ohm; 120 m of conductor at 0.0175 ohm mm2/m gives 2.1 ohm (1.0 mm2) or 0.84 (2.5 mm2); series current 11.7 / 12.45 A, drop 24.5 V / 10.5 V (4.5%), urn 2,400 / 2,730 W; 20 L x 88 K = 7,360 kJ, 41 min ideal, 51 / 45; cable heat 4.7 / 2.2 W/m; the rating (about 10 A coiled thin flex, from memory) and RCD matter more than the volts; meter at the far socket checks (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Weighed Bucket, Saint Hilda's hall (invented), near Kirkbymoorside, North Yorkshire, Sat 18 Oct 2053; text only.)* Counting coins by weight plus the stall tally: 50p 8.00 g, £1 8.75 g (from memory); 16V - 7.25b = W, so V 104, W 1,157 g gives 70 pound coins and 68 fifties; V 96, W 1,113 g gives 58.3 (not whole), a stray 12 g £2 coin, then 60 and 72; a 20-coin sample is off by about 0.11 in the share.
- *(Done: The Butt That Filled in an Afternoon, Saint Decuman's allotment shed (invented), near Watchet, Somerset, Sat 4 Oct 2053; text only.)* Rain butt: 7.68 m2 roof at an assumed 80% is 6.14 L per mm, a 200 L butt fills at 32.6 mm, 25 mm gives 154 L, 40 mm gives 246 L (46 lost to one butt); 12 beds x 10 L = 120 L a week, 360 L over a 21-day dry spell, two butts hold 400 L (65 mm); efficiency and prices guessed (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Last Tray In, Saint Walstan's hall (invented), near Diss, Norfolk, Sat 27 Sep 2053; text only.)* Dough proving vs temperature: ready = 60 x 2^((24-T)/10) min (79 at 20 C, 104 at 16, 138 at 12, 182 at 8, 224 at 5), over at 1.5x; seven trays on a 40-minute oven cycle from minute 80 (80..320) each matched to a room whose window holds it; trial roll per room; Q10 of 2, 1.5x limit and finger-dent test quoted from memory (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Pile That Smells of Ammonia, Saint Modwen's community garden (invented), near Burton upon Trent, Staffordshire, Sat 11 Oct 2053; text only.)* Compost C:N: 120 kg grass at 20% dry matter = 24 kg dry, C 10.8 (45%), N 0.635 at C:N 17; target 30 with leaves at 60 gives x = 36.7 kg dry (43 as weighed, 85% dry matter); leaf ratio 40 gives 73 dry, 80 gives 29, so add by the bag and trust smell and temperature; all ratios quoted from memory (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Shelf That Smiles, Saint Brannoc's parish library (invented), near Braunton, Devon, Sat 20 Jan 2052; text only.)* Plywood shelf sag: 1.2 m span, 0.6 m deep, 18 mm, 40 kg: 5WL^3/(384EI) = 6.1 mm (E 5 GPa quoted from memory), L/198 vs L/300 rule of thumb; stress 1.8 MPa so stiffness not strength; 36 mm gives 0.76 mm, a middle bracket (two separate spans) 0.38 mm (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Back Row and the Front, Saint Aidan's hall (invented), near Wooler, Northumberland, Sat 8 Mar 2053; text only.)* Hall PA: 94 dB at 1 m gives 88.0 at 2 m, 69.9 at 16 m; room noise 60 plus 15 wants 75; +5.1 at the speaker is 93.1 at the front (1.2 h on the 85 dB / 3 dB workplace guide), +10 is 98 (24 min); a second 82 dB speaker 2 m from the back gives 76.9 there, front unchanged; delay about 51 ms (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: One More Post Than Panels, Saint Colman's glebe (invented), near Hawick, Scottish Borders, Sat 16 May 2048; text only.)* Fence 24 m centre to centre: 13.1 panels is the wrong sum; n bays need n + 1 posts; bay = 1.83 + 0.1 = 1.93 m, 12 bays = 23.16, remainder 0.84 gives a cut panel of 0.74; with 95 mm posts 1.925, 23.1, 0.9 (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: Two Coats and a Tin, Saint Oswin's hall (invented), near Hexham, Northumberland, Sat 14 Sep 2047; text only.)* Paint: 38 x 3.4 = 129.2 m2 less 14.4 openings = 114.8; label 12 m2/L gives 9.6 L a coat, 19.1 for two; test patch 2 m2 took 200 ml = 10 m2/L so 23 L, +5% = 24, order 25 (2x10 + 5), boxed; rough-wall allowance quoted from memory (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Short Load, Saint Egbert's churchyard (invented), near Thirsk, North Yorkshire, Sat 13 Apr 2047; text only.)* Gravel path 38 x 1.2 x 0.075 = 3.42 m3 finished; x1.3 loose-to-compacted (quoted from memory) = 4.45 m3; x1.6 t/m3 = 7.1 t; 6 t lays 32 m of 38; order 7.5 t (+5%); the assistant owns that it checked the volume but not the order (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Plate Is for a Cold Tyre, Cill Chriostóir parish car park (invented), near Ballina, Co. Mayo, Sat 6 Mar 2049; text only.)* Tyre pressure: P2/P1 = T2/T1 in absolute terms; 2.5 bar gauge = 3.51 abs at 278 K; air at 313 K gives 3.96 abs, 2.94 gauge; bled to 2.5 hot, cold it is 3.12 abs, 2.11 gauge, 0.39 bar (16%) low; 40 C air and quarter-low warning threshold assumed or quoted from memory (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Wet Road at the School Gate, Saint Bride's school (invented), near Biggar, South Lanarkshire, Tue 17 Nov 2048; text only.)* Stopping distance: thinking 0.67 s (Highway Code, from memory) = 9 m at 30 mph (13.4 m/s), 6 m at 20; braking v^2/2a with a 6.7 dry (13.5 m at 30, 6 at 20), halved wet (27, 12); child 25 m ahead: 30 dry 22.5 stops, 20 wet 18 stops, 30 wet 36 needs, impact v^2 = 180 - 2x3.3x16 = 73, 8.6 m/s, about 19 mph; wet-halving is a rule of thumb (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Peas in the Jar, Saint Aldhelm's fete field (invented), near Malmesbury, Wiltshire, Sat 14 June 2048; text only.)* Jar count: 10 peas = 80 mm so 8 mm each, ball volume 4/3 pi r^3 = 268 mm3; 1.8 L x 64% random packing (quoted from memory) = 1,152 cm3, about 4,300 peas (4,000 to 4,600 for 60 to 68%); diameter is cubed so 7.5 to 8.5 mm gives 3,500 to 5,100; count 4,212, 38 guesses, median 3,100, one 60,000 (all figures invented except the arithmetic).
- *(Done: The Nineteenth of March, Saint Cuthman's school (invented), near Steyning, West Sussex, Fri 7 Feb 2048; text only.)* Birthday problem: class of 28 = 378 pairs x 1/365, expected 1.04, P(some match) 65% (23 pupils 51%, 30 pupils 71%); a match for one named child 7.1% (27 others); 204 pupils, given child 43%; uniform days assumed, seasonal skew quoted from memory; 19 March is Saint Joseph (all names invented).
- *(Done: The Remainder After Four-Forty, Saint Fillan's hall (invented), near Crieff, Perthshire, Dec 2047; text only.)* Raffle by dice: 3 d10 = 1000 rolls, mod 440 leaves 120 spare, so tickets 440 and 1 to 119 get 3/1000, the rest 2/1000; Brennans' tickets 1 to 40 = 12% vs 9.09% fair, three in a row 0.173% vs 0.075% (x2.3); some household of 54 + Brennans: sum p^3 = 0.10% per window, ~0.8% over 8 windows; re-roll outside 001 to 440, accept 44%, 2.27 rolls a draw; lottery rules left to the local authority (all figures invented).
- *(Done: The Turn-Round Time, Saint Ceinwen's parish (invented), near Rhayader, Powys, All Saints 1 Nov 2048; text only.)* Hill walk timing by Naismith (5 km/h + 1 h per 600 m, 1892) at group pace 3 km/h + 1 min per 10 m: 7.5 km, 480 m up = 198 min, down 150 min, stops 40 min, 6 h 28 total; start 11:00 home 17:28 after sunset (~16:50, from the vestry almanac); start 10:00 home 16:28; turn-round 13:40 = 16:20 minus 2.5 h walking and 10 min stops; the leader turns at the clock, not at the chapel (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: Six Centimetres in the Middle, Saint Botolph's Green (invented), Fenwick Parva, Cambridgeshire, Boxing Day 2049; text only.)* Pond ice: Stefan's law h = sqrt(2 k FDD / (rho L)) = 3.5 cm x sqrt(freezing-degree-days) (k 2.2, rho 917, L 334 kJ/kg); days of -2, -5, -6, -4, -3 give FDD 20 and a ceiling of 15.7 cm, about 12 with a 2.7 coefficient (quoted from memory); drilled 9 cm at the bank, 6 mid-pond, 3 at the spring inlet, so the holes beat the sum and the thinnest place decides; ~10 cm guide for one person on foot quoted from memory, check with fire and rescue; skate becomes a walk on the grass (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Tank That Covers a Quarter, Saint Fiacre's allotments (invented), near Swaffham, Norfolk, March 2049; text only.)* Rain catchment: 140 m² roof x 0.8 runoff = 112 L/mm; driest July 11 mm gives ~1,230 L (first flush 140 L a shower, ~700 L net); 200 m² x 4 mm/d x 31 d = 24,800 L, so a 10,000 L tank covers 45%, 5,000 L about 25%; 80 m² drip at 2 mm/d is 4,960 L, covered by 5,000 L + 700 L; runoff factor, crop need and drip rate quoted from memory, rain book invented; mesh on overflow, not-for-drinking sign (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Candlemas Air, Saint Elwin's hall (invented), near Alston, Cumbria, Candlemas Sunday 2 Feb 2053; text only.)* Hall CO2: 9x12x4.5 = 486 m3, 60 people x 18 L/h = 1,080 L/h, so ~2,200 ppm/h sealed; at 0.3 air changes/h 3,100 at 1.5 h and 3,800 at 2 h (430 outdoor), meter agreeing is luck; 12 K heat cost 1.95 kW per air change (1.2 kg/m3, 1 kJ/kg/K); one extra change levels off near 2,100, three near 1,200; 5,000 ppm working-day limit quoted from memory; the 91-year-old and the baby decide how far to open (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Freezer That Keeps a Day, Saint Ninian's hall (invented), near Elgin, Moray, 3 to 5 Jan 2053; text only.)* Power cut, 200 kg frozen food: 200 x 2 kJ/kg/K x 17 K = 6,800 kJ (1.9 kWh); at ~1 kWh/day heat leak that is ~45 h; thermometer -18 to -9 in 16 h is 0.56 K/h, so -1 at ~46 h; both routes agree to the guess; thaw plateau makes temperature a poor guide near 0; half moved to a generator freezer, half handed out; refreezing/thaw-hour guidance quoted from memory (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Apples Off the Ground, Saint Kenelm's orchard (invented), near Ledbury, Herefordshire, Sat 10 Oct 2048; text only.)* Parish cider: 20 L is ~21 kg at 3.9 kJ/kg/K, 12 to 71 C is 4,830 kJ (1.34 kWh); a 2 kW ring at 70% gives ~57 min a batch; 240 kg x 0.65 L/kg is ~156 L (8 batches, ~7.5 h) so only 40 L heated for the children's table, the rest sold labelled raw; 71 C (160 F) quoted from memory; ice-water check, reading 2 means 69 in the pot; patulin survives heat, so rot is binned (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Last Light on the Hill Road, Saint Aidan's chapel (invented), above the Breamish valley, Northumberland, Sat 9 Dec 2051; text only.)* Winter pilgrimage timing: slowest walker 22 min/km (2.7 km/h), 4.5 km and 300 m up; Naismith (from memory) adds 10 min per 100 m, so up 99+30 = 129 min, down 99; a 15:00 Mass ends 15:45 and reaches the gate 17:24, about an hour after the end of civil twilight (~16:20); sunset ~15:40 and twilight length quoted from memory, look up the date; Mass at 12:30 with a 10:20 start gives the gate at 14:54, about 40 min before sunset; half-way turn-back by 11:45; Mr Teague's choice is his own (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Tail of the Procession, Saint Oswin's (invented), Northumberland, Corpus Christi Sunday, June 2050; text only.)* Procession timing: 480 walkers 4 abreast is 120 rows x 1.2 m = 144 m (+20 = 164 m); 20 m in 20.5 s is 0.98 m/s, in 36.4 s is 0.55 m/s; 1,100 m takes 18.7 min at 0.98 (tail passes a point in 2.8 min), 33.3 min at 0.55 (tail 38.3, past a 30-minute closure); frame walkers start 480 m out so 620 m at 0.55 is 18.8 min, arriving with the head; crowd stretching not modelled; council marshal rules and who walks left to the walkers and the licensing office (all figures invented).
- *(Done: The Ringing Chamber at Ninety-Three, Saint Wystan's (invented), Hartwell Magna, Suffolk, February 2050; text only.)* Bell-ringers' noise dose: energy average of 92.8/93.4/93.1 is 93.1 dB(A); dose = L + 10 log10(h/8), so 2 h gives 87.1 against 85, and 85 is reached in about 1.26 h (3 dB exchange); Sunday 40 min at 91 gives 80.2; felt-sleeved rope holes and a flap cut it to 86.2 (dose 80.2); GB action values 80/85/87 quoted from memory, volunteer status unchecked; ear whine goes to a GP/audiology first (all readings invented).
- *(Done: The Hay That Warms Itself, Saint Colum Cille's field (invented), near Kells, Co. Meath, last week of August 2049; text only.)* Damp hay self-heating: kitchen microwave drying test, 120 g to 94 g is about 22% moisture (other samples 20, 25, 19) against a keeping limit of about 15% for small squares; probe 38 / 42 / 55 C over four days in an 18 C shed; checkpoints quoted from memory (check twice a day above about 50, ring the fire service near 65, fire risk above about 70); do not open or climb a hot stack, brigade opens it with water ready (core 72 C, 101 of 400 bales lost; all readings invented).
- *(Done: The Third Hamper, Saint Fachtna's (invented), near Skibbereen, Co. Cork, Sunday 12 Dec 2049; text only.)* Raffle run: 12 of 516 tickets is 1 in 43 a year, 1 in 79,507 three years running for that one family; any of 40 households (shares cubed, mix 2x40, 4x24, 4x20, 10x12, 10x8, 10x6) in a given 3 years is 0.17% (1 in 570); any household, any 3 in a row over 20 years is 2.93% (1 in 34, exact Markov sum, same mix each year assumed); sum cannot show fair or crooked; fix is a numbered stub book, one drum emptied in public, a child drawing, winning numbers published (all counts invented).
- *(Done: The Gazebo That Slides, Saint Cuimín's (invented), near Kinvara, Co. Galway, Sunday 6 Dec 2048; text only.)* Wind on a 3 m walled gazebo (7.2 m2, Cd 1.2, rho 1.25): 60 km/h gives 174 Pa dynamic, 208 Pa, about 1,500 N; tipping needs 1,500 x 1.2 = 1,800 N m against 122 kg x 1.5 m (22 kg frame guessed + 4 x 25 kg), so tips at about 60 km/h; sliding with mu 0.5 at 38 km/h, with a 52 kgf scale pull (mu 0.425, invented) at 35 km/h; 350 kg would be needed to hold at 60; walls off, ropes to railings, lift not calculated (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Tower That Waits, Saint Mochua's Well (invented), near Balla, Co. Mayo, 15 August 2048; text only.)* Outdoor Mass PA: level = 100 - 20 log10(r) dB (100 dB at 1 m from the plate), so 74/68/61.4/60 at 20/40/85/100 m against a guessed 60 dB noise floor and a rule-of-thumb 10 dB margin; tower 55 m down the field needs 55/343 = 160 ms (343 m/s at 20 C; 336 at 8 C moves it 3.5 ms) plus 10 ms precedence, so at 85 m the tower lands 9.5 ms after the altar and the pair sum to about 71 dB; undelayed it would echo 160 ms late; echo threshold 30 to 50 ms quoted from memory (noise, plate and rules of thumb are illustrative; field acoustics ignored).
- *(Done: The Freezers in the Dark, Saint Brigid's (invented), near Dysart, Renfrew County, Ontario, 13 to 16 January 2048; text only.)* Pantry power cut: 1.3 W/K leak (250 kWh/yr label, COP 1.8, 22 C to -18 C); 2 kJ/kg/K and 70% water at 334 kJ/kg; reach -2 C at 4 C room in about 61 / 30 / 12 h for 110 / 55 / 22 kg (lumped model, real freezers warm unevenly; ~48 h full / 24 h half quoted from memory of US guidance); generator ~5.5 h to pull Big back down, outdoors only, CO warning (readings, outage length and people invented).
- *(Done: The Loop That Leaks, Saint Teilo's (invented), above Llandovery, Carmarthenshire, 4 to 11 November 2049; text only.)* Church hearing loop, one turn round 18 x 10 m: vertical field at 1.2 m averages 0.088 A/m per amp (0.069 centre; Biot-Savart, steel ignored), so 400 mA/m needs 4.55 A; installer's 2 A gives 176 mA/m (-7 dB; third pew -8 dB); loop 73 uH, 0.64 ohm, so 3.6 V at 1 kHz and 10.9 V at 5 kHz; a 3 A / 12 V amplifier tops out at 264 mA/m (-3.6 dB), two turns need 21 V at 5 kHz, so a 6 A / 20 V unit; leak next door 124 / 89 / 49 / 19 mA/m at 3 / 4 / 6 / 10 m (calculated, not measured), hall users moved to the far end; 400 mA/m and +-3 dB quoted from memory of IEC 60118-4 (illustrative figures, plates and readings invented).
- *(Done: The Wooden Spoon, Saint Colman's Hall (invented), near Swinford, Co. Mayo, October 2048; text only.)* Allergen cross-contact: a "nut-free" card cannot be printed for a stew cooked with a wooden spoon from a drawer that held the peanut-brittle spoon; 50 mg peanut dust in 20 L is 1 mg in a 400 ml bowl, about 0.25 mg protein vs the 0.2 mg (1 in 100) reference dose used in VITAL-type schemes (illustrative, uneven mixing ignored); card reads "no nuts in the recipe ... cannot guarantee free of traces"; fresh pan and spoon, her bowl first; pens checked in date; adrenaline early, 999/112 regardless, second pen at 5 min (spoon dust amount invented).
- *(Done: The Pot on the Cold Stove, Saint Senan's Hall (invented), near Kilrush, Co. Clare, a Saturday in May 2049; text only.)* Cooling 20 L of soup: ~80 kJ/K, lidded pot on a stove at ~4 W/K (guess) gives tau 5.6 h, so 90 C at 17:00 reads 53 C at 21:00, passes 63 at ~19:37 and 57 at ~20:25, and is ~32 C at 06:00; stirred in running 11 C tap water at ~40 W/K (guess) tau 33 min, 53 to 21 C in ~49 min (done ~21:50, inside the committee's 57-to-21-in-2-h rule); six shallow tubs to the fridge, reheat to 75 C, plain sandwich for the frail; assistant will not say safe or ruined (illustrative figures; guidance differs by country, perfringens doubling ~10 min near 45 C).
- *(Done: The Chairs Behind the Car, Saint Eunan's Hall (invented), near Letterkenny, Co. Donegal, a Saturday in June 2051; text only.)* Trailer plate 750 kg gross, 230 unladen, so 520 payload; 120 chairs x 4.6 kg + 6 tables x 14 kg = 636 kg, 116 over; two trips (80 chairs + 6 tables = 452 kg, 682 gross; then 40 chairs + sound system about 244 kg); nose weight rule of thumb 4 to 7 % of the loaded trailer (27 to 48 kg at 682) against a car limit of 50 kg (handbook figure invented), bathroom-scale reading 44 then 39 kg after moving a column back; tyres to the plate's 41 psi; no claim the single trip would have failed (illustrative figures; plate, handbook and weights invented, brake and licence rules not covered).
- *(Done: The Oil on the Shelf, Saint Fiachra's Hall (invented), near Enniscorthy, Co. Wexford, a Saturday in September 2050; text only.)* Home-made garlic in oil: spores on the bulb, no oxygen, pH above 4.6 (raw garlic quoted about 5 to 6), toxin not detectable by smell; guidance is refrigerate at once (5 C or below) and use within about 4 days, or freeze; jars made Tue 19:00, 37 h on the pantry shelf (13 to 14 C, guessed), then a fridge reading 9 C, are 85 h old on Saturday 08:00, so the 4-day clock is met on paper only; no claim any jar was unsafe; bin, then fresh cubes (20 ml, 72 a load, 12 bags of 6 a load, 3 h to freeze, 24 bags, 88 euro) sold frozen with a label (illustrative figures, no lab test).
- *(Done: The Beans on Low, Saint Fintan's Hall (invented), near Clonakilty, Co. Cork, a Friday in February 2050; text only.)* Dry red kidney beans put straight into slow cookers: raw beans carry phytohaemagglutinin (FDA guide: 4 or 5 raw beans can cause illness; onset 1 to 3 h); beans heated to about 80 C have been reported about 5x more toxic than raw, so the rule (FDA, UK FSA) is soak, discard the water, rolling boil at least 10 min; probe readings 93 / 88 / 82 C at 14:05 are invented and say nothing about the hours before; three batches boiled on two rings (about 15 min up, 10 min at the boil each), supper 45 min late; tinned beans are already cooked (illustrative figures, no safety claim beyond the published rule).
- *(Done: Feels Like Minus One, Saint Beuno's (invented), Glyderau above Capel Curig, Gwynedd, 5 October 2047; text only.)* Wind chill index 13.12 + 0.6215T - 11.37 V^0.16 + 0.3965 T V^0.16 (V km/h; valid T <= 10 C, V >= 4.8): 5 C at 40 km/h gives -0.7 (app shows -1), 8 C at 20 gives 4.9; fitted for bare dry skin, so says nothing of wet cotton at rest (qualitative only); lunch moved out of the wind, zip test, umbles, 999 for no shivering and strange (illustrative, wind-map halving invented).
- *(Done: The Scale at the Stile, Saint Dunstan's (invented), Pilgrims' Way, Kent, 19 June 2048; text only.)* Over-drinking on a hot walk: a 62 kg girl (37.2 L body water, Na 140 mmol/L = 5,208 mmol) drinks 5 L in 5 h, sweats about 3 L (Na 40 mmol/L, assumed, range 20 to 80) and passes 0.6 L: scale +1.4 kg, Na 5,088/38.6 L = 131.8 mmol/L; at the same pace +2.8 kg and 124; a 75 kg man who drank 2.4 L is -1.1 kg (1.5%); rule is drink to thirst (2015 consensus statement), weigh at stops, gain means stop drinking and eat salt; confusion or repeated vomiting means 999, not the water barrel (illustrative figures, urine sodium and metabolic water ignored).
- *(Done: The Cool Sun at the Pass, Santa Maria delle Nevi al Colle Sàrtor (invented), Aosta Valley, 4 to 6 August 2049; text only.)* UV index = 40 x erythemal W/m2, so 10 is 0.25 W/m2; MED 200/250/350 J/m2 (skin I/II/III) gives 13/17/23 min at index 10 (15/18.5/26 at 9); 10:30 to 13:00 at 9 is about 2,000 J/m2 (8 MED for type II); redness lags 4 to 24 h; sun 47 deg at 11:00, 55 at noon, 61 at 13:35 CEST, so a 6 m wall shadows 5.6 / 4.2 m but on the west side shades nothing; open-sky shade halves dose (guess); SPF 30 at a quarter of the tested 2 mg/cm2 acts like about 2 to 3 (rule of thumb); thin cloud passes 80 to 90% (illustrative figures; burn count invented).
- *(Done: Sixteen Amps and a Hot Cupboard, Saint Declan's Hall (invented), near Ardmore, Co. Waterford, 14 December 2048; text only.)* Socket-circuit load: urn 2800 W (12.2 A) + hot cupboard 1500 W (6.5 A) + microwave 1700 W input (7.4 A) = 6,000 W (26.1 A) on a B16 (3,680 W; about 2,944 W continuous), 1.6x rating so it trips within the hour; a bigger breaker protects the wire not the kitchen, so refused; hot cupboard + microwave to the unused 32 A kitchen ring (13.9 A), urn alone on the 16 (12.2 A), kettle 9.6 A kept off; coiled reel derated (16 A unwound, ~6 A wound, plate not seen); electrician in January (illustrative figures; trip curve and cable size not known).
- *(Done: The Morning It Was Too Cold, Our Lady of Perpetual Help (invented), Nelson Lake, Minnesota, 7 to 16 January 2049; text only.)* PV string Voc rises in the cold: 41.6 V x (1 + 0.0027 x (25 - T)); 13 panels read 564 V at 9 C but about 608 V at -21 (612 at -24) against a 600 V inverter; limit -15.5 C; 12 panels give 585.5 V at the -39 C record (595 at 0.30 %/K), good to about -50 C; 26 panels become 2 x 12, 7.7 % of power lost; rewired in the dark by a licensed electrician (illustrative figures, irradiance and panel-vs-air temperature ignored; what over-voltage does to a unit is not claimed).
- *(Done: The Tap That Let Go at Night, San Isidro de Quinuamayo (invented), Cajamarca, Peru, 12 to 15 May 2048; text only.)* Gravity line 2,000 m of 26 mm bore PE, fall 75.4 m (gauge 7.4 bar shut; phone map said 78): taps shut is the highest pressure, not the lowest; Hazen-Williams C=140, 0.22 L/s peak (240 people x 20 L/day x 4) loses 19.9 m, so 5.4 bar flowing vs 7.4 shut against fittings rated 6.3; pulse 300 x 0.41/9.81 = 12.7 m (speed assumed 250 to 400); break-pressure tank at 38 m fall gives 3.7 bar shut, about 5.0 with pulse, 28 m flowing; opening the spring valve wider does nothing shut and 0.35 L/s would lose 47 m (illustrative figures; water not tested, no safety claim).
- *(Done: The Wind at Two O'Clock, San Bernardo di Prato Alto (invented), Piedmont Alps, 2 to 3 November 2048; text only.)* Cargo drone 17 kg out / 14 kg back, 4 rotors 0.8 m (A 2.01 m2), hover P = T^1.5/sqrt(2 rho A)/(0.65 x 0.85) = about 1,870 W at 1,250 m (rho 1.085); cruise 0.75 x hover (assumed); 11 km, +1,300 m (60 Wh); 900 Wh usable, plan <= 675; round trip 558 Wh calm, 594 at 3 m/s, 617 at 4, 688 at 6, 906 at 9 headwind; limit about 5.4 m/s; one-way at 9 m/s leaves 127 Wh (illustrative, body angle and gusts ignored).
- *(Done: Ten Pounds at Three and a Half Thousand, Santa Rosa de Chaupimayo (invented), Cusco region, Peru, 12 to 14 June 2049; text only.)* Pressure canner at 3,400 m: air 66.6 kPa; 10 psi weight gives 135.6 kPa abs = 108.3 C vs 115.3 C at sea level; 116 C needs 174 kPa abs = 15.6 psi gauge here (15 psi 115.2 C, 20 psi 121 C); z=10 C spore-kill ratio about 4x at 115 and 19x at 108 relative to 121 (illustration only); no process time given, lab and checked dial gauge do that (steam-table figures, not tested).
- *(Done: One in Four and a Board, Saint Moling's (invented), Ballinvarrig, Co. Carlow, 6 to 8 November 2047; text only.)* Ladder statics on a smooth wall: friction needed = (fW + w/2)/(W+w) x cot(theta) = 0.949 cot(theta) for 80 kg at the top of a 9 kg ladder; 63.6 deg (foot 2.0 m) needs 0.47, 75.6 deg (foot 1.12 m, rest 4.36 m) needs 0.24; shoe friction measured by dragging a loaded foot with a spring scale (0.31 wet, 0.58 dry, invented readings); tie the top; wall friction, sideways slip and wind not modelled.
- *(Done: The Banner in the Gust, Porthgwyn (invented), north Cornwall, 2 to 3 March 2052; text only.)* Wind load on a processional banner: q = 0.5 x 1.25 x v^2, force = q x Cd (1.2 assumed) x 2.16 m2; 9 m/s gives 131 N predicted vs about 120 N on a luggage scale (Cd nearer 1.1); 15 m/s gives 140 Pa and 364 N; upper-hand grip about 3.4 x the push (lever 2.2 m, hands 0.5 and 1.0 m above the belt cup) = 1,240 N against a 52 kg boy's 510 N; rule: grip = carrier's weight at about 9.6 m/s, furl above it (furled about 0.26 m2, 36 N at 15 m/s); 6 m/s in the lee gives 58 N and 200 N of grip (illustrative figures, Cd and lever simplified).
- *(Done: The Clock That Gains in Winter, Saint Ninian's (invented), Wigtownshire, December 2048; text only.)* Steel seconds pendulum, L = g/pi^2 = 994 mm, rate change = 0.5 x 12e-6/K x dT x 86,400 = 0.52 s/day per K; 12 C to 2.4 C predicts 5.0 s/day vs 5.3 measured (37 s a week); rating nut 1 mm pitch = 43 s/day per mm, so one of 12 marks (0.083 mm) is 3.6 s/day; August at 22 C loses about 5 (illustrative; air, friction and rod-vs-thermometer temperature ignored).
