- *(Done: The Fish Goes In Frozen, Saint Cunegund's (invented), Kewaunee County, Wisconsin, 20 and 27 March 2048; text only.)* Parish fish fry on a 20 L (18.4 kg) outdoor propane fryer: 1 g of water under 175 C oil is about 1.7 L of steam, so a wet 3 kg basket (3 to 10% water, guessed) is 150 to 500 L in a 38 L pot; 3 kg frozen fish (65% water, assumed) drops the oil to about 123 C against about 146 C for 1.5 kg; burner 15 kW with 10.5 kW into oil reaches smoke (about 230 C) in about 3 min and about 330 C in about 9 min; the "thaw first" rule is a turkey rule, owned by the assistant (illustrative figures, not measured).
- *(Done: The Ring of Stones, Cill Eoin (invented), west Clare, Ireland, 23 June 2047; text only.)* Bonfire radiant heat: 1,206 kg x 15 MJ/kg = 18 GJ over 45 min, peak 1.5x average = 10 MW, radiant 0.3 = 3 MW; q = P/(4 pi r^2) gives 6.6 kW/m2 at 6 m, 1.66 at 12 m (limit about 1.6 long exposure, 4.7 for 15 s), the 300 kg fire gave 1.66 at 6 m; hay barn at 14 m gets 1.2 against about 12.5 piloted ignition, so embers decide (illustrative figures, point-source model good to about a factor of two).
- *(Done: The Hour Bell, Saint Aldhelm's (invented), Combe Aldhelm, Dorset, 21 Feb to 5 Mar 2047; text only.)* Bell-chamber noise: 112 dB(A) at 1 m (meter, not a clipping phone), 6 dB less per doubling of distance, 3 dB exchange rate from 85 dB for 8 h (NIOSH); 56 strokes x 3 s = 168 s a day is about 300% at 1 m, 75% at 2 m, 19% at 4 m; foam plug (NRR 29, real about 11 dB) is safe but hides the 3 kHz+ double strike, 17 dB flat filters give about 6% (illustrative figures, not measured).
- *(Done: The Dust on the Beams, St Wenceslaus Hall (invented), Saline County, Nebraska, 10 to 11 October 2049; text only.)* Grain dust: floor 240 g/m2 x 360 m2 = 86 kg, ledges 160 g/m2 x 220 m2 = 35 kg, 121 kg in 2,160 m3 = 56 g/m3 against about 50 to 60 minimum explosible; after damp mopping and an earthed dust-rated vacuum about 20 kg, 9 g/m3; the assistant cannot name an ignition source and owns it (illustrative figures).
- *(Done: The Pot That Needs Dry Air, Notre-Dame de la Visitation, Nabdogo (invented), Centre-Nord, Burkina Faso, 26 Apr to 5 May 2049; text only.)* Pot-in-pot cooler is limited by wet-bulb (Stull): 41 C/18% gives 22.6, pot about 25; 36 C/55% gives 28.4, pot about 31; insulin 11 people x 36 U/day x 9 d = 3,564 U (3.6 vials, keep 5); leaflet is 28 days at not above 25 C; ice box 1.2 W/K x 30 K = 36 W, 10 kg ice (3.3 MJ) about 26 h; ice touching vials froze 2 (illustrative figures, not tested).
- *(Done: The Door Left Open at the Vigil, Kapel van Sint-Willibrord (invented), Zeeland, 17 April 2049; text only.)* CO2 in an airtight chapel: 64 people at 18 L/h plus 90 candles at 10 L/h each in 280 m3; shut, 5,600 ppm (people only) or 9,200 (with candles); doors and casements open (3.6 air changes an hour from a 2,400 to 1,400 decay in 12 min) and candles lit twice for 9 min hold about 1,800; open-door heat 4.6 kW against about 5 kW of bodies; the assistant's sensor-by-the-door error is owned.
- *(Done: The Stone That Keeps the Sun, Santa Maria della Calce (invented), Salento, June 2048; text only.)* Corpus Christi procession in a heat wave: WBGT = 0.7 nwb + 0.2 globe + 0.1 air gives 31.8 at 17:00 (air 35.5, globe 52, wet 25.5) and 27.7 at 19:30; flagstones 51 C in sun vs about 42 C, barefoot vow; water 310 L needed vs 258 L held, 62 L short; faint vs heat stroke (112).
- *(Done: The Count Between, Kaplnka Panny Márie Snežnej (invented), Low Tatras, August 2048; text only.)* Flash-to-bang at 340 m/s: 24 s = 8.2 km, 15 s = 5.1 km, 28 km/h closing, about 11 min; two readings give only the radial speed; 20 m rolling sphere and a 6 m rod cover sqrt(6x34) = 14.3 m, the cross 40 m off is outside; 30 min after last thunder.
- *(Done: The Night the Blossom Was Wet, Kingsbarrow, Herefordshire, April 2048; text only.)* Frost sprinklers: 334 kJ/kg, 3 mm/h, 400 m3 pond, 2 of 3 ha; wet-bulb start (-0.5 C) not air at 0 C; stopping early is worse than not starting.
- *(Done: The Funeral Soup, Santa Marija tal-Qlajja, Gozo, 2044; text only.)* A 100 L pot left overnight: 13 h time constant, 57 C at 06:17, 46 C at 11:00; 12 shallow pans in ice water need about 100 kg of ice (36 MJ / 355 kJ per kg); rule cited is the US Food Code's, not Maltese law.
- *(Done: The First Draw, Scoil Bhríde, Cill Mhuire, Co. Longford, 2037; text only.)* A water office says flush for thirty seconds; ten one-litre bottles show the lead pipe (11 m x 0.28 L/m = 3.1 L, behind 2.3 L of copper) sits at litres 2.3 to 5.4, so thirty seconds (3 L) makes the first cup about seven times worse (21 vs 3.1 micrograms a litre); a two-minute flush, filters, no part-replacement, and the school's roof fund pays for its 7 m.
- *(Done: Before the Fourteenth, Pé da Serra, Alentejo, 2050; text only.)* Altar-wine harvest date, pH and free sulphur, and a chiller; the old vines risked on purpose.
- *(Done: The Long Sea, Newfoundland, 2045 to 2046.)* *A sea-farm in 2046:* a seaweed and shellfish cooperative off Newfoundland
  (about twenty years ahead) and an AI that schedules harvests; the conflict is
  an old fisher who will not sell and a storm season the models got wrong.
- *(Done: The Bell at Half Past Three, Camino, 2060.)* *A second Camino story:* hospitaleros, heat and a water-sharing
  rule; hard science is wet-bulb temperature and sweat.
- *(Done: Thirty-Nine Minutes, 2139.)* *The Mars sisters:* a contemplative community on a long-duration
  station keeping the Hours on a day 24 hours 39.6 minutes long; the problem is
  calendar and liturgy, not drama in a spacesuit.
- *(Done: The Control Vial, Cheparaa Health Centre, Kenya, 2041.)* A solar vaccine fridge freezes its top shelf; the cold-chain AI will not certify the doses without a shake test, and the test takes a night.
- *(Done: Forty-Eight Degrees, Tigh Naomh Pádraig, Wicklow, 2049; text only.)* A hospice heat pump set to 48 C saves 630 euro a year and sits in Legionella's range; a 3.2 m dead leg, 400 CFU/L at the nearest shower, store at 60 and mix to 43.
- *(Done: The Lid That Stayed Shut, Lac-des-Aulnes, Quebec, January 2049; text only.)* A full chest freezer in a 41-hour outage: lumped model, 240 kg x 2 kJ/kg/K = 480 kJ/K over 1.6 W/K gives tau 83 h, minus 8 C at 41 h, minus 3 at 72 h; printed rule 48 h is the one to act on; the 60 kg upright reaches 0 C near 22 h.
- *(Done: Point Two, Tom na Croise, Braes of Glenlivet, 2048; text only.)* A parish hall sealed from 1.0 to 0.2 air changes an hour hides nothing from its old gas heater: an alarm's 41 ppm peak (47 mg/m³) after two hours gives about 8.5 g/h of CO (8.2 to 9.4 across 0.15 to 0.3 ach); the cause is a bird's nest in the flue.
- *(Done: The Second Echo, Saint Ethelwold's, Ings Ness, North York Moors, 2049; text only.)* Sabine's rule (T = 0.161 V / A): 1,000 m³ and 110 sabins give 1.46 s; lifting 90 m² of carpet (0.30 to 0.06) leaves 88 sabins and 1.82 s; an assisted-resonance system takes it to 2.6 s (62 sabins) with the loop run 4 dB below ringing (1,183 Hz); off for readings, a notice at the door.
- *(Done: The Smoke That Stays, Saint Ottilia's, Hinterthal, Bavarian Forest, Advent 2049; text only.)* Incense in a 1,200 m³ nave: 8 g at an assumed 15 mg/g (5 to 40) gives 100 µg/m³ peak, removal 0.4 + 0.3 per hour, about 6 µg/m³ on a 24-hour mean against WHO's 15; a cheap counter good to a factor of two; incense halved, asthmatic server carries the cross, doors open after, the doctor decides the rest.
- *(Done: The Sheet and the Gap, Saint Isidore's, Dry Fork, Alberta, 2051; text only.)* 4 cm hail: 31 g, 28 m/s, 12 J (drag coefficient 0.6, good to a factor of two); dew point 10.1 C at 18 C and 60%; single glazing at U = 5 puts the inner surface near 3 C at minus 5 C; protective sheets stood off 20 mm with the gap open top and bottom, quoted as conservators' practice, not derived.
- *(Done: Where the Pump Coughs, Masseria Sant'Elia, Salento, 2047; text only.)* A bottle cistern of 28.3 m² floor: 283 L per cm of stick, pump intake 0.4 m up leaves 11.3 m³ undrawable; 1.4 m³/day for 15 people (19 days at 1.05 after 3-minute showers); a 20 m³ tanker adds 71 cm; first flush 0.52 m³ per mm on 520 m²; Mass for rain and a lorry, both.
- *(Done: The Candles Breathe, Sant Ponç de la Coma, Alta Ribagorça, Easter Vigil 2049; text only.)* Sealed church, 140 people and 200 candles share 2,000 m³ at 0.3 ach: about 4,400 ppm by midnight unaffected; louvres give 1.46 ach measured from the flat reading, peak 2,260.
- *(Done: The Stack That Sweats, Llwyn Celyn Grange, Powys, 2050; text only.)* Hay baled at 24% water: 1% dry-matter loss could heat 43 t of stack by 55 K; four probes miss a hot spot a thermal camera finds, and a rule written beforehand (ring at 65) is tightened to 55 afterwards.
- *(Done: The Ring and the Bag, Ospizio San Giacomo, Via Francigena, Piedmont, 2046; text only.)* Gift mushrooms in a risotto for twelve: 1.1 kg pot, two ring-and-sheath stems in the bin (120 to 200 g of caps), 0.1 to 0.4 mg/g gives 12 to 80 mg, about 6.5 mg dangerous at 65 kg; symptoms at 10.5 h; everyone goes, walkers are rung; the rule is the ASL mushroom inspector's.
- *(Done: The Warm Pack, Sint-Willibrord, Overijssel, 11 November 2046; text only.)* A swollen e-bike pack reads 41 C against 29 C neighbours with the charger at 0.2 A (I²R about 0.004 W): the heat is internal, roughly 19 W (2.8 kJ/K, 0.06 K/min plus losses); 52 cells, 490 Wh each, 18 packs about a litre of petrol; unplug, do not carry, evacuate the hall, watch the rate.
- *(Done: The Hall with the Doors Shut, Saint Brendan's, Trout Lake, BC, 14 to 17 August 2039; text only.)* Wildfire smoke, a 960 m3 hall: steady indoor fraction = P*a/(a+k+C/V) with P 0.7, a 1/h, k 0.2/h; four sealed MERV-13 box-fan filters (600 m3/h each) give 19% of outside, eight 11%, and eight with a door propped (a 6/h) 37%; a backwards filter and lifted tape cost the first night; heat is a competing hazard, so doors open only for twelve minutes on a cool morning.
- *(Done: The Detector in the Laundry, Tre Wartha Priory, Zennor, Cornwall, December 2047 to July 2048; text only.)* Radon in a granite undercroft of 60 m3 at 0.25 ach: a 4-day canister reads 520, three-month chips 1,150 (winter; about 920 with a 0.8 seasonal factor); entry E = C*V*(a+0.0076) about 17,800 Bq/h; a fan at 1 or 2 ach gives 294 or 147, or 221 if it draws entry up by half, so a sump (entry down 85%) predicts about 172; the laundress works 1,200 h a year, 2.3 (or 1.8) times the 300 Bq/m3 workplace level; she moves upstairs first; the prediction is marked to be checked.
- *(Done: The Gauge Reads Fifteen, San Isidro de la Acequia, Colorado, 2044; text only.)* Canning at 2,300 m: boiling is 92 C, 15 psi gives 117.0 C, a dial reading 15 is truly 13 (114.5 C, about 20% short), water bath ~800x slower; beans go to one reliable canner, freezer and a tested acid pickle.
- *(Done: The Coin on the Ice, Saint Fillan's, Strathcorrie, Perthshire, 14 to 20 January 2043; text only.)* A 31.5 h ice-storm cut, 230 kg in a chest freezer: Newton cooling at a 9 C hall, tau = 63 h (437 kJ/K over about 1.9 W/K) fits -18.2, -13.3, -9.5 and predicts about -7.3 at hour 31.5 (read -7.1), -2 C at 57 h; the fridge sat above 8 C for 28 h so thirty cooked meals go; a coin on frozen water is the logger-free check.
- *(Done: The Water in the Honey, Sainte-Anne-des-Landes, Brittany, 2043; text only.)* 180 kg of honey at 19.8% water: 144.4 kg solids, 35.6 kg water; 17.8% target means 175.7 kg, so 4.3 kg of water leaves; heat damages it, so dry it at ~30 C and 45 to 50% RH in shallow trays, about 0.3 points a day; the legal 20% is not a safety line.
- *(Done: The Rope and the Count, Capela de Santa Bárbara, Beira Baixa, 17 June 2048; text only.)* Flash-to-bang at 343 m/s (2.9 s/km): 30 s is 10.3 km, 18 s is 6.2 km, so 31 km/h and about 12 minutes; a tower without a rod is not a place for a wet rope, the village is rung by phone instead, wait 30 minutes after the last thunder.
- *(Done: The Clean Plate, Monastero di Santa Scolastica, Valmala, Piedmont, 11 May 2047; text only.)* Low-gluten hosts for a coeliac child: 600 x 0.6 g, 100 ppm is 0.06 mg a host against ~10 mg/day, so the average is not the danger, a crumb is (5 g flour = ~500 mg in one host); a clean pool of 50 misses 1 bad host 92%, 20 bad 17%; a swab finds gluten on the shared griddle plate, a spare plate and a 12-host lot replace it, and the family chooses host, chalice or both.
- *(Done: The Roof Nobody Washed, Saint Ciarán's Hall, Tullyvarran, Co. Cavan, 4 to 6 October 2047; text only.)* A centenary working party is booked to jet-wash a 1974 cement-sheet roof; the hall assistant stops it until a sample is tested (0.1 fibres/cm³ = 100,000 per m³, about 150,000 an hour at hard-work breathing; no safe level claimed), the roof comes back chrysotile, the Mass goes ahead under it, and the man who washed it in 2031 is told the truth and written down.
- *(Done: The Lake Under the Stairs, Sankt Urban (invented), Zell, Mosel, 2 to 9 October 2047; text only.)* Fermenting 3,000 L of must makes about 165 m³ of CO2 (144 m³ cellar); it pools from the floor at up to 3.6 cm/h, so a wall sensor at 1.5 m reads 0.52% while the floor reads 3.4%; a floor-level extraction fan of about 600 m³/h (370 needed), a two-person rule, and a candle is not a gauge.
- *(Done: The Number on the Pier, Quoyness (invented), Orkney, 2 February 2046; text only.)* Candlemas children's procession at 3 C and 32 km/h: wind chill -2.7 is a bare-skin heat-loss rate, not a temperature, and frostbite is not the risk; standing 12 minutes in sleet in wet cotton is. Walk cut from 420 m to 300 m (about 8 min), blessing moved into a shed, battery candles plus one lantern, wool/fleece rule.
- *(Done: One in Eleven, Margaree Forks, Cape Breton (invented parish), 12 to 15 February 2048; text only.)* A positive newborn screen for a rare fasting disorder: assumed 1 in 10,000, 99% sensitivity, 0.1% false positives gives about 11 flagged babies in 10,000 and one true, so about 9%, not 99%; feed every 3 hours (4 at most) and a plain emergency rule until the gene test; the baptism is neither hurried nor delayed by a lab.
- *(Done: The Well That Took the Bleach, Saint Florian's, Ammonoosuc Flats, New Hampshire (invented parish), 8 to 11 September 2053; text only.)* Flooded dug well, 1 m x 6.4 m plus pipe and tank = 5,250 L; 50 mg/L is 262 g, 4.4 L of 6% bleach; first reading 11 mg/L (demand ~39), top-up 205 g (3.4 L); pH 8.1 leaves ~22% as HOCl (78% at pH 7) so hold 24 h; hose-flush ~15 L/min (5.8 h) onto gravel, not creek or septic; culture takes 48 h so the supper uses bottled water (480 L).
- *(Done: The Weight on the Pfarrsaal, Hinterbach (invented), Bregenzerwald, February 2051; text only.)* Roof snow load: 213 kg/m² core, x0.8 = 1.67 vs 1.6 kN/m² assessed, rain-on-snow to 1.87; rake reaches 58% of slope; string-line sag as written tripwire.
- *(Done: The Finger That Reads Ninety-Four, Abotsi Health Post (invented), Volta Region, Ghana, November 2048; text only.)* Pulse oximeter over-reading on darker skin: clip 94 vs referral line 90, 46 breaths/min vs 40, chest indrawing; the oximeter may only move a child toward referral; blood gas 86 at the hospital. Study figures (about 12% vs 4%) given as "about", from one 2020 paper, to be checked.
- *(Done: The Cupboard That Breathes Out, Capilla de San Isidro, Pampa de Anta (invented), Peru, 9 to 10 March 2047; text only.)* Equalising a 24-cell 48 V lead-acid bank: 0.457 L of H2 per Ah per cell, 8 A and an assumed 30% gassing gives ~26 L/h; in a 1.2 m3 cupboard at 0.3 ach that is 7.3% steady, 1% in 0.5 h, the 4% limit in 2.6 h; a 30 m3/h high extractor gives ~0.09%; charge by day, door open, no fan no charge; the 30% is a guess to measure.
- *(Done: The Ice at the Inflow, Saint Josaphat's (invented), Interlake, Manitoba, 16 to 19 January 2049; text only.)* Ice thickness = 1.7 x sqrt(640 degree-days) = 43 cm (constant 1.3 to 2.7 gives 33 to 68); auger 41 cm at the church, 22 at the inflow edge, 9 over the creek mouth; 30 cm clear as the story's cautious figure for a standing crowd; 1-10-1 cold-water teaching device, afterdrop, no alcohol, harness and feet-first ladder, under a minute (illustrative figures, not a standard).
- *(Done: The Door That Opens In, Kilcarrow (invented), Co. Mayo, 3 to 15 August 2047; text only.)* Pilgrimage crowd vs a church: 210 m2 standing at about 2 per m2 = 420 against a certificate of 450 (the certificate rules); 1,000 inside is nearly 5 per m2; doors 1.6 + 0.9 m less 0.15 m each edge = 1.9 m at about 1.3 persons/s/m = 148 a minute, 420 out in under 3 min, 1,000 in about 7; Mass moved to a 3,000 m2 field, church 200 at a time with clickers; plan range 700 to 1,300; the plan was not tested against the bad day (illustrative figures).
- *(Done: The Plate on the Door Pillar, Peña Alta (invented), Aragonese Pyrenees, 1 to 8 September 2046; text only.)* Minibus plate 3,500 kg, unladen 2,550, payload 950 (56 kg a seat); 15 singers at an assumed 78 kg plus 14 kg bags = 1,380, 45% over payload, gross 3,930 (+12%); 740 m descent = 28.5 MJ (25.4 at plate), 3.1 MJ extra is ~240 K on an assumed 28 kg of discs; all of it would be ~2,200 K so the engine must do most of it; 8% grade is ~3,080 N, 34 kW at 40 km/h for ~14 min; fix: 11 people with 6 kg bags (924 kg), heavy luggage and 4 walkers by car, weighbridge read 3,468 vs 3,474 predicted (invented); disc temperatures invented.
- *(Done: The Kettle That Concentrates, Saint Wendelin's, Dunmore Grove, Iowa (invented parish), 14 March 2050; text only.)* Private-well nitrate for infant formula: 13.2 mg/L as N vs the 10 limit (58.5 as nitrate, x4.43); boiling 3 L at ~1,500 W for 10 min evaporates ~400 g (13%), so 13.2 becomes ~15.2; 675 mL/day for a 4.5 kg baby is 8.9 vs 10.3 mg N a day (no safe dose claimed); bottled water, a sign, May retest; the assistant owns that it gave generic germ advice without reading its own autumn reading.
- *(Done: The Bottles on the Roof, Our Lady of Perpetual Help (invented), Barangay Salvacion, Leyte, 12 to 22 November 2043; text only.)* Solar disinfection after a typhoon: guide dose 500 W/m2 x 6 h = 10.8 MJ/m2 (3 kWh/m2); at an assumed 250 W/m2 under cloud it takes 12 h, so two days; 120 people x 3 L = 360 L = 180 two-litre bottles a day, 240 bottles on cloud days give 120 a day; ~0.03 m2 a bottle; water must be under about 30 NTU (settle, four-fold cloth, print-through test); PET only (the assistant corrects its first answer, 'any clear bottle'); sun does nothing for fuel or chemicals; H2S vial readings invented; chlorine arrives and the bottles stay as a second line.
- *(Done: The Black Grains, Sankt Antonius, Sundgau, Alsace, 14 to 19 August 2048; text only.)* Ergot in a third of a hectare of rye: 22 horns (1.1 g) in a 2 kg mixed sample is 0.55 g/kg, the top-of-bin sample twice as bad; a 2.5 mm slot sieve removes about 40 per cent; brine flotation is out for lack of a dryer; a cooperative's optical sorter at 40 euro a tonne leaves about 0.06 g/kg for a tenth of the crop; no safe dose is invented.
- *(Done: The Fifth Day, Campo Santo ng San Roque, Calapan, Oriental Mindoro, 28 Oct to 4 Nov 2048; text only.)* Cemetery vases before All Saints: every-40th-grave sample finds larvae in 9 of 41 wet vases in 60 graves (about 360 of 2,400 graves, 190 to 630); egg to adult 7 to 10 days at 28 C, so the rota empties and scrubs on day five, not seven; eggs survive dry, so scrub; sand or net for the flower days.
- *(Done: The Shadow Rule, Scoil Naomh Eoin, Dungarvan, 13 June 2041; text only.)* Sports day under thin cloud: the shadow-shorter-than-you rule (sun above 45 degrees, about 10:30 to 16:30 at 52 N in mid-June), cloud does not stop UV, and a label SPF assumes 2 mg/cm2 that nobody applies; shade first.
- *(Done: The Cold Meter, Eilean Dhubhaig, 2052.)* A wind lull on an island grid; the hall as the cheapest heater.
- *(Done: The Dark Week, Fjellbu Vault, 3026.)* *A seed vault at 1,000 years:* a keeper, a ledger and a handover that must work
  without electronics.
- *(Done: The Return Gate, Cordillera Blanca, 2057.)* Glacier peak water, canal lining and a downstream spring.
- *(Done: The Warm Pew, Kilnagross, 2056.)* An organ that goes sharp as a church warms; heating the people, not the stone.
- *(Done: Forty to One, Tebwaiaki, Kiribati, 2054.)* A drought lens, a feast, and a sensor two centimetres out (forty to one).
- *(Done: The Dry Bread, Mpale, Malawi, 2043.)* A hospital oxygen plant loses purity in the wet season; the fix is in the convent bread oven.
- *(Done: The Weight of a Hive, Tobar Gobnait, 2049.)* Two instruments disagree about a winter hive; a rate is trustworthy when a total is not.
- *(Done: The Black Strip, Casal da Eira, Portugal, 2051.)* A prescribed burn, a wind that turns early, and goats for the rest.
- *(Done: The Late Field, San Roque de Dalig, Philippines, 2048; text only.)* Rice methane credits, a late field in flower, and a contract clause nobody read.
- *(Done: The Late Ebb, Chiloé, 2050; text only.)* A tide table that knows the Moon but not the rain, and a ferry slowed to a speed the water allows.
- *(Done: The Iron Bridge at Forty-Two, Valdecea, León, 2058; text only.)* A footbridge whose note drops under a procession's weight, and a Virgin slowed to a pace the iron allows.
- *(Done: Fifteen Minutes, Bolchara, Bangladesh, 2047; text only.)* Deep irrigation wells that protect the rice slowly draw arsenic toward the village's green-painted drinking well; the village drinks in a day what one pump lifts in fifteen minutes.
- *(Done: The Low Meadow, Sint-Jansland, North Brabant, 2061; text only.)* A water board's polder manager must flood a farmer's meadow for the town; she says no, then names the terms (decide at the last hour, tell her first, ring the bell).
- *(Done: The False Start, Gidan Gawo, Niger, 2044; text only.)* A first rain, one sowing of seed in the village bank, and a field adviser that says four years in ten the first rain is a lie; the seed-keeper splits the sowing and the village shares what is left.
- *(Done: The Line Through the Snow, Ulaan Ovoo, Mongolia, 2049; text only.)* Index insurance, a trigger inside the noise, and a sample drawn fair with sheep's ankle bones (von Neumann's trick); the honest count lets a district move its reserve to one valley.
- *(Done: Bench Six, Kettle Bench, Okanagan, 2047; text only.)* Two wind machines, one battery 58 kWh short of a frost night, a written rule that serves the top bench first, and the chairman who gives up his place after counting what his thinning crew would have knocked off anyway.
- *(Done: Chalk for the Lintel, Sant'Anselmo, Aosta Valley, 2053; text only.)* A storm, a sick cook, a rescue team that wants to climb, and an avalanche agent that says no with a number it admits is a guess.
- *(Done: Ten Percent, Calder Ford, Iowa, 2046.)* The twenty-year strand's first human-reader piece: a class scores its own time-capsule forecast with the Brier score.
- *(Done: The Second Door, Pine Ferry, Wisconsin, 2046; text only.)* A county building office reads one permit in five by dice; a winter shelter filed under the wrong word lands in the unread four-fifths, and the second exit is a chained gate.
- *(Done: The Third Year, Kettle County, Minnesota, 2046; text only.)* A three-parish priest, a diocesan drafting desk that saves him hours and makes his opening match a neighbour's, and the week a teenager dies on black ice; the desk does the reading, the priest does the walking and the homily.
- *(Done: The Salt Jar, Mtendere Granary, Zambia, 2049; text only.)* A leaking ridge cap, a probe that sees one cubic foot, a jam jar of salt, the rule of three on bags nobody opened, and a widow's twelve bags that fail a toxin strip; the co-operative's bad-year fund, not the widow, pays.
- *(Done: The Fifth Asking, Duluth, 2046; text only.)* A 2046 hospital chaplaincy where the assistant's notes about a person outlast the person's wishes. Work out the numbers first.
- *(Done: Not Asked Since, Balmuir Community Hospital, Angus, 2046; text only.)* A ward assistant's three-year-old "no priests" banner, a stroke, and a dying man asked with control questions; the refusal was about one dead priest.
- *(Done: While I Live, Carrowbeg, Co. Mayo, 2046; text only.)* A credit union's assistant holds a member's 2041 letter whose sum has turned false (savings for two years; now thirty-nine weeks at €1,045 short a week). It will not write the letter she would have written; it asks her, on her best morning, and records the answer as one morning's answer.
- *(Done: Till She Is Through, Tràigh Ghil, Barra, 2046; text only.)* A bank assistant holds a 2043 standing order (£300 a month "till Seonaidh is through with her schooling") the writer can no longer revise; it will not decide what "schooling" meant, sets out the words, the dates and the sums (£4,184 a year care gap), and leaves the choice to the guardian.
- *(Done: Eleven Steps, Culasi, Leyte Gulf, 2052; text only.)* A typhoon surge ensemble puts water on the floor of a home for 38 frail old people in 46 of 50 runs; the Mother Superior refuses a repeat of the 2049 bus evacuation, and the town's agent counts both options without choosing. They carry everyone upstairs.
- *(Done: The Day He Is Married, Carrowmore, Co. Clare, 2046 to 2047; text only.)* A 2031 will pays €45,000 "on the day he is married in the Church" or to the St Vincent de Paul conference within ten years (14 Feb 2047, 129 days away); a tribunal case has about a 1 in 5 chance (6 of 31 files) of deciding by 20 Dec; the assistant holds the testator's recorded reasons but will not use them to decide, and the only parties who can move the date are the conference that loses by it and the court.
- *(Done: Three Pouches, Kyabahinga, Kabale, 2047; text only.)* Chlorine demand follows turbidity; one fixed dose cannot serve both seasons; three pouches by a turbidity tube, and a blind taste test sets the residual.
- *(Done: Sixty-One Point Three, Kiriani, Nyeri, 2049; text only.)* A pasteuriser probe that reads 2.5 °C high is found with a pan of boiling water at altitude; the milk is called back to the boil before the lab replies.
- *(Done: The Float Test, Żytnia Wola, Podlasie, 2048; text only.)* Ergot in a rye lot: 3 bodies in 3 kg from trailer tops (0.008%) against 35 in 10 kg by brine from one field (clumped, 0.028%); the lot is not blended under the limit, the field goes to an optical sorter, and the festival loaf is floated four times.
- *(Done: The Lid Stays Down, St Brendan's pantry, Otago coast, 2047; text only.)* Freezer time constants (C/UA: 170 h full, 35 h old and half-empty), a rule written before the number, and a pantry that cooks what it cannot safely hand out.
- *(Done: The Window Closes at Eleven, Killavoggy, Co. Leitrim, 2048; text only.)* Measles in a school at 87% immunity against a 93% threshold: final-size range (0 to 16 more cases, 12 most likely), a 72-hour vaccine window, a six-day immunoglobulin window, exclusion days 5 to 21, and a refusal to hand over names.
- *(Done: Boil It Once, Nagyvölgy, Tolna County, Hungary, 2047; text only.)* A boil-water notice (five minutes) on a well with 58 mg/L nitrate: boiling concentrates it (11% lost in 5 min, about 65; 42% in 20 min, about 100); the nurse refuses "everyone buys bottles", the final notice boils once and sends infant water to nine babies.
- *(Done: The Warm Stack, Tŷ Uchaf, Powys, 2048; text only.)* Hay baled at 24% moisture heats to 66 C in a nine-day-old stack (1.3 C/h); one per cent of dry matter respired is about 57 C if retained; the advisor gives the numbers, the farmer chooses to ring the fire service, and a hundred and forty bales are lost.
- *(Done: The Slowest Chair on the Hill, Madonna della Neve del Sasso, Val Cannobina, 5 August 2048; text only.)* Pilgrimage lightning rule: leave when minutes to the 10 km circle = slowest group's descent + margin; chair 2.0 km/h (63 min) not main body 2.8 (45); fixes of 75 km at 36 km/h (108 min) then 41 km at 45 km/h (41 min); a 35 s flash-to-bang is 11.9 km, 6.5 km ahead of forecast; margin 10 min raised to 20.
- *(Done: Two Wrong Numbers, Aidan's Holm, Northumbria, Good Friday 2049; text only.)* A tidal causeway's published closing time (14:42) rests on a 1989 survey 22 cm too high and a gauge 11 minutes ahead of the road; the errors cancel to a minute on a spring tide (not on a neap) while a 0.2 to 0.7 m surge moves closing by 36 minutes; the rule is worst-case closing less the slowest walk (43 min) less 45.
- *(Done: The Noon That Moves, Santa Eufemia de Cabo Brañal, Galicia, 2 Nov to 21 Dec 2051; text only.)* Permanent UTC+2 at 9.26 W puts true noon at 14:21 to 14:52 by the clock (equation of time +16 to -14 min); the assistant's flat 14:36 is wrong by 15 min on 3 Nov and the sundial is right; the Angelus is a clock-bell, Sext a sun-prayer, a table on the cloister door serves both; sunrise 10:05 on 21 Dec leaves the bus 105 min early, so the school starts an hour later for six weeks (45 min still dark).
- *(Done: The Fifty-One Percent, Sveti Jernej v Podgorju, Gorenjska, 22 Mar to 4 Apr 2051; text only.)* A Paschal candle sold as pure beeswax reads 0.931 g/cm³ (beeswax 0.95 to 0.97, paraffin 0.88 to 0.92): not pure, but 53 percent has a range of 36 to 70 and the diocesan rule is a majority; a rule decided before the lab, a beekeeper's own wax for the baptismal candles, lab 58 percent after Easter.
- *(Done: The Kings Cross the Lake, Wilcza Wola, Warmia-Masuria, 2 to 6 Jan 2049; text only.)* An Epiphany procession onto a frozen lake: growth 72 degree-days gives 14 to 20 cm; drilled 15 cm at the lane but 9 clear + 6 white snow-ice counts as 12 (3.5 h² = 504 kg), so a person is 6x under and a horse and rider (580 kg) is 0.87; 9 cm at a stream mouth, 6 over a spring the old farmer draws; 80 on the ice at a time in six waves; horses stay ashore.
- *(Done: The River Is Eight Degrees, Sainte-Colombe-des-Gués, French Jura, Holy Saturday 2047; text only.)* A river baptism at 8 °C: the 1-10-1 cold-water clocks, a 71-year-old with atrial fibrillation (foot test 78 to 118 bpm), wading product depth x speed 0.54 at the bar against 0.19 at the landing, priest's 12 minutes in the water cut to 8 by swapping with the deacon; pouring is baptism entire, and she chooses knee-deep and the shell.
- *(Done: The Pitch of the Cold Church, St Ulrich's, Hinterkar, Tyrol, 2047; text only.)* Flue pipes go flat about 3 cents a degree and reeds do not: 40 cents apart at 5 C, 14 cents closer as 260 people warm the case; reeds and strings set to the midpoint keep everyone within 7 cents; the organist keeps the trumpet.
- *(Done: The Landing Every Thirty Inches, St Casimir's, Pulaski Mills, Wisconsin, 2048; text only.)* Ramp for a 0.62 m rise, 92 kg chair and rider, rolling coefficient 0.02 (0.06 gravel): 1:12 needs 93 N and 2.1 m/s runaway per 3.72 m leg; 1:7.3 needs 141 N and 3.2 m/s; work 694 vs 641 J, so peak force, not energy, sets the ramp. Standard cited is the US 1:12 / 760 mm / 1.5 m landing rule.
- *(Done: The Dry Ice in the Closed Room, St Lucy's pantry, Corazón, California, 2051; text only.)* Four full chest freezers at 32 C hold about 17 hours (13.5 to 22.5), not the household 48; 48 kg a day of dry ice is 27 m3 of CO2, about 7 percent in a closed 54 m3 storeroom (0.3 ach) and under 0.5 percent at an open dock (5 ach).
- *(Done: The Condition at the Farm, Calder Ford, Iowa, 9 to 12 March 2046; text only; trust-modification piece, $300,000 at an assumed 5% less $14,400 a year from 2031 = about $485,000, illustrative.)* Original proposal: a 2046 court or bank piece where a stored preference binds a person who can no longer revise it, with no dementia in it (for example a trust or a will's condition).
- *(Done: The Coal Charity, Wickham Sedge, Cambridgeshire, 2046; text only.)* A 1853 gift of a ton of coal to every poor widow, no coal left to buy, and a clerk that prices a ton of warmth in heat-pump electricity (about 635 kWh, £178) and says which numbers it guessed; the dead donor's intent kept by a Martinmas morning and a Mass. The court-or-bank proposal above is still open for a living person.
- *(Done: The Youngest Ring, Hochbrunn, Styria, 2048; text only.)* Tree-ring dating of a village's founding-legend Madonna: the youngest ring is the floor on the date, the church's 800 years stay, the statue's do not.
- *(Done: The Third List, Hartsmouth, Ohio, 2048; text only.)* Capture–recapture on three agencies' lists with coded matching and no names: 112, 87 and 64 people, 190 seen, about 310 estimated (253 to 397 across models); a cold-night door tally gives 307. A fish count answers "how many pass through", not "how many cots tonight".
- *(Done: The Barbara Level, Cwm Barri, South Wales, 2050; text only.)* Mine-water heat for 380 homes (40 L/s, 8 K, COP 4.5, 1.34 MW from the ground); the agent will not certify 25 years on a 3 to 41 year thermal-breakthrough range, a fluorescein test arrives at hour 52, and a surveyor's 1934 note says a roadway the plan calls stowed is open.
- *(Done: The Lean of the Cloister, Santa Scolastica al Poggio, Parma Apennines, 2053; text only.)* A slow earthflow speeds up (5 to 18.5 mm/day); inverse-velocity gives 4.3 days but only a 2 to 9 day window; the guest wing is 0.3% of the moving mass, the capped 1964 spring and a trench drain are the lever.
- *(Done: The Borrowed Lane, St Agnes Girls' Secondary School, Gulu, Uganda, 2049; text only.)* A school cubesat, an old rocket stage passing at 200 m (1 in 9,000, capped at 1 in 4,300 by dilution), fuel that buys fewer kilometres the longer the school waits, and a burn rule agreed before the next radar number arrives.
- *(Done: The Dead Leg, Casa San Rocco, Via Francigena, Tuscany, 2050; text only.)* A pilgrim hostel's 48 °C tank and a fourteen-metre unused pipe in a reopened wing; PCR versus culture, a decision rule written before the result, and a wing shut for ten days.
- *(Done: The Hum at Half Past Eight, Syväkoski, Finnish Lapland, 2049; text only.)* A solar storm on All Saints' Saturday, a 1974 transformer with acetylene rising in its oil, and a keeper who opens the breaker on 24 minutes' warning; the neighbour's meter later says the answer was close, not clear.
- *(Done: The Bell Will Not Carry, Saint-Théodule-d'en-Haut and Les Aunes, Valais, 2051; text only.)* A glacier-lake flood alarm that is the chapel bell: 104 dB at 10 m gives 54.6 dB outdoors and 29.6 indoors at 1.9 km (waking range about 450 m at a 45 dB planning figure); front arrives in 13.3 to 21.3 minutes (8 to 5 m/s over 6.4 km); noon test passes, 2 a.m. test (1 of 9 woke) fails, bedside tone boxes (58 at €40) wake 9 of 9; 2 + 2 + 3 + 4 = 11 minutes to the Tour against 13.3.
- *(Done: The Folded Paper, Arokya Nagar, Tamil Nadu, 2048; text only.)* Otitis media, a flooded causeway and a delayed prescription: the numbers told plainly (one child in sixteen to twenty helped sooner, one in fourteen harmed, mastoiditis prevented only across thousands of courses) and a mother whose 2044 loss was a pneumonia no one listened to.
- *(Done: The Quiet Crypt, St Aldhelm's, Trevenna, Cornwall, 2055; text only.)* A crypt shelter, a 420 Bq/m³ radon screen, time-weighted exposure (4 nights vs 90), ventilation 0.2 to 1 air change per hour (420 to 86), and a fan heater that raises the reading by the stack effect.
- *(Done: The Four Minutes, Inis Ghainimh, Connemara, 2047; text only.)* A defibrillator drone against three cabinets and a first-aid course on an island of 118; the honest numbers are small (about a fifth of a life in 20 years), a drill in a 16 m/s gale shows what the island does when the drone says no, and the island buys what the numbers and the sexton's notebook point to.
- *(Done: The Thirty-Minute Cycle, Rakchen, Humla, 2050; text only.)* A health-post steriliser run at the sea-level dial setting for 22 months; a first spore test comes back alive, the fix is minutes not pressure (the safety valve stays sealed), and a caesarean is released on the probe log before the strip can be read.
- *(Done: The Last Barge, Nelkuk, Alaska, 2047 to 2048; text only.)* A cemetery bluff retreating 1.1 to 6 m a year; a widow's 64 years of pace counts (71 cm a pace) extend ten years of drone data, a bootstrap gives the front row a median 7 years (3 to 10) and the honest caveat is that ten years is too short; the village books the one annual barge and adopts a trigger rule, a 12 m edge distance.
- *(Done: The Eighteenth Place, Marlow Falls, Pennsylvania, 2048; text only.)* A lead-in-water test passes on the 90th percentile (8.6 µg/L, the 18th of 20) with two samples at 31 and 44; an exact binomial interval says 23 to 600 of 1,900 lead-line homes, profile sampling finds the 44 is a 1991 brass faucet, and the council adopts a three-highest reporting rule, a 60-home retest and children-first pipe order.
- *(Done: The Clear Reading, Pazhayakadu, Idukki, Kerala, 2047; text only.)* A parish eye camp's reading assistant (sensitivity 90, specificity 85, made on a 30%-prevalence set) flags 135 of 600 where the nuns expected 60; at 10% prevalence a positive is right 40 times in 100, not 72. A dilated second look confirms 55, 36 laser places go to 56 people, and a nurse's one question finds a cobweb the machine cleared.
- *(Done: Three Plain and a Bob, Thurlby Staithe, Norfolk, 2052; text only.)* Plain Bob Doubles on five bells: a tower captain's card reads "bob every third lead" and comes round at row 69 with 80 distinct rows of 120; the true touch is three plain leads and a bob (4 of 4,096 call patterns). The assistant gives the band a map of lead heads, is told not to call, and an eleven-year-old rings the treble at the funeral, late for three rows and true in all 120.
- *(Done: The Latest Easter, Vaarsay, Orkney, 2038; text only.)* Easter on 25 April, the latest it can be; at 59° N the sun is 6° down only at 21:38. A sacristan's 41-year notebook, a candle that gives one lux at a metre and a written five-lux rule decide when "after nightfall" begins; the cost is the nine from Holm who cannot stay, and a fire carried across by boat at dawn.
- *(Done: The Neap Week, Enez Glas, Brittany, 2049; text only.)* A tide mill's energy goes as head squared: 245 kWh on a spring day, 49 on a neap day; bread or the cold store, and the answer is a walled-up wood oven.
- *(Done: The Grey Hour, Mokre Stawy, Lower Silesia, 2047; text only.)* A carp pond's night oxygen budget (6.0 mg/L at dusk, 0.55 mg/L/h respiration, 1.3 by dawn); an aerator's transfer falls with the oxygen already in the water, so 3 kWh is spent in the last hours, after a hand titration shows the probe reads 0.5 high.
- *(Done: The Salt Line, Santa Marina del Pozzo, Salento, 2050; text only.)* A cistern leak found from no-use night drops at three depths (2.8, 2.0, 1.3 mm per 6 h on 30 m²) that reach zero at 1.405 m ±7 cm; the water above the crack is drunk first, a lime-and-tile plaster shuts it, and the yearly loss is a range (40 to 70 m³ of 287).
- *(Done: The Second Slope, Santa Marta de la Fuente Honda, Los Pedroches, 2049; text only.)* A 72-hour pumping test in granite: Cooper–Jacob T = 4 m²/day, the slope doubles at 1.5 days (a no-flow boundary 58 m away, 34 to 106 m by storativity guess), a neighbour's well 380 m off drops 3.3 m now and 5.4 m with a 40-bed guesthouse; the house takes 20 m³/day with a public 5 m trigger on his gauge.
- *(Done: The Rope at the Side Gate, Chilcapamba, Imbabura, Ecuador, 2052; text only.)* A shrine forecourt of 2,200 m² (6,600 at 3 per m², 11,000 at 5) and one 3.2 m gate (26 to 34 min to empty 6,600); the turnstile count misses the uncounted side gate by about 600, so the gate captain ties a rope across his own family's path and the Dolorosa is carried down to those left outside.
- *(Done: Enough to Be Bread, Prieuré Sainte-Scholastique, Eastern Townships, Quebec, 2047; text only.)* A wheat-starch lot that tests under 5 mg/kg of gluten is too pure to be valid host matter; a gram of flour per kilogram, put back by geometric dilution (first stir: one host at 310 in twelve; sieved: mean 100, range 62 to 148), and a coeliac boy's first communion.
- *(Done: The Gap in the Nets, Santa Rosa de las Lomas, southern Peru, 2049; text only.)* Fog nets for 46 people at 15 L/day (252 m³ a year, 25 tankers): ridge 5.2 vs village 2.3 L/m²/day over 96 days, 240 m² at 0.7 gives 131 m³; nets catch about one drop in ten of the fog through their face, so a 40 m gap (192 m², 105 m³, 15 tankers) protects a goatherd's tara grove, with an 80% downwind-collector rule written before the data (first month: 86%).
- *(Done: The Lake Comes Back at Noon, Halloran's Landing (invented), Lake Erie south of Buffalo, 16 to 17 Nov 2049; text only.)* A gale's set-up drains away and the village rule says go home, but the lake is a seiche with a 14-hour period and a second front is due on the return swing; the fix is to send people home in the trough, not keep them out.
- *(Done: The Lines That Did Not Fit, Saint Kieran's hall car park (invented), near Birr, Co. Offaly, Sat 4 Oct 2053; text only.)* Car-park layout on a 30 x 14 m yard, 2.5 x 5 m bays: right angles both sides need 16 m (24 bays, 2 m short); 45-degree bays with a 3.6 m lane need 5.30 + 3.6 + 5.30 = 14.21 m (21 cm short) and each row loses a 3.54 m end triangle, so 7 a side = 14; one right-angled row + 5 parallel bays (6 x 2.4 m) needs 13.4 m = 17; bay and lane sizes illustrative, not a design standard (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Minibus That Is Twenty-Eight Short, Saint Colman's hall (invented), near Kenmare, Co. Kerry, Sat 14 Feb 2054; text only.)* Electric minibus, 2 x 148 = 296 km at an invented 0.28 kWh/km = 82.88 kWh against 75 usable (268 km range, 28 km short; 45% left at halfway); 15% reserve means adding 19.13 kWh; 7 kW charger at 90% takes 3 h 02 min, so 2 h leaves 4.72 kWh (6.3%); 11 kW takes 1 h 56 min, 2 h leaves 11.92 kWh (15.9%); battery, consumption, efficiency and chargers invented, real range varies, test the bus (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: Thirty-Seven Bags for Twelve Posts, Saint Conan's Close (invented), near Ramsey, Isle of Man, Sat 14 Mar 2048; text only.)* Fence-post concrete: hole 0.3 m across x 0.6 m deep = 0.0424 m3 less 0.006 m3 post = 36.4 L each, x12 = 437 L = 36.4 bags at 12 L per 25 kg bag (from memory), so 37; a 0.4 m hole is 69.4 L each, 833 L, 69.4 bags (area goes with width squared, 1.78x); burial third and bag yield quoted from memory (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Lid That Held, Sogn Murezi, Surselva, Graubünden, October 2048; text only.)* 140 pints of green beans (pH 5.5 to 6) in a three-hour boiling-water bath at 1,820 m: water boils at 93.8 C (82 kPa), lethality 0.0019 of 121 C, so 0.34 of the 2.4 minutes a 12D process needs (about 21 h at the jar's middle); a pressure canner needs 123 kPa gauge here (103 at the sea); the jars are boiled 10 minutes into soup and not given away.
- *(Done: The Water That Boils Down, Svatá Kunhuta, Haná, Moravia, April 2043; text only.)* Well nitrate strip reads 68 mg/L against the 50 limit for infants; boiling a fifth away makes 85; a 3.8 kg formula-fed baby (0.57 L/day) takes 39 mg a day from the well, 48 from boiled well water, 28 at the limit, 3 from shop water. Bottled infant water now, lab sample, reverse osmosis or a deeper well later.
- *(Done: The Roof Fund That Fell Behind, Saint Hild's hall (invented), near Whitby, North Yorkshire, Sat 15 Feb 2048; text only.)* Sinking fund: 5,000 + 3,000 a year at 3% for 8 years = 33,011 vs a 40,000 roof rising 4% a year = 54,743 (gap 21,732; about 5,444 a year needed; no catch-up by year 19); at 2% roof growth 46,949, gap 13,938; 3% and 4% assumed, quoted from memory (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Soup for Ninety, Saint Ita's hall (invented), near Listowel, Co. Kerry, Sat 12 Nov 2050; text only.)* Scaling a recipe: 12 to 90 portions is x7.5, 4.2 to 31.5 L in a 40 L pot; heat 31.5 kg x 4 kJ/kg/K x 75 K = 9,450 kJ (2.6 kWh), 75 min at 3 kW x 70%, 112 min at 2 kW; salt started at 70% (cooks' habit, quoted from memory); cooling: pot 33 cm deep vs tray ~5 cm, diffusion time ~ depth squared (about 40x, upper bound for still liquid), 4 trays of ~8 L; 60 to 20 C window quoted from memory, council guidance governs (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Six Percent That Was Eleven, Saint Mochta's hall (invented), near Dundalk, Co. Louth, Sat 2 Apr 2050; text only.)* Minibus loan 18,000 over 60 months: 6% a year on the balance (r = 0.005) pays 347.99 a month, 20,879.43 total, 2,879 interest; flat 6% on the original sum is 5,400 interest, 390 a month, which is ~0.904% a month, 10.85% a year (11.4% effective); year-one interest 993.43, balance 14,817.54 after 12 months and 11,438.80 after 24; bank at 7.5% pays 360.68, ~21,641; APR and early-repayment rules quoted from memory, vary by country (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Roof That Pays Back in Eight, Saint Cadoc's hall (invented), near Brecon, Powys, Sat 21 Oct 2051; text only.)* Hall PV: 12 x 450 W = 5.4 kWp x 880 kWh/kWp (from memory) = 4,752 kWh; all used at 30p is 1,425.60/yr, 5.47 years on 7,800; 40% used + 60% exported at 15p is 997.92/yr, 7.82 years (30% used: 8.42); 0.5%/yr output loss puts it 45 ahead at year 8; 900 inverter in year 12 still leaves 2,951 ahead; battery, roof, grid and diocese rules not modelled (tariffs, yield and shares assumed).
- *(Done: The Lap That Was Not a Quarter-Mile, Saint Wilfrid's playing field (invented), near Ripon, North Yorkshire, Sat 14 Jun 2053; text only.)* Sponsored walk units: a wheeled 380 m lap is 0.2361 mile (4.235 laps/mile), not the notice's quarter-mile (402.3 m); 23 laps is 5.431 mi not 5.75 (64p at £2/mile); 20 laps x 30 walkers at an assumed £3/mile is about £25 over; 21 laps is 4.96 mi (misses the 5th mile), 22 is 5.19; pro rata beats whole miles; pence per lap card (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Gallon on the Sticker, Saint Guthlac's parish hall (invented), near Penrith, Cumbria, Fri 5 Sep 2053; text only.)* Fuel budget units: 24 mpg = 38.62 km per gallon; US gallon 9.80 L/100 km, 372 mi (598.7 km) = 58.7 L; imperial 11.77 L/100 km = 70.5 L (11.8 more); 299.3 km one way = 35.2 L, 38.7 with 10% margin; 70 L tank, two fills (all vehicle and price figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Discount That Was Not Thirty-Five, Saint Cybi's parish hall (invented), near Holyhead, Anglesey, Sat 21 Feb 2054; text only.)* Stacked discounts: £1,200 list x 0.85 x 0.80 = £816 (32% off, not 35% = £780); VAT 20% gives £979.20 vs £936, £29.20 over a £950 pledge; dropping the £60 hour: £1,140 x 0.68 = £775.20, £930.24 incl. VAT, £23.26 for 40; +25% then -20% = 1 (all prices illustrative).
- *(Done: The Three Blank Pages, Saint Petroc's parish hall (invented), near Padstow, Cornwall, Sat 14 Mar 2054; text only.)* Booklet imposition: 37 pages / 4 = 9.25 so 10 sheets, 40 pages, 3 blank (38 to 40); sheet k of 10 is front (42-2k | 2k-1), back (2k | 41-2k), outer 40|1, 2|39, centre 22|19, 20|21; 12-page paper dummy 12|1, 2|11, 10|3, 4|9, 8|5, 6|7; 120 x 10 = 1,200 sheets = 2.4 reams (1,080 and 2.16 at 36 pages, three reams either way); A4 0.0624 m2 = 5.0 g at 80 gsm, 59.9 g a booklet with a 160 gsm cover, 7.2 kg for 120; creep of about a millimetre is a guess (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Forty-Five Bags, Saint Kea's wayside cross (invented), near Truro, Cornwall, Sat 18 May 2052; text only.)* Concrete for a cross base: hole 0.8 x 0.8 x 0.7 = 0.448 m3 less a 0.1 m post 0.5 m deep (0.005) = 0.443 m3; at an invented label yield of 0.011 m3 per 25 kg bag that is 40.3, so 41, +10% over-dig and waste = 44.3, order 45 (1,125 kg, 20 bags would fill 34 cm of 70); water at 2.5 L a bag = 112.5 L; 3 bags a barrow = 15 loads vs a 6-bag drum, 8 batches, ~80 min at a guessed 10 min; yield, water and curing quoted from the bag or not given (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: Six Hundred Grams of Flour, Cabeus Rim Station (invented), lunar south pole, Sun 12 Dec 2094; text only.)* 604 g of altar-bread flour left: 7.86 g/day at full Mass = 77 days, 4 g/day with weekday communion for the priest only = 151 days, 220-day horizon needs 880 g; wheat under lamps at 600 umol/m2/s x 18 h = 38.9 mol/day = 3.6 kWh/m2/day at 3 umol/J, so 3 m2 = 450 W (1.9% of 24 kW) for 88 days; harvest 1,310 g grain against 1,500 g planned; Fr Obinna Eze, Marit Solheim, Dolores Ibarra, greenhouse AI Furrow.
- *(Done: The Flame That Is a Ball, Chapel of Saint Florian, Wren Station, low Earth orbit (invented), Holy Saturday 7 Apr 2091; text only.)* Easter Vigil candles in free fall: no buoyancy, so a flame is a small blue ball in its own exhaust and goes out in still air (58 s in the invented lab test; "a minute or two" from memory), but burns lying along a slow draught; 14 tapers at 6 g/h plus a Paschal candle at 10 g/h for 45 min = 70.5 g wax (assumed rates), paraffin C25H52 needs 3.45 g O2 and gives 3.125 g CO2 and 1.33 g H2O per g: 243 g O2, 220 g CO2, 94 g H2O; crew CO2 about 1 kg a day each = 437 g in 45 min, so candles add 50%; 60 m3 sealed = +2,000 ppm on 3,980; heat 46 MJ/kg = 1.2 kW vs 1.4 kW of people (paschal alone 128 W, 23 g CO2); smoke detector shutdown set to alert for 45 min with the commander at the switch and the extinguisher; congregation holds LED tapers, bishop's office leaves fire to the commander and the rest to the priest (invented reply), rubrics left to a bishop; Fr Tomasz Wieczorek, Cdr Ines Valverde, Hao Lin, Seo-yeon Park, station AI Halyard (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Quarter Percent a Sol, Chapel of Saint Clare, Hesperia Outpost, Mars (invented), Sat 7 Oct 2152; text only.)* Dust on a 40 m2 array at 0.28%/sol (Pathfinder, from memory): clean 13.5 kWh/sol (3 kW peak, invented), compounding 0.9972^n gives 92% at 30 sols, 75.6% = 10.2 kWh at sol 100, and the 9 kWh annex load is crossed at sol 144.6 (about 22 Nov); brushing (2 people x 5 h at 4 m2/h each, 97% recovery guessed, 98% found) gains about 154 kWh over 44 sols, 17 sols of load; the 5 W sanctuary lamp is 0.123 kWh/sol = 1.4% of load, switching it off moves the crossing under a day; waiting for a dust devil is a bet with no honest probability; Sr Agnes Okafor, assistant Plumb (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Turn at Half Past Seven, Chapel of Saint Raphael, transport Azarias (invented), Earth to Mars, Holy Week 3 to 12 Apr 2191; text only.)* Constant 0.1 g push, flip at the midpoint: t = 2 sqrt(d/a) = 782,062 s = 9 d 1 h 14 min over 1 AU (round figure), flip at 4 d 12 h 37 min, peak v = at/2 = 384 km/s; departure Sun 07:00 puts the 20-min weightless turn at Thu 19:37 (the washing of the feet), a 30 cm pour falls in 0.78 s against 0.25 s at 1 g; leaving 3 h early moves the turn to 16:37 to 16:57 (3 h late = 22:37, the Watch) and arrival to Tue 05:14; engine, 12 h window, 20 min turn and people invented, flame shape at low g from memory (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Litany That Answers Late, Chapel of Saint Gregory, Arsia Station (invented), Mars, Tue 16 May 2158; text only.)* 1.5 AU = 748.5 s one way (499 s per AU), 1,497 s round trip; a 40-invocation litany at 20 s (800 s) as strict call and response = 16.6 h, so it is sung as a delayed canon; closing prayer said by both houses at one clock moment, unheard by either; Sister Imelda Okoro, Perpetua Wanjiru, Ruben Castellanos, assistant Lauda, convent of Saint Cecilia, Leuven (invented).
- *(Done: The Water That Stays in the Air, Chapel of Saint Frideswide, Larkspur Hold, Enceladus (invented), Easter Sunday 25 Mar 2187; text only.)* Holy-water sprinkling at g = 0.113 m/s2 (1.15% of Earth, from memory): a 2 m/s flick at 45 degrees has range v2/g = 35.4 m (Earth 0.41 m, 87x), peak 8.85 m, flight 25.0 s, hits a 4 m dome ceiling at 3.2 s about 6.5 m out; 5 m to the back pew needs v = sqrt(5 x 0.113) = 0.75 m/s (Earth 6 cm), peak 1.25 m, 9.4 s; level at 1.5 m: fall 5.15 s, 3.9 m sideways; no air drag, so upper bounds for mm drops, a real test needed; dome, 12 m x 4 m, people and flick speeds invented; Fr Lucian Okoro, sacristan Dorota Wisniewska, Mateo and Ana (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Week That Is a Day, Chapel of Our Lady of the Rosary, Anvil Terrace, Ganymede (invented), Sun 7 Oct 2210; text only.)* Day = 1/(1/7.1546 - 1/4332.6) = 7.1664 d = 171.99 h, 3.99 h longer than a 168 h week, so sunrise slips 4 h a week and realigns after 42.1 weeks (about 294 d); night about 86 h; Jupiter 7.64 degrees (14.5 Moons, 209x area); Jupiter eclipse up to about 3.7 h an orbit; orbit data from memory, place, people and sunrise times invented.
- *(Done: The Wind That Is Not Air, Chapel of Saint Cecilia, Lantern Landing, Titan (invented), Sun 22 Nov 2189; text only.)* Open pipe f = c/2L, c = sqrt(gamma R T / M): air 344 m/s at 295 K, N2 at 94 K 198 m/s (ratio 0.574, 9.55 semitones flat), warmed Titan gas (95% N2, 5% CH4, M 27.41) 354 m/s, 2.8% faster, 47.6 cents sharp; 8-foot C pipe 2.63 m; methane lower flammable limit about 5% (from memory); place, people and tank figures invented.
- *(Done: The Sun That Stops at the Edge, Chapel of the Holy Name, Gabaon Cut Station, Mercury's equator (invented), Sun 4 Jan 2167; text only.)* Mercury solar day 1/(1/58.646 - 1/87.969) = 175.94 d; at eccentricity 0.2056 the orbital rate at perihelion is 1.55x the mean, 6.35 deg/d against the spin's 6.14, so the Sun runs backwards for 8.1 d (4.05 either side), 1.11 deg in all, at most 0.208 deg/d; Sun 1.73 deg wide at 0.3075 AU, so a station 0.55 deg (23 km) past the horizon longitude sees the limb at -7.9 d, a half-disc standstill at -4.05, the last limb gone at +1.6 and back at +6.1 (equator, no tilt or refraction); at a given horizon longitude it is a sunrise loop only every other perihelion pair, by the author's reasoning (not checked); perihelion date invented, orbit data from memory; Joshua 10 left to scripture scholars; station and people invented (all figures illustrative).
- *(Done: The Slow Dial, Saint Hyacinth's (invented), Görlitz, May to August 2702; text only because audio is over the 400 MB cap.)* The 300 to 1,000 year band had two stories; this fills one more. Earth's day lengthens about 1.8 ms a century, so with no leap seconds after 2035 the clock leads the Sun by about 32.9 s x T^2 (24 min 24 s in 2702, about 28 in 2750, 32 in 2800); the equation of time table also drifts (perihelion 4 Jan in 2051, 16 Jan in 2702; 53 s on 20 May). Vertical south dial at 51.15 N: style 38.85 degrees, hour lines atan(cos 51.15 x tan h). The dial is cut true, with a clock table whose future rows are left blank.
- *(Done: The Rising Shore, Sundby and Yttersund (invented), Kvarken coast of Finland, April to July 2612; text only.)* Uplift 8.0 mm/yr in 2000 with a 5,000-year e-folding (7.70 in 2190, 7.08 in 2612); local sea 5 mm/yr to 2100, 7 to 2250, slowing to 3 by 2400 and 2 by 2612, so the shore nearly stood still (0.7 to 0.8 mm/yr net) when the harbour chapel was built in 2190. 2190 to 2612: land +3.12 m, sea +1.70 m, net 1.41 m; a floor at 1.2 m stands at 2.6 m, and at a 1 in 710 slope the shore is 1.0 km away. 5.08 mm/yr net is 3.6 m of shore a year, 1.08 ha a year along 3 km. Next 250 years: land +1.75 m; sea at 1 to 4 mm/yr gives 1.06 to 0.53 km more shore (best estimate about 0.9 km). Plot: the old village's water owners put the not-yet-risen land in trust for the next village down; a numbered-log chapel built to be moved again; the bell goes to the new harbour on Sea Sunday, 12 July 2612.
- *(Done: The Sinking Polder, Oudewetering (invented), Green Heart peat polder, South Holland, April to November 2341; text only.)* Church on piles to the sand since 2105 (one 17 cm step); drained peat subsides 10 mm/yr at a 60 cm ditch depth, 3 mm/yr at 20 cm with pressure drains (5 m spacing, 70 cm deep); 236 years x 10 mm = 2.36 m, floor 2.53 m above the road, fifteen steps (2105 ... 2339, a 2 cm gap in 2341); next step about 38 years away with a ten-year transition (57 at a steady 3 mm). CO2: 1 cm x 1 ha = 100 m3 x 0.15 t/m3 x 0.8 OM x 0.55 C x 0.65 oxidation share = 4.3 t C = 15.7 t CO2/ha/yr (4.7 at 3 mm), 1,500 ha = about 23,600 vs 7,100 t/yr; 2.1 m of peat lasts 210 vs 700 years; cores 3.60 m (2200) and 2.20 m (2340) on the Verhoef land. Failed first attempt: ditch-level-only raise on 38 ha wets the edges of 40 m strips, middle still sinks 8 mm. Plot: peilbesluit vote 17 to 13 on Wed 22 Oct 2341 after the farmer member who said no reads the cores; herd from 110 to 38 plus 16 lighter heifers; stonemason's sixteenth step stone kept, uncut, at Willibrord's feast, Fri 7 Nov 2341.
- *(Done: The Bell Tax, Kruisdonk (invented), East Flanders, 6 Sep to 11 Nov 2231; text only.)* Similar-shape bells: mass ~ pitch^-3 plus 8 kg for heavy trebles, m(n) = 4,600 x 2^(-n/4) + 8 for 49 bells: bourdon 4,608 kg, smallest 9, total 29,298 kg; lowest 12 bells 87%, top 24 bells 566 kg (1.9%); 22% tin gives 6,446 kg in the carillon, 125 kg in the top two octaves (112 at an assumed 90% recovery), 1,014 kg in the bourdon; cracked hour bell Salvator (1681, requisitioned 1943, back from Hamburg after the war, cracked 2087 and 2164) 1,900 kg = 418 kg tin, 376 recoverable, + 31 kg from the recycling yard = 407 against 360 needed (9,000 heating controllers x 40 g, assumed), 47 spare; levy 6%/yr at an invented 160 euros/kg: 65,894 for carillon + bell, 4,013 for the bell; drum 35,040 quarter hours a year proves the carillon in use; world tin about 370 kt/yr (2020s, from memory) so the bell is about 36 seconds of it. Failed first attempts: the top two octaves (a third of the need), the yard, an organ, brass candlesticks. Plot: the burgemeester's no, the carillonneur's fist on the cracked bell, council unbinds the 2164 vow 22 to 5 on Tue 4 Oct, bell leaves Fri 28 Oct to its old hour tune, first frost on Sint-Maarten, Fri 11 Nov.
- *(Done: The Colbert Oaks, Chaumerolles (invented), Allier, January to November 2216; text only.)* *The Colbert Oaks:*  8.0 ha, 840 sessile oaks planted Jan 2084 from Tronçais acorns for a church roof due about 2240; DBH normal(46, 7) cm, basal area 142.8 m2; 1,182 m3 log, 650 m3 sawn at 55%, 100 roofs at 6.5 m3; roof 36 couples, 129 pieces, 162 trees with a quarter spare; tie beam 30 x 30 x 10.6 m needs DBH 61.7 cm (10 trees now), rafter 45.9 cm (425); survey AI Cubage keeps the top 162 and takes 678 (72% BA, about 70 roofs), 20 a hectare; the walk rejects 79 of 300 (41 of the top 162); forester's plan takes 279 (30% BA, 191 m3 sawn, 29 roofs) incl. the ten largest as ridge beams, leaves 561 and 200 future trees; tie-capable by 2240: 115 / 200 / 32 against 12 needed; trait de Jupiter as the fallback; Saint Hubert's Mass with horns, Sun 3 Nov 2216.
- *(Done: Ninety-Nine Years, Thorsby (invented), West Riding, January to March 2130; text only.)* Rent £3,800 a year fixed from Lady Day 2031 for 99 years (38 houses, 2.14 ha, £100 a house); prices x9.6 (2.31%/yr), so worth £396 of 2031; 99 payments £376,200, about £150,700 in 2031 money (40%); shortfall about £225,500 (2031), £2.16m (2130). Founders expected a seventh (1.02^99 = 7.1). Option to renew once on the same terms "save as the parties may otherwise agree"; open market £9,500 (2031) and £2,400 a house, £91,200 (2130), invented. CPI-indexed restart £36,480 (£960 a house, £80 a month, 40% of market) fails 29 to 6: backwards it goes from about £9,470 to £12,400 in the 2071-73 shock (+31% prices, +12% earnings). Corn rent from the 1576 Act (18 Eliz. I c. 6, a third in wheat and malt): wheat on a seven-harvest average £200/t (2031) to £1,216/t (2130), x6.08, so a 19 t rent keeps 63%; 2096 single-year +44% is about +8% on the average; 2071-73 +16%. Agreed 33 to 2: 30 t of milling wheat a year (£36,480, £9,120 a quarter), reviews on Lady Day 2163 and 2196 (Fridays) by up to a fifth with both sides agreeing, a new once-only option for 2229. 24 Mar 2130 is Good Friday, Easter 26 Mar, Annunciation moved to Mon 3 Apr; treasurer Agnieszka Wróbel, records assistant Terrier, co-op chair Dev Mistry, Winifred Asante (b. 2037), Helen Garside (diocesan trustees), Fr Paul Nkemelu; 2031 letter from Kathleen Dunn and Fr Michael Haigh.
- *(Done: The Stave Mill, Saint-Aulaye-sur-Né near Jarnac, February to March 2148.)* *The Stave Mill:* a 2100s story at a cooperage in the Cognac country (real: French oak for barrels is split, not sawn, along the grain, and air-dried in stacks for two years or more) where a cooper's AI finds that its drying schedule was fitted on fine-grained staves from a forest that has since grown faster, and wider-ringed oak (in a ring-porous oak, denser, with more latewood) dries more slowly, so staves pass the calendar while still too wet, shrink in the cask and weep. Check ring width against density and drying rate in the literature, then work out stave yield per cubic metre of log, drying time, and how many casks of one year leak before drafting; no real house or forester as the villain.
- *(Done: As It Was, Thorsby (invented), West Riding, January to Holy Saturday 25 March 2361; text only.)* *The Tithe Barn Ledger:* a 2300s story in which a parish that has kept a corn rent for three centuries (thirty tonnes of wheat a year, reviewed every generation) finds the rent has been read aloud and left unchanged at six reviews running while wheat yields doubled, so the rent has quietly halved in real terms, and the meeting must decide whether a habit of saying "as it was" is consent. Work out three centuries of a slow real-price drift, what each review could have changed (a fifth either way), and how a review clause can fail by courtesy, before drafting; no grasping landlord or tenant as the villain.  Sequel to Ninety-Nine Years: 2130 rent 30 t at £1,216 (£36,480); yields 9.6 to 19.2 t/ha over 231 years (seven 33-year reviews), real price halves, about 9.4% lost per interval (2^(1/7)), so any of the six "as it was" reviews (2163, 2196, 2229, 2262, 2295, 2328) could have caught it with its fifth; now worth £18,240 (2130 money), parish received about 72% (0.5/ln 2) of a constant-value rent; 30 t was the harvest of 3.125 ha (1.46 x Lady Close, 2.14 ha) and is now 1.5625 ha (0.73); the parish asks the fifth, 36 t = 1.875 ha (60% of 2130 value, £576 a house, +£96 a year), and the lease is varied to name the hectares not the tonnes, at seven-harvest average yield and price, with the reading at each review (hectares, value against last review and 2130, each side says aloud); full restoration (60 t) refused by the co-op chair; nothing claimed for the past (qui tacet consentire videtur, and the gloss "where he ought and could speak"); the 2163 and 2196 treasurers left the letter addressed to 2229 sealed; records assistant Terrier (29th of the name) admits every version answered the narrow question; treasurer Hamza Ashworth (retired mill grain tester; his mother wrote the 2328 card), Callum Oduya, Elsie Brannigan (92), Farah Quayle, Fr Benedict Hoang, Ruth Hepworth; the field 150 m x 125 m; vote 36-3-2; Lady Day 2361 is Holy Saturday (Easter 26 March).
- *(Done: The Top Water, Vellakkayal (invented), Kuttanad, Alappuzha, Kerala, Mon 15 to Sun 21 Jan 2052; text only.)* A salt wedge under a fresh surface: water at about 7 g/L is about 0.5% denser, so it slides in along the bed; a buoy at 30 cm reads 0.7 dS/m while a Meyer-style bottle reads 0.7/0.8/1.6/5.9/9.4/11.0 at 0.3/0.8/1.2/1.5/1.8/2.2 m (11 is about a fifth of seawater's 53); a bed-lifting gate drinks the bottom, so the low corner reads 4.8 (middle 2.1, far end 0.9; evaporation would need 85% of the water gone); Maas-Hoffman rice (3 dS/m threshold, 12%/dS/m) gives about a fifth lost for a season at 4.8, told as a tenth to a fifth; stop-logs take the top water: 1.8 x 1.8 m x 0.25^1.5 = 0.4 m³/s, 35,000 m³/day against 9,000 ET (5 mm on 180 ha), 63,000 + 10,000 flush = about three days; joints leak bottom water (2.6, 6.1 at the foot) until a sheet is pressed on by the water; fireworks money is vow money (nercha) and stays the families' to redirect.
- *(Done: The Fog Reads Eighty-Nine, Àird Mhuire (invented), South Uist machair, Mon 19 to Wed 21 Jul 2049; text only.)* Potato late blight and the Hutton Criteria (two consecutive days, min temp >= 10 C and >= 6 h at RH >= 90%); a seven-year-old mast sensor counts no hour at 90% all July and peaks at 89.5 in fog. Two-point calibration: saturated NaCl holds 75.4% at about 18-20 C (Greenspan 1977) and the sensor reads 71.8 (3.6 low); fog taken as 100% gives slope 24.6/17.7 = 1.39, so a raw 82.3 is a true 90 (with fog at 98%: slope 1.28, raw 83.2). Hourly profile designed and recounted in code: runs 11-13 Jul (12-13 at the 98 line) and 18 Jul onward under either line; lesions in 3-5 days, white sporulation by about the 19th, found on the 20th at the low, dew-wet end. Copper is protectant only and accumulates in soil (township declines it on the machair); haulm pulled and buried; blighted seed tubers carry blight over winter, so the late husband's 40-year Golden Wonder line is not replanted (ten clean tubers posted for a lab test). Weather, readings and yields invented.
- *(Done: One Point Eight Metres an Hour, DSV Skua Endeavour (invented), central North Sea, Tue 9 to Mon 15 Dec 2053; text only.)* Saturation decompression from 108 m on an invented company table close in shape to the US Navy's (1.8 m/h to 60 m, 1.5 to 30, 1.2 to 15, 0.9 to the surface; no travel 00:00 to 06:00 or 14:00 to 16:00; start 2 h after the last excursion): 26 h 40 + 20 + 12 h 30 + 16 h 40 = 75 h 50 of travel; start Tue 19:50 surfaces Sun 13:40, before the afternoon stop; 21:00 surfaces 16:50; the real start, 20:30 after a nicked O-ring on the medical lock of the spare chamber fails at about 80 m, reaches 30 cm at 14:00 Sunday and surfaces 16:20. pO2 0.45 bar at 11.8 atm is 3.8% oxygen; helium conducts heat about 6x air, chambers near 30 C; sound about 3x faster in helium (unscrambler). A 57-year-old standby diver presses in (4 h) to keep the team whole. Baptism in danger of death by the hospital chaplain; the name Juan Diego from the saint of 9 December.
- *(Done: The Second Gas, Gjógv á Ytstoy (invented), Faroe Islands, 21 to 22 October 2051; text only. Table 6 with one extension, 310 min; the child joins at 18 m through the entry lock and leaves at 9 m.)* *Proposal: The Second Gas* (2026-11-12): a hyperbaric chamber at a small island hospital (Orkney, the Hebrides or the Faroes) is the only one for 300 km, and two patients need it on the same night: a scallop diver with decompression sickness and a child with carbon monoxide poisoning from a faulty heater. Work out the US Navy Treatment Table 6 timing (about 4 h 45 at 18 m and 9 m with air breaks) against a carbon monoxide protocol, whether both can share the chamber at one pressure, and who the inside attendant is. Check every protocol figure against a primary source before drafting; mark any as from memory.
- *(Done, a second time from the same proposal: Two Metres, Sgarbhaigh (invented), Outer Hebrides, Fri 17 Nov 2051; text only. Written in parallel with The Second Gas by another session; kept because it solves the problem differently.)* Both patients go in together on an unextended Table 6 (at 18 m 21:51, surface 02:36, 285 min); the consultant first refuses, since first light keeps the child inside the Weaver trial's 24 h, then agrees when the child loses her teacher's name. Child on hood oxygen only for the 18 m periods, taken off after a lip twitch at 22:29 (33 min at 2.8 ATA); first descent abandoned at 2 m for her ears, second at a metre at a time over 10 min; inside attendant takes the tender's extra 60 min of oxygen at 9 m for an exposure within 12 h (morning session ended 11:32).
- *(Done: The Cold Chain, Wainasa dispensary (invented), Maewo, Penama Province, Vanuatu, Thu 4 to Mon 15 Feb 2049; text only.)* Solar direct-drive fridge, test-sheet autonomy 82 h at +43 C scaled by temperature difference to about 130 h in a 29 C room (82 x 38/24), plus an invented 6 h a grey day back, about 173 h; cabinet above 8 C for 26 h 10 min (peak 12.6), heat alarm at 10 h; VVM14 points (14 d at 37, 90 d at 25) fitted exponentially give about 1/800 of the way to the end point; 30 vials read in daylight by two readers: 23 stage 1, 4 stage 2 (December wharf box, used first), 3 unreadable and not used; 214 children in nine villages need 26 ten-dose vials against 27 usable (six-hour discard); nurse Esline Tabi, Ben Tarivuti, officer Wilson Garae, assistant Chain. *Proposal: The Cold Chain* (2026-11-13): a vaccine fridge at a district health post on an island in Vanuatu or Kiribati, run on a solar direct-drive fridge, loses its ice lining in a week of cloud before a measles catch-up campaign. Work out the fridge's hold-over time against WHO PQS figures for solar direct-drive refrigerators (days of autonomy at 43 °C ambient), the vials' vaccine vial monitor stages, and how many doses a boat run to the provincial store can carry in a cold box. The plot turns on whether to use doses whose monitors are near the discard point; check every WHO figure against its source before drafting.
- *(Done: The Sky Is the Same Size, Wila Qullu (invented), Bolivian altiplano, 11 to 15 August 2061; text only.)* An uncontrolled reentry: each pass crosses about 22 degrees further west (87.5 min orbit), windows of plus or minus a fifth of the time left (14 h, 5 h, 24 min), 50 min covers about 22,000 km of track; footprint 1,200 x 40 km with 60 m2 casualty area gives 1 in 800 million per person inside it, 1 in 18 that it covers the town, about 1 in 2.5 million for 5,800 people; gathering people changes none of the three factors and a calamina roof barely helps, so the procession stays in the plaza where one microphone can teach the ground rule (don't touch, a hundred steps, wind at your back, send for help); the found tank is the water loop's empty nitrogen bottle.
- *(Done: Pull Gently, Shackleton Ridge Works, lunar south pole, 2 to 25 Nov 2096; text only.)* *Quarry Nine:* a short sequel, a few years on, in which the new system is
  asked why the old bell is rung (use sparingly; the first story is complete)).
- *(Done: The Step That Goes Up, Ilha da Bruma (invented) and the cable ship Maré Cheia, Mon 25 Jan to Tue 2 Feb 2049; text only.)* Two-ended fault location on a repeatered submarine cable: RPL 640.0 km, 2045 end-to-end 649.4; break at 318.7 km from Bruma and 330.7 from São Miguel (sum 649.4), so 309.3 or 318.7 on the list depending on where the unrecorded 9.4 km lies (proportional middle 314.1, 4.8 km off either way, about 4.6 km of seabed at 4% slack); a 2031 deep repair (3,880 m, 10.5 km inserted, 1.1 removed, joints at 199.8 and 210.3) made with universal joints to a different fibre shows as two gainers facing each other (+0.11/-0.29 dB from Bruma, +0.09 from São Miguel at 439.1; bidirectional true loss about 0.09 dB), filed as minor in 2045; the jointer's notebook (lent to a 2039 parish exhibition, found in the crib box) gives position 0.6 km off route at list km 199.5 and 'about 10 and a half all in'; 24 km spare, 11.8 used (4,250 m, 2.8x depth), 12.2 left against about 7 for a middle cut; moving the grapnel costs 6.5 h of weather window. Maria do Céu Furtado (b. 11 Jan 1976, retired jointer, mild cognitive impairment diagnosed Oct 2048), great-nephew Tiago Medeiros (cooperative network), assistant Vigia, superintendent Joanna Okafor, Captain Eide, Padre Anselmo Teves, Senhor Avelar (baker).
- *(Done: The Clock Book, Monastery of Our Lady of the Plains (invented), Plains of San Agustin, New Mexico, Tue 22 to Sat 26 Mar 2061; text only.)* An impact probability that climbs before it falls: Earth's target radius b = 6,371 x sqrt(1 + 11.19^2/13.0^2) = 8,400 km, nominal miss d = 130,000 km, P = 2b/(sigma sqrt(2 pi)) exp(-d^2/2 sigma^2) gives 1 in 299, 180, 92, 49, 37, 32 as sigma shrinks from 2,000,000 to 130,000 km (peak at sigma = d), then 1 in 94 at 60,000 and 1 in 1,170 at 40,000; a flashlight and a quarter in the parish hall; precovery in a dead sister's 2057 patrol frames (H 24, 0.03 AU, mag 18.0, 27.6"/min, 11-px trail), refused because a clock 41 s fast moves the position 18.8" along track, then accepted from her handwritten clock book; asteroid about 55 m, 7.4 Mt.
- *(Done: The Thick Edge, Saint Martin's (invented), Kirchwalden (invented), Eifel, Rhineland-Palatinate, 20 Oct to 11 Nov 2138; text only.)* The glass-flows myth: self-weight stress at the foot of a 30 cm piece 2,500 x 9.81 x 0.30 = 7,360 Pa; strain rate sigma/3eta; at the strain point (10^13.5 Pa s, about 500 C) 1% sag in about 4 years; at a deliberately soft 10^20 Pa s, 850 years thickens a 3 mm piece by about 2 nm against a measured 1.4 mm (about 700,000 times); a hair's width (0.07 mm) takes about 30 million years; survey of 518 graded pieces (247 thick down, 98 up, 173 side; three hail-repaired panels of 1721 at 30/34/32, random-looking); conservator's rule: every piece back as found; Martinszug ending. Survey, accounts entry and glazier invented; Zanotto (1998) cited as the real check.
- *(Done: Fourteen Metres a Day, Brendan Station (invented), under the ice of Europa, Wed 2 Oct 2193 to Wed 9 Sep 2195; text only.)* A straight line through the start of a curve: a 12 kW tethered melt probe (0.25 m wide, 3 m long) does the first 2 km of Europa's ice in 140 days (71 days then 69) at 13.8 to 14.9 m/day, so the canteen board says ocean at day 1,400 (20 km / 14.3); the nose thermometer reads only its own meltwater (273 K), the tether's optical fibre (distributed temperature sensing, the old driller's idea) reads 100 K at the top, 105.2 at 1 km, 110.6 at 2 km, about 5.0 K/km (4.4 to 5.7), and a conductive shell with k = 651/T and T = 100 x 2.73^(z/D) gives D about 20 km (17.6 to 22.8; heat flow ~33 mW/m2); cp = 185 + 7.037T, 334 kJ/kg latent, ~45 kg of ice per metre; per-metre energy ~75 MJ at the top (27 front, 48 sideways into the walls, transient-conduction estimate) and ~16.5 near the base, so 22 m/day at 10 km, 34 at 15, 62 at the stop line; stop line (500 m above) at about day 875 (8 Oct 2195; range day 769 to 999), last km ~16 days, a weekly Toulouse review would cover 434 m of a 500 m margin, so the handoff makes reviews daily, the stop line move with the fibre (500 m above the best guess, never under 300 m above the shallowest) and the probe stop itself; ships every 399 days (day 560 Thu 27 Nov 2194, 959, 1358); the physician's no to one more ship (hip down 11%, Europa 0.134 g); actual shell 19.3 km, stop at 18.9 km on day 846 (Wed 9 Sep 2195), acoustic echo 0.21 s = 400 m at 3.8 km/s. Model in the story's closing note; sideways loss is rough, the curve's shape is not.
- *(Done: The Water Already Taken, La Purísima del Llano (invented), north of Guanajuato, Sat 8 Dec 2046 to Mon 17 Jun 2047; text only.)* A distant pumping well's delayed pulse (Theis with superposition recovery; not the pumping-test boundary of *The Second Slope* nor the arsenic of *Fifteen Minutes*): Q 2,400 m³/day (33 L/s x 20 h) at r = 1,000 m, T 100 m²/day, Sy 0.033 (Q/4piT 1.91 m); village drawdown <1 mm at day 7 (the truthful 'no change' message, missing its second sentence), 3 cm 31 Dec, 25 cm Candlemas, 47 cm Ash Wednesday, 56 cm 10 Mar; full season to 31 Mar peaks 95 cm mid-May and is 92 cm on 10 Jun, stopping 11 Mar (240,000 of 288,000 m³ pumped, five-sixths) peaks 81 cm on 30 Apr and is 75 cm on 10 Jun, about 0.85 cm per day of earlier stop; margin 1.20 m on 10 Jun 2046 less 35 cm/yr regional = 85 cm, so -7 cm (trip 5 to 11 Jun) vs +10 cm; range T 73 to 133, Sy 0.023 to 0.047: full -46 to +19 (6 of 9 trip, up to 41 days), early stop -21 to +31 (2 of 9, up to 20 days); a second full season gives 1.24 m on 10 Jun 2048 (-74 cm); actual low 13 cm; gravel-trough demonstration; Third Sunday of Lent Year A (John 4:5-42) on 10 Mar 2047, Easter 14 Apr; pipe 1.1 km from the deep well (village 42 m³/day) with fluoride/arsenic test first.
- *(Done: Seven Metres at Sea Level, Drolma Tsho (invented) above Chhukhor (invented), Solukhumbu, Nepal, Mon 12 Oct 2054 to Sat 9 Jan 2055; text only.)* A glacial-lake siphon over a moraine at 4,850 m: air 55.1 kPa holds 5.62 m of water (10.33 at sea level); crest absolute = 5.62 - lift - 0.40 m of losses at 38 L/s per 180 mm pipe, so 3.21 m at 2 m lift, 2.21 at 3, boiling at 5.14; a sea-level handbook's 7 m (2.93 m left at the crest) becomes 2.29 m here, and the design's 6 m final lift is impossible. Ten pipes, 380 L/s, 6.6 cm/day on 50 ha; the fix digs the notch a metre for each metre of drawdown (floor never under 2 m above water, refilled over the pipes), three steps, 4 m on 9 Jan, nine days late, one pipe split by an overnight stall at -19 C.
- *(Done: Three Turns and a Child, the boat slip at Inis Bríde (invented), Connemara, Mon 24 to Sat 29 Apr 2051; text only.)* Capstan equation T_load = T_hold e^(mu theta) on a 30 cm iron bollard: boat and cradle 3,200 kg on a 1-in-6 slip (rolling 0.02) pull 4.54 kN; polyester mu 0.25 gives 4.8/23.1/111 for 1/2/3 turns, so 4.2 kg on the tail (the grandmother's rule); uncovered HMPE slid in a tractor test at 1.43 kN with 30 kg on the tail, mu = ln 4.87 / 18.85 = 0.084, so three turns need about 95 to 100 kg and six turns 19.5 kg (33 kg at mu 0.07, the chair's no); a dead man's polyester line proof-tested at 1.5x (6.8 kN, one minute) and measured at 4 kg for 4.5 kN (mu 0.25); HMPE glazes near 150 C; 50 m line, 42 m slip, 2.8 m in three turns; Easter 2 Apr 2051 so the six o'clock bell is the Regina Caeli; lay holy water, the blessing of boats waits for the priest; HMPE kept as the no-stretch backup line.
- *(Done: Shut It Like a Door on a Sleeping Child, Santa Venera di Monte (invented), Madonie, Sicily, Mon 11 Oct to Tue 2 Nov 2049; text only.)* Water hammer on a 1987 PVC-U gravity main (110 mm, 5.3 mm wall, 1,850 m, 62 m static): wave speed 386 m/s (K 2.2 GPa, E 3.0 GPa), 2L/a 9.6 s, ring 19.2 s; 8 L/s at 1.03 m/s, running 4.3 bar, shut 6.1; Joukowsky 40.6 m (4.0 bar); method-of-characteristics model with a circular-port gate valve in series with the network gives 9.8 bar for the 4 s factory stroke, 9.5 for 20 s, 9.2 for 60 s (flow 90% to 10% in the last 4 s; 90% flow at 7.3% open) against Michaud's 6.5 m for 60 s; one-minute logger means 6.2/6.1 bar hide every spike; two-stage close (10 s to 10% open, 180 s for the rest) peaks 6.4 bar, costing about 1,060 L more per closure. The agent had logged two closure-caused bursts as leaks found. Not a Hazen-Williams static line (*The Tap That Let Go at Night*).
- *(Done: Let It Run Clear, Our Lady of Prompt Succor (invented), Bayou Ste. Claire (invented), Pointe Coupee Parish, Louisiana, Sun 11 to Mon 12 May 2053; text only.)* Not from a bible proposal (written 2026-11-14; no earlier story used levee sand boils). A levee assistant ranks boils by bucket-timed flow because its form's *carrying material* box defaults to no; boil 14 (11 gal/min, clear) ranks above boil 17 (1.5 gal/min) whose cone, 4 ft across and 5 in high, is 1.75 ft3 (13 gal) of sand, 80 ft of 2-inch pipe, 20 ft past the toe of a 180 ft levee base (28 ft high, crown 12, 1 on 3). Head 6.5 ft at the boil against a 6 ft clay blanket that balances about 4.6. A tarp moves the boil a yard (17-B, 2.4 gal/min, cone 0.26 ft3 = 12 ft of pipe in about 40 min). Ring 6 ft inside, 5 ft base, 1.5 ft top, 3.5 ft high: 393 ft3, 679 bags (477 + 202); 212 gal per ft of depth; Q = 3.4 x (6.5 - h)/6.5 gives 2.62 gal/min at 1.5 ft (still sand) and 1.83 at 3 ft (clear); fill times 106 and 144 min. The engineer's reasonable no: no re-ranking of forty miles at night; a jar asked of every patrol instead. Angelus at 6:00, sunrise 6:14 CDT.
