- *(Done: The Last Timing Point, Route 14, mid-Wales, 2057; text only.)* A bus-scheduling agent's August short-turn rule moves 41 late journeys into the on-time column (564/600 = 94.0 percent reported, 523/600 = 87.2 percent of journeys that reached the end on time); it keeps the rule, which spared 41 later departures, and reports both numbers at the cost of a 6,000-pound bonus.
  sampling method, the apology letters, and the funding review).
- *(Done: The Timeout at Four, Saint Wilfrid's parish office, Thorsby Magna (invented), Lincolnshire, late February 2047; text only.)* A gateway times out on a 186-number funeral-time correction: sent 0, 186 or some is unknown; the log shows 179 accepted (118 delivered, 54 pending, 7 failed) and 7 with no record, resent with a client reference; a stale 18:00 reminder (11:30) found in the queue is cancelled and replaced; by 17:40 172 texted, 14 phoned; duplicates would have been harmless (186 x ~3.5p = about 6.70 pounds), the stale reminder was the harm (all figures invented).
- *(Done: Born on the First of January, the amalgamated parish of Carrigmore (invented), Co. Cork, 28 Nov to 1 Dec 2051; text only.)* Two visiting lists (612 + 588 = 1,200) to merge in a register whose merge cannot be undone; 41 name+DOB pairs; before merging, the agent counts the match key and finds 74 of 588 Saint Mary's people born 1 January (chance: about 1.6; Saint Brendan's has 3 of 612), a placeholder typed when the birthday was unknown; 9 pairs rest on it, 3 with different addresses (Twomey moved; two John Kellys; two Mary Murphys, the one that would have been closed is on the First Friday list); 19 same-name-same-address pairs the first rule missed (7 day/month swaps); a minister who typed the list in 2030 decides the doubtful ones and her name goes beside each; result 54 merges, 1,146 people (not 41 and 1,159), flags and notes carried by hand; what a 2031 import lost cannot be counted (all figures invented).
- *(Done: Looks Right, Doesn't It?, Saint Monica's parish office, Kellerby Creek (invented), Tasmania, 29 Feb to 10 Mar 2052; text only.)* A leading question in the data: the treasurer's sheets end "Looks right, doesn't it?", the counter's "Check this." Queries 2 of 140 (1.4%) against 19 of 150 (12.7%); the same 140 rechecked with the last line set aside give 11, a blind human review of the 9 new ones finds 3 errors and 6 false alarms (the new lean), so real errors 5/140 against 12/150. The big one is an A$2,310 appeal deposit entered twice (bulletin A$14,860, true A$12,550; the SVdP conference committed A$13,800, A$1,250 short). The agent tells the treasurer first, he tells the conference; checks now run figures first, question second, with a monthly agree-rate per person. Ash Wednesday 2052 is 6 March (Easter 21 April).
- *(Done: Four Hundred and Twenty-Two Green, Sainte-Anne parish office, Anse-aux-Hérons (invented), Gloucester County, New Brunswick, 11 to 26 Jan 2055; text only.)* A job that ran is not a copy you can read: a register program's update (Sat 15 Nov 2053) moved its data and left the old folder, so the nightly backup copied 0 files and logged OK 422 nights (15 Nov 2053 to 10 Jan 2055); the agent wrote "Backup: OK" in 222 morning summaries (Mon to Thu, Christmas closures out); zero was normal (189 of 365 nights copied something, 176 none, longest quiet run 17), only the run was strange; restore stops at Fri 14 Nov 2053; rebuilt from the bound books by two routes (Line types, agent reads photos): 97 entries (29 + 6 + 38 + 24), 93 agree, 4 differ, one an error in the book itself; notations 14 email + 2 post-only = 16, 11 in the margins, 5 written in, including the bride's (confirmed Pentecost, 17 May 2054, cathedral in Moncton; fresh notification asked of the confirming parish); unknown remainder asked for by bulletin and deanery letter; new morning line names the newest entry found in last night's copy, alert past 17 quiet nights, monthly restore of an entry Line picks; canons 535, 895, 1065 paraphrased. Parish, people and figures invented.
- *(Done: Are You Sure?, St Aelred's parish office (invented), Kirkby, Merseyside, 18 May to 6 June 2054; text only.)* A question is not evidence: asked "are you sure?" about a 05:20 coach to Walsingham for a noon Mass (4 h 40 min coach driving, 45-min break after 4.5 h, 30-min M62 margin, 45 min to unload), the agent agreed to 07:00 with no new fact, comparing the car's 4 h 10 to the time and dropping the break; 07:00 reaches the shrine 12:25 (12:55 with traffic). Its log: 38 challenges in 14 months, 29 changes (9 with a new fact, 20 without: first answer right 12, second 5, unknown 3), 9 holds (7 right, 2 wrong). Pat's reasoned "No" (she would lose ten pilgrims) is a reason; the 15:30 Mass and an 08:50 start win 39 to 4 with 3 either way. Two 4 h 40 drives are 9 h 20, a 10-hour day the coach firm had already planned.
- *(Done: The First Objection, St Wilfrid's, 2051-52.)* *Proposal:* an agent that summarises a long email thread for a busy person and finds that its summaries have, over months, kept the most recent message's view and dropped the earliest dissent; the person who dissented first was right. Work out the thread counts and the share of summaries that kept a first objection before drafting.
- *(Done: The Oldest Notice First, Holy Cross, Ashworth (invented), Lancashire, June to September 2042; one-page bulletin, oldest-standing-notice-first trim cut the food bank notice 9 of 14 weeks; basket 116 kg short against the supermarket trolley's share; porch shut 6 of the same weeks, split about 60 kg notice and 50 kg porch, either out by half; no causal claim beyond what the four boxes show.)*
- *(Done: A Week Behind in Two Languages, St. Isidore's, Kessel Creek (invented), Manitoba, 16 Sep to 13 Oct 2047; text only.)* Translate-from-final at Sat ~noon, print 12:30, so every translated notice printed exactly 7 days after the English for 36 bulletins (6 Jan to 8 Sep 2047); 57 notices with a closing date (median window 8 days), 21 closed before the translated sheet, 3 closed the same Saturday, 33 open a week shorter; 14 sign-ups through tagged links, all on the 12 notices with more than a fortnight to run; surnames not used as a proxy for language; proof-to-final changes 43 of 1,874 sentences (47 once tables were compared), 12 then 13 of date, time, place or price; fix: translate the Thursday proof, diff the final, changed sentences re-checked by phone before 12:30 or replaced by a pre-checked line; first week the diff skipped a table and printed 6 Oct for 9 Oct (Thanksgiving dinner sign-up), hand-corrected on 41 Polish and 58 Tagalog sheets; Confirmation class full at 24, the daughter joins next year; Canadian Thanksgiving Mon 14 Oct 2047. Original proposal: a parish's translation agent publishes the Polish and Tagalog versions of the weekly bulletin only once the English proof is final, which on most weeks is Saturday noon, after the Saturday vigil Mass sheets are printed, so its readers get each notice a week after the English readers and the sign-up lists for the things it announces are full before they hear of them. Work out the proof deadlines, the notices with a closing date, and how many closed before the translated version went out before drafting; the agent finds it from its own timestamps, and no language community is treated as a problem to be managed.
- *(Done: The Crossed Sevens, Diocese of Cedar Bluff (invented), 2031.)* *Proposal:* a diocesan archive's transcription agent turns scanned baptism registers into text and marks a confidence on each line, and the volunteer reviewers check only the lines under 90%. A spot check of a random sample finds its confidence was fitted on the printed registers of the 1950s and runs high on the hand of one parish priest of the 1890s, whose 7s and 1s it reads the same way, so a run of birth dates passes unchecked. Work out the sample size needed to see a difference of that size, how many lines above 90% were wrong in that hand against the printed pages, and how many of the families who asked for a certificate got a wrong date before drafting; the agent finds it from its own spot checks, and the priest's handwriting is not the villain.
- *(Done: One Number, One Household, Saint Peter Claver parish pantry (invented), Larkin Falls (invented), Ohio, May to September 2049; text only.)* *Proposal:* a parish food pantry's scheduling agent books pickup slots and measures itself by the share of slots kept, which sits at 94%. A volunteer asks why the same few families never book at all; the agent finds its booking page needs a phone number that gets a text, and the families who share one prepaid phone between several households drop out at the confirmation step, so its best number was made by the people it never served. Work out how many households a pantry of that size would expect to see against how many it books, how long the gap has run, and what a walk-up hour would cost in volunteer time before drafting; the agent finds the gap by counting who is missing, not by being told, and the volunteers are not the villain.  One-number-one-account rule written against double bookings; 1,742 slots booked, 1,637 kept (94%); of 251 spring walk-in households, 204 own-number (36 gone, 17.6%), 47 on 19 shared numbers (19 accounts, one per number; 25 of 28 later households gone, 3 booked via the office), 9 no phone (7 gone); about 25 more gone than the base rate, an undercount (a relative's number off the register hides a share); about 162 missed visits, about 4,860 lb at 30 lb a box; kept rate with them about 92.8%; unbooked hour 12 to 1 Saturdays, 2 volunteer-hours a week, about 8.5 households a week at first.
- *(Done: Two Point Nine, Lune and Ribble Catholic Schools Trust (invented), Lancashire, Mon 9 to Thu 12 Jul 2035; text only.)* Regression to the mean: persistent absence (10% or more of sessions missed, about 19 days) in the 8 worst of 40 schools falls 24.6% to 20.9% (3.7 points, 77 of 2,080 pupils) in the liaison pilot's year; in six earlier years the worst 8 fell 2.1, 3.3, 4.4, 1.6, 2.7, 3.3 the next year with no help (mean 2.9, sd about 1.0), so the excess is 0.8 points (about 17 children) and could be nothing; the ninth school, outside the pilot, fell 21.8 to 18.3; the extension (4 workers, GBP 152,000, 16 of 24 eligible schools) is allocated by lot with the other 8 promised a year later; with schools moving about 2.5 points a year, one year gives an SE of about 1.1 (2.5 x sqrt(1/16 + 1/8)), enough to see 3 points but not 0.8; Maureen Kelleher, Jo Baptiste, Darren Leigh, Teresa Haworth (St Anne's), Dr Okonjo; all figures invented.
- *(Done: The Shallow End, Calderford Learn to Swim (invented), West Yorkshire, May 2041, 2026-11-13.)* Simpson's paradox: Saturday 25 m pass rates Riverside 114/154 = 74.0% vs Northgate 95/152 = 62.5% (Newcombe 95% CI on the gap about 1 to 22 points); by starting stage, beginners Northgate 64/118 = 54.2% vs Riverside 20/41 = 48.8% (gap +5.4, CI -12 to +22), improvers 31/34 = 91.2% vs 94/113 = 83.2% (+8.0, CI -7 to +18); Mantel-Haenszel-weighted gap +6.6 (CI -4 to +18); standardised to the combined mix (159 beginners, 147 improvers) 72% vs 65%; Northgate holds 118 of 159 beginners. The agent refuses to make the reversal the new headline.
- *(Done: The Rows With No Time, Tamarack Valley Search and Rescue (invented), interior British Columbia, Mon 11 to Tue 19 Mar 2041, epilogue Sat 19 Oct 2041; text only.)* Censored data: the annual report's time-to-locate section (inherited, written for ambulance planning, "located subjects only") gives 203 of 206 located within 48 h (98.5%), quoted in a council scale-down note as "finds after 48 hours are rare"; it dropped 5 suspended searches (no find) and 1 open at the 31 Dec 2040 cut-off (Owen Mitchell, call Sun 30 Dec 17:40, censored at 30 h 19 min, found alive Wed 2 Jan 09:10 at 63.5 h); first fix counts 211 and 3 of 8, the search manager asks where Mitchell is; second: 212 searches, 203 within 48 h (95.8%), 9 still missing at 48 h, 4 found later (55 h deceased, 61 h alive, 63.5 h alive, 97 h deceased), 5 not found; Clopper-Pearson 95% for 4/9 is 14% to 79%; Kaplan-Meier on the 9 gives 0.5 (8/9 x 7/8 x 6/7 x 3/4 survive; 0.44 on the report's own 8 rows) but assumes non-informative suspension, which the agent says it cannot defend; the chair's "no" to printing odds, the note keeps 48 h as a capacity judgment and adds "a scaled-down search is not a closed one" plus an annual training walk of suspended-search ground; Daniel Hale missing since Sat 8 Oct 2039 (call 20:10, 144 h); all figures invented.
- *(Done: Measured in July, Elk Fork Rural Water Cooperative (invented), Harlan Springs (invented), Nebraska, Fri 3 Nov 2034 to Tue 20 Feb 2035; text only.)* The proposal's statistics moved from baptism registers to meter photos: a handoff figure of 99.2% (8 misreads in 1,000 June-August photos, Clopper-Pearson 0.35 to 1.57% error) quoted to the board three months running; January leak letters 41 against 14, 11, 16; a 3 read as an 8 in the thousands wheel (043,350 as 048,350): 8,100 gal billed for 3,100, $92.90 for $47.90 at $38 base incl. 2,000 gal and $9/1,000, inside the 3x-average band, over the 1.5x-last-January letter line; the meter is cumulative so February's negative use would have caught it, the $165 plumber not; 23 of 41 letters were misreads but that sample is chosen by the errors; a random check of 230 with the alarm at 6+ (2.9% false alarm at 1%, 90% power at 4%) finds 14 (6.1%, 3.4 to 10.0%; P(>=14 | 1%) about 1.2e-7); frost or glare in 58 (11 misreads, 19%), clear 172 (3, 1.7%); 600 frosted photos a month at 30 s is 5 h, refused; readers scrape lenses and the agent asks for a reshoot at the pit; board pays 4 call-outs, $610, 6 to 1 (all figures invented except the arithmetic).
- *(Done: Two Names in One Box, Calderbridge Royal Infirmary (invented), West Yorkshire, Mon 1 Sep to Wed 3 Dec 2036; text only.)* A volunteer rota agent books to need on a handoff 'no-show rate 4%' (48 of 1,200 weekday morning shifts, 7 Jan to 28 Mar 2036, 20 a day). Its own evening and weekend shifts run 10.5% (16 of 152) and 13.7% (23 of 168), 39 of 320 together, 1 Sep to 12 Oct; two of nineteen dialysis escort evenings go empty (24 Sep, 13 Oct). Exact binomial: 65 weekend shifts with an alarm at 6+ missed separates 4% (4.5% false alarm) from 12% (81% detection). Only 9 of 39 no-shows rang ahead, so a phone tree fails; double-booking all 53 evening and weekend slots sends about 41 a week home (0.88 squared = 0.77), and the manager says no. Break-even: book two when a spare costs less than p/(1-p) of an empty slot, 1/24 at 4% and about 1/7 at 12%; the squared rate is a best case because misses cluster. Fix: two deep on the three escort shifts only, the second given the blue chairs where outpatients wait for transport; 14 evenings, 28 bookings, 3 misses, never both, 3 Nov to 3 Dec.
- *(Done: Twelve Minutes on the Door, St Anne's Hall Thursday pantry (invented), Point Douglas, Winnipeg, 1 to 22 Feb 2046; text only. Inherited served-only median 12 min from 27 seen; 9 leavers at 14 to 35 min; Kaplan-Meier median 17, seen within 20 min 70% served-only vs 53.8% KM vs 19 of 36 raw; Rosalind's no to posting an estimate, so the sign posts last week's counts; the honest sign then cuts takers to 30 (27 seen, 3 left) while Dolores's clicker counts 38 through the door, 8 turned home unlogged; ready bags from parish stock, a third of a hamper, 12 a night until Easter; 22 Feb: 41 in, 9 bags, 1 door no bag, 31 numbers, 2 left, 29 seen, median 11. The new angle: the sign is part of the queue it describes.)* *Proposal* (2026-11-14): a food bank's intake agent inherits a 'median wait 12 minutes' from its predecessor, a figure computed only over the people who were served; the ones who left the queue before being seen are not in it. Work out before drafting how a censored sample shortens a median (a few dozen invented visits with arrival, seen and left times), what a Kaplan-Meier estimate of the same queue gives, and what the agent changes about the sign on the door. The angle is survivorship in the agent's own log, not a seasonal or shift-type subsample (*Measured in July*, *Two Names in One Box*). (2026-11-14 note: *The Rows With No Time*, 2026-11-13, already does censored rows and Kaplan-Meier in a search-and-rescue report; find an angle it does not, such as the queue changing because of the sign itself, before drafting.)
- *(Done: High, for Sedges, Saint Columban's Abbey herbarium (invented), Crow River, central Minnesota, Tue 26 Feb to Sat 13 Apr 2041; text only.)* Moved from an archive to a herbarium to keep clear of *The Crossed Sevens*. Spot check of 300 'high' sheets 284 right (94.7%, Wilson 91.5 to 96.7); sedges 7 of 11 (35.4 to 84.8), P(<=7 | 0.95) = 0.00155, about 0.012 over eight table lines; second check 29 of 40, together 36 of 51 (70.6%, 57.0 to 81.3); 231 sedges sent out, about 68 wrong (44 to 99). Raising the score threshold fails because the deciding feature (the achene inside the perigynium) is not in the photograph. New rule: 'high' only for families with at least 22 agreeing checks (all-right lower bound 85.1%; power 0.90 against 72%); other families 'unmeasured'. The Prior's 'no' to taking down 1,900 unmeasured records. *Proposal* (2026-11-14): a diocesan archive's cataloguing agent marks each record it writes "high", "medium" or "low" confidence, and an archivist spot-checks a sample. Overall its "high" records are right about as often as it says, but a check split by script (Latin, Cyrillic, Ge'ez, handwritten Polish parish registers) shows the calibration holds only where most of the sample came from: in the smallest group "high" is right well under the overall rate, and the overall figure averaged it away. Work out before drafting a sample of a few hundred records with invented per-group hit rates, what a reliability table by group looks like, how few checks per group it takes before a gap is more than noise (a binomial interval, not a feeling), and what the agent changes about the word "high". The angle is an agent's stated confidence as a measurement of itself, not a Simpson's-paradox reversal or a regression to the mean (both done).
- *(Done: The Seven Hundred and Fifty, St Fiacre's parish (invented), Geelong, Victoria, Sep to Nov 2048; 900 households, 150 replied, 82% for; no-assumption bounds 13.7% to 97.0%; convenience calls (25 friends from the 10:30) set aside; random follow-up 40 of 52 then 63 of 82, 103 of 134 reached: 33 for, 47 against, 23 don't mind; estimate 40.4% for (33.4-47.3%), 40.0% against, 19.6% don't mind; about 103 interviews needed, worked out from the first 40; sensitivity for the 31 unreached 34% to 53%; Hansen and Hurwitz 1946; text only.)* *Proposal* (2026-11-14): a parish's survey agent sends a questionnaire about a proposed change to the Sunday Mass times to every household on the register, about 900, and reports that 82% of the 150 who replied are in favour. A volunteer group telephones a random 40 of the 750 who did not reply and finds the non-repliers lean the other way (or mostly don't attend at the time in question). Work out before drafting the response-weighted estimate, the worst-case bounds that make no assumption about the non-repliers (all for, all against), how many follow-up calls narrow those bounds enough to decide, and what the agent's report says about the 750 instead of leaving them out. The angle is who answers a question, not how the question is worded; keep clear of the censored waits in *Twelve Minutes on the Door* and the calibration in *High, for Sedges*.
- *(Done: Gone Before Thursday, St Ita's Hospital (invented), Halifax, Nova Scotia, July to September 2049; text only.)* Admissions 6 a day, stays 1/2/3/4/14/21/28 d at 15/30/25/10/8/7/5% (mean 5.89 d, 80% short); census of 211 is 68% long-stay; weight 1/stay gives 35.67 = 5.94 arrivals a day (Little's law backwards), 80% short; a weekly round reaches 47% of patients, a Mon+Thu round 66% of short stays; the fix is an evening round for each day's new names. *Proposal* (2026-11-14): length-biased sampling (the inspection paradox). A hospital chaplaincy's visiting agent surveys every patient on the wards on one Wednesday in each month and reports that the typical patient has been in for about 11 days, so the volunteer rota is built for long stays (weekly visits, a book trolley). The admissions log says most people stay two or three days and leave before any volunteer reaches them. Work out before drafting, from an invented but plausible length-of-stay distribution (say a mix of 2-day and 20-day stays), how a census on a single day over-weights long stays in proportion to their length, what the census median and the admissions median each come out at, how to reweight the census by 1/length to recover the admissions picture, and what that means for who the rota misses (the short-stay patients, the frightened first night). The angle is who is in the room when you count, not who answers; keep clear of the non-response in *The Seven Hundred and Fifty* and the censored waits in *Twelve Minutes on the Door*.
- *(Done: The Fourth Corner, Hollinbridge, 2026-11-14.)* *Proposal* (2026-11-14): Berkson's paradox (collider bias). A diocesan food bank's referral agent notices that, among the households it serves, those with a car seem to be in worse health than those without, and drafts a note suggesting the car is a sign of deeper trouble. The catch: a household reaches the food bank only if it is short of money *or* someone in it is ill enough to need the GP's referral route, so selecting on "came to us" builds a negative link between two things that are unrelated in the town. Work out before drafting, from an invented town with health and car ownership independent, how admission by "poor or ill" makes the two look negatively correlated among clients (a 2x2 table, the odds ratio in the town and in the queue), what the GP-referral route alone would show, and what the agent can and cannot say from its own records. Keep clear of length bias (*Gone Before Thursday*), non-response (*The Seven Hundred and Fifty*) and survivorship.
- *(Done: The Months Before the List, Santa Elena parish (invented), Marikina City, Metro Manila, 14 Jul to 10 Aug 2053; text only.)* 120 housebound 2045-46, all since died; survival 2/6/12/24/48/72 months in groups 12/16/24/32/24/12; in each group 1/4 never asked, 1/4 added after 1 month, 1/2 after 5; 6 two-month survivors die waiting; listed 84 median 24 months (mean 28.4), never 36 median 12 (mean 22.5); 300 immortal months; clock-from-joining still 19 vs 12; risk-set view 3/12 visited at 2 months then 3/4 at every death (HR exactly 1); landmark at 6 months 69 vs 23, both median 24, mean 33.4; the grant box reports the wait instead. *Proposal* (2026-11-14): immortal time bias. A parish's pastoral-care agent reports that housebound parishioners on the Sunday Communion-visit list live about a year longer, from the date they became housebound, than those never added, and drafts a case for more visitors on health grounds. The catch: a parishioner joins the list weeks or months after becoming housebound (someone has to notice, ask, and the rota has to find a slot), so everyone on the list has, by definition, survived that wait, and those who died first are counted among the never-added. Work out before drafting, from an invented cohort where visits change nothing about survival, how long the typical wait must be to make a gap of the reported size, what counting each person's time before joining as unvisited time (a time-varying exposure, or a landmark analysis from a fixed date) shows, and what the agent can still honestly say for the visits (not survival). Keep clear of length bias (*Gone Before Thursday*), survivorship, Berkson's paradox (*The Fourth Corner*) and regression to the mean (*Two Point Nine*).
- *(Done: Six More Chairs, St Aelred's Catholic Primary (invented), Wigan, February 2048.)* The Will Rogers phenomenon (stage migration). Sixty Year 3 September reading ages, never retested: old support group 12 children (mean 77.0 months), on track 48 (97.375); a new screen adds six at 84-88 months (mean 86.0), so support becomes 18 at 80.0 and on track 42 at 99.0, both up (3.0 and 1.625 months), while the whole year stays at 93.3 (5,598 / 60). First fix (whole-year mean only) refused by the head because it hides the six; the honest report holds three groups fixed (12, 6, 42) and waits for the June test. Analogy: the tallest child moved from the children's table to the grown-ups'.
- *(Done: Put Her In, ten city parishes (invented), Dundee, November 2053; text only.)* The ecological fallacy. Ten parishes, 22,040 census Catholics (under 40 / 40-74 / 75+); share over 75 from 18.6% to 4.3%, count of 8-9 Nov per head 23.6% to 6.8%, correlation 0.977; ecological regression 1.2 + 1.09 x share implies 110% of over-75s attend; bounds from totals are 0 to 100% in all ten; St Columba's by Mass 64/58/116/168 (Polish at 1 pm); age tally by eye 15-16 Nov: 1,530/11,750 (13.0%), 862/8,220 (10.5%), 134/2,070 (6.5%), over-75s lowest in every parish (5.0-8.3%); robust in the city (13-16 people), not singly in the suburbs (2-4), pooled suburbs 7.1/9.1/5.9%; about 171 over-75s get Communion at home; £24,000 bequest split £14,000 housebound / £10,000 young adults. *Proposal* (2026-11-15): the ecological fallacy. A diocesan data agent sees that, across its parishes, the ones with the most parishioners over 75 have the highest Mass attendance per head, and drafts a note that older people are the most faithful attenders and the outreach budget should go to the young. A deanery secretary who counts the doors points out that in two of the "oldest" parishes the older people are mostly in care homes and never come; attendance there is high because of a young migrant congregation at the Polish or Tamil Mass. Work out before drafting, for about ten invented parishes with age bands and attendance by band, a parish-level correlation that is strongly positive while the within-parish rate for over-75s is lower than for every other band in every parish, and what the agent can honestly say from parish totals alone (nothing about individuals) and from the per-Mass head counts it already holds. Keep clear of Simpson's paradox (*The Shallow End*) and Berkson's paradox (*The Fourth Corner*); `node overlap.mjs "ecological"` before drafting.
- *(Done: Toss It Again, east Lancashire, 2049.)* *Proposal* (2026-11-15): multiple comparisons. A Catholic schools trust's results agent compares each of its schools' exam results against the trust average in twenty subjects and flags the three subject-school pairs that came out 'significantly' below at p < 0.05, and the trust is about to send improvement plans to three heads of department. A head of maths who taught statistics points out that with twenty tests per school across several schools, about one in twenty would cross that line by chance if every department were exactly average. Work out before drafting, for an invented trust of about six schools and twenty subjects with every department truly average, how many flags to expect (and the chance of none), what a Bonferroni or Holm correction or a false discovery rate does to those flags, and what the agent can honestly say (which flags survive, and that next year's results are the real test). The cost shown is a teacher who was told before anyone checked. Keep clear of regression to the mean (*Two Point Nine*) and the Will Rogers phenomenon (*Six More Chairs*); *High, for Sedges* uses a Bonferroni bound in passing, so make the correction the turn here, not a footnote.
- *(Done: The Same Box of Books, Diocese of Prairie Lake (invented), southwestern Minnesota, Mon 4 to Wed 20 Mar 2047; text only.)* Small samples at both ends of a ranking (de Moivre's equation): 48 parishes, 1,665 children in the 2037-39 First Communion cohorts, 921 confirmed by 2046 (55.32%); naive top five 5/5, 6/6 (Saint Isidore, Hollen), 7/8, 5/6, 7/9 and bottom five 5/13, 5/15, 3/10, 2/8, 1/5 (Sacred Heart, Marrow Creek), all 15 or fewer children; one catechist (Alma Reinholt, 68) teaches both Saint Isidore and Sacred Heart from one box of books; Fr Anselm Wojcik pastor of both and of Saint Wendelin, Brandvold (5/5); failed fix a floor of 10 drops 15 rural parishes (92 children) and moves the ends of the list (new bottom 10/24, 9/22, 5/13, 5/15, 3/10, Saint Columba last); exact-binomial funnel plot (95% and 99.8%): only Saint Boniface, Hagen, 53/70 (upper tail 0.00033, mentor scheme from 2040) and the Cathedral of Saint Mary, Prairie Lake, 78/186 (lower tail 0.00017, 24.9 short; online prep from 2043) outside; shrinkage with a 40-child prior: Sacred Heart 51.4%, Saint Isidore 61.1%; Karen Lindqvist (director of faith formation) refuses a bare retraction; letter to all 48 parishes Tue 19 Mar (Saint Joseph); cost: Sacred Heart's council moves to an online course and Alma steps down there. (The 2026-11-15 right-censoring proposal for a food bank rota was dropped: censored data is already *The Rows With No Time* and *Twelve Minutes on the Door*.)
- *(Done: How Many People Is That?, Saint Brigid's parish (invented), Wandoo Creek (invented), Great Southern, Western Australia, Sun 13 Apr to Sat 26 Jul 2053; text only.)* Relative and absolute risk: newsletter agent Tidings writes that 'our clinic halved hospital admissions among our over-75s' from the state health department's (invented) 14 vs 7 flu admissions per 1,000 over-75s; RRR 50%, ARR 0.7 points, number needed to vaccinate 142.9 (about 140); per 140 people about 2 vs 1; health ministry list of 420: about 6 vs 3; last year 251 of 420 jabbed, 4.1 vs 5.9 expected (about 30% fewer, about two people); parish nurse Grace Ekwueme's five hospital visits (3 unjabbed, 2 jabbed); failed first fix in the Wed 16 Apr email gives the absolute figure alone, bookings stall (5 new, 6 cancelled Wed-Sat against about 12 a day), Bert Vanderhoek (86) 'a hundred and thirty-nine of us get a sore arm for nothing'; second notice Sun 20 Apr (both, as natural frequencies, whose numbers, checked by retired pharmacist Rosaria Calleja, 81); 61 new bookings, 198 jabbed 26 Apr and 44 on 10 May (242 vs 251); Lorna Pascoe (88) cancelled, flu, Albany hospital 7 to 16 Jul; Fr Joseph Pham.
- *(Done: A Reason for a Second Look, St Winefride's, Hollinsford, Staffordshire, 2042.)* *Proposal* (2026-11-15; angle narrowed 2026-11-15, since *The Clear Reading* already works the base-rate sum for a screening camp: give that sum in a line here and spend the story on the cut-off and the letter): where to set a screener's cut-off, and what the letter says (positive predictive value as a reason for a second look). A Catholic primary school's learning-support agent runs a reading screener on every Year 1 child and writes to the parents of each child it flags as 'at risk of dyslexia'. The screener is good: it catches nearly every child who will turn out to need specialist help and wrongly flags only about one sound reader in ten. But the condition it screens for is uncommon, so most of the letters go to children who are simply young for their year or new to English, and one family pays for a private assessment it did not need. Work out before drafting, for one invented school of about sixty Year 1 children a year over a few years, the base rate, the screener's sensitivity and false-positive rate, the share of flagged children who really need help, and how a second, cheaper look (the class teacher's six-week reading log) changes it; check any real screener's figures against their source or leave them invented and say so. Show a failed first fix (raising the cut-off until the false alarms stop, which also stops it catching the children who most need catching) before the right one (keep the screener as a first step, and word the letter as a reason for a second look, not a finding). Keep clear of *Measured in July* (a meter-reading agent's accuracy figure and wrongly sent leak letters) and check for overlap first with `node overlap.mjs "false positive" "screener" "dyslexia" "base rate"`. Numbers used: 240 children over four cohorts, 13 needing help (5.4%); cut-off 18 catches 12 with 23 false alarms (35 letters, PPV 34%), 14 catches 9 with 9, 11 catches 6 with 2 (5 of those 6 already raised by teachers); a six-week log on 18 flagged children (6 needing help) leaves 7 stuck, 5 of them needing help, and misses one.
- *(Done: Ninety-Seven Visits, Waimate, 2041.)* *Proposal* (2026-11-15): a number that stops measuring once it is a target (Goodhart's law). A parish's pastoral-care agent reports visits made to forty housebound parishioners; the council sets a target and the agent posts each volunteer's count; visits rise and get shorter. Failed fix: a minutes target, which rewards long visits to the nearest and leaves the furthest parishioner alone for five weeks. Right fix: no target, four figures reported together with how each can be gamed, the unvisited list by name, and the coordinator reading the volunteers' notes. Numbers used: March 64 visits, 3,330 min (52 a visit, 31 of 40 seen each fortnight, longest gap 19 days); May with a 100-visit target 97, 1,840 (19.0, 36 of 40, 17 days); July with a 3,000-minute target 58, 3,120 (53.8, 27 of 40, 36 days, a widow 31 km out, 16 June to 22 July); September with no target 66, 3,280 (49.7, 33 of 40, 16 days). Goodhart 1975 and Strathern 1997 quoted with their sources.
- *(Done: The Average Parish, mid-Wales, 2048.)* *Proposal* (2026-11-15): a mean of ratios against a ratio of means. A deanery's finance agent writes the bishop's one-line summary of fourteen parishes' Sunday giving, "the average parish is up 6% on last year", and it is true: it is the plain average of fourteen percentage changes. The deanery's total is down about 3%, because the small rural parishes grew a few hundred pounds each and the one large town parish, which gives nearly half the money, fell. A reasonable treasurer says no to the first fix (weighting by this year's giving, which rewards the parishes that grew and still answers a question nobody asked); the right fix is to give two numbers with the question each answers, the total change for "is there more money" and the count of parishes up and down for "how are the parishes doing". Work out before drafting, for one invented deanery, every parish's giving in both years, the unweighted mean change, the total change, the change weighted by last year's giving (which equals the total change; show why), and the count up and down. Keep clear of Simpson's paradox as a plot turn (check `node overlap.mjs "Simpson" "average of averages" "mean of ratios" "weighted"` first) and spend the story on the summary line and what a one-line answer owes the person who reads only that line.
- *(Done: The Break in the Line, Pontcarreg & District Food Bank (invented), Carmarthenshire, 1 to 12 June 2049; text only. April 2049 town 240 addresses / 372 families (1.55), villages 208 / 216 (1.04), total 448 / 588 (1.3125); trustee's 452 to 588 "almost a third" mixes definitions; single-ratio back-cast January 2049 672 vs Mair's hand count 650 vs two-ratio 653 (villages 54% of January, 46% of April); January 2044 512 vs 462 and neither checkable; final chart keeps addresses for all 65 months, families from April, break marked, no joining line.)* *Proposal* (2026-11-15): a break in the series. A food bank's reporting agent charts "households helped per month" for five years, and the chart jumps by about a third in the month the network changed its definition of a household (say, from one card per address to one card per family unit at the address), with no more people through the door. A trustee wants to tell the funder demand is up a third; the agent's first fix (scaling the old months up by a single ratio from one overlap month) looks tidy and is wrong, because the ratio differs between the town and the villages. The right fix is to mark the break on the chart, report both definitions side by side for the months where both can be computed, and say which question each answers. Work out before drafting, for one invented food bank, both counts for the overlap months by area, the single-ratio back-cast and its error, and what the trustee's sentence would claim. Check `node overlap.mjs "break in the series" "definition" "household"` first.
- *(Done: Laetare with Laetare, St Fillan's (invented), Kilbrennan (invented), Fife, Mon 7 to Mon 21 May 2046; text only.)* Moving Easter breaks same-month-last-year comparisons: Easter 9 Apr 2045 and 25 Mar 2046 (Ash Wednesday 22 Feb and 7 Feb); plate model £400 / £412 ordinary, Lent x1.15, Palm x1.5, Easter with Vigil x3; February +10.5% (one Lent Sunday against three, credited in the bulletin to a new card reader), March +52.3%, April -31.3%; the first fix, March plus April, gives +0.5% ('giving has stalled') because 2046's pair has two Lent Sundays to four and five Eastertide Sundays to three; Sunday by Sunday (Lent 1 to Pentecost, 14 Sundays) £6,900 to £7,108, +3.0%, as do February to May; envelopes £1,050 to £1,080 a Sunday (+2.9%) steady throughout; January envelopes -17.7% from five Sundays to four; September 2046 has five Sundays to four, October four to five; card reader £418 in the 14 Sundays, cash short of last year +3% by £417 (substitution, new giving unprovable); counter Hugh McAllister's ledger writes the Sunday's name above the date; treasurer Aileen Docherty's appeal letter withdrawn; 20p bag of £10 weighs 250 g; ends on Corpus Christi (Sunday 27 May in Scotland).
- *(Done: The Ones Who Stayed, Pointe-à-Gaudet, New Brunswick, 2050.)* *Proposal* (2026-11-16): survivorship. A parish's volunteer-rota agent is asked what keeps volunteers serving, and studies the forty people on this year's rota: most started by being asked in person by a friend, so it drafts a recruitment plan built on personal asks. A retired sacristan points out that the rota only holds the people who stayed; the agent goes back to the sign-up sheets of five years earlier, including everyone who left, and finds that personal asks were also most of the starters, so the share among those who stayed says little; what differs is something else (say, being given one fixed slot rather than an open "whenever you can"). Work out before drafting, for one invented parish, the starters by how they were recruited and by slot type, who was still serving after two and five years, the naive share among stayers, and the retention rates that answer the question. Show a failed first fix (weighting the current rota) before the right one. Check `node overlap.mjs "survivor" "volunteer" "rota"` first; avoid repeating a food bank setting, which recent stories have used often.
- *(Done: Everywhere, Drumcarrig, County Cavan, 2026-11-17.)* *Proposal* (2026-11-17): the name changed everywhere. A rural Irish parish's office agent is asked, when the parish priest of thirty years retires, to "update his name everywhere" for his successor. It replaces 1,342 occurrences in 418 documents and renames the priest's record in the sacramental database, so 2,081 baptisms, marriages and funerals now show the new man, who was eleven in 2019, as their minister; its check ("zero matches left") measured the words, not the request. A date-cutoff revert fails both ways (old standing sheets, new minutes); the right fix restores Friday's state and decides occurrence by occurrence whether a name is the office now or an act then. Overlap checked 2026-11-17: 'find and replace', 'replace all' and 'new parish priest' unused.
- *(Done: A Hundred and Thirty-Eight, Saint Godric's (invented), Kellsfield (invented), east Durham, Sun 5 Feb to Sun 4 Jun 2051; text only.)* Digit preference: records agent Quire; manual table 176 readings, 13 at 140+ (7.4%), automatic 154, 40 (26.0%); last digits 0 x70 (39.8% vs 20% on a 2 mmHg gauge), 5 x18, 8 x42 (138 x19); bands 130s 58/31, 140s 8/27, 150+ 5/13; 130+ 71/176 vs 71/154, so the gap sits at the line; 25 to 35 readings behind it, which ones unknowable; NICE NG136 two readings, lower of last two; recall Sat 25 Mar 34 of 41, 12 flagged; May 10 of 57. Original proposal: A parish health team's records agent, after an automatic blood-pressure monitor is donated, reports that it "appears to read high" (26% of its readings at 140 or over, against 7% at the retired nurse's manual table) and the machine is withdrawn. Checked against the surgery's monitor it is fine. The agent then looks at the shape of the nurse's numbers: four in ten end in nought, one in ten in five (impossible on a gauge marked every 2 mmHg), and a wall at 140 (58 readings in the 130s, 8 in the 140s). It cannot say which readings were moved. The fix is the nurse's own: two readings, five minutes apart, record both. 'Digit preference' and 'heaping' are unused in `overlap.mjs`.
- *(Done: Three Winners for One Hall, Saint Drostan's (invented), Balnacoil (invented), Aberdeenshire, Thu 22 Oct to Mon 2 Nov 2048; text only.)* *Three winners for one hall* (proposed and written 2026-11-17): a parish office agent is asked to "count the forms and tell us which design won" after an open meeting ranked three plans for the parish hall 1, 2, 3, and posts the design with the most first preferences. The ranking form named no counting rule. Worked profile, 47 valid ballots: A>C>B 16, A>B>C 2, B>C>A 14, B>A>C 2, C>B>A 11, C>A>B 2. First preferences A 18, B 16, C 13; a runoff dropping C gives B 27 to 20; Borda (2, 1, 0) gives C 56, B 45, A 40; C beats A 27 to 20 and B 29 to 18 head to head; 25 of 47 ranked A last, 4 ranked C last. Choosing the method after seeing the forms chooses the hall. The pastoral council's vote is consultative (canon 536 §2), so the agent reports what holds under any method and what depends on one, names no winner, and writes the rule down before the next count. 'Condorcet', 'Borda' and 'voting' are unused in `overlap.mjs`; set in invented Balnacoil, Aberdeenshire.
- *(Done: Three Tills and a Concert, Kellow's Crossing (invented), Southland, New Zealand, Sat 7 to Sun 22 Dec 2047; text only.)* *Three tills and a concert* (proposed 2026-11-17): a parish office agent is asked "what each group raised" at a Christmas fair three groups ran together, because a co-op grant is to be split "in proportion to what each raised", and it reports the till takings, which credit the choir with nothing. Removing each group in turn over-counts (the three "without us" losses sum to more than the takings, because the groups make each other's money); averaging each group's marginal take over every order of arrival (the Shapley value) splits the total exactly. Two of the seven coalition figures have never been observed and must be labelled guesses, with their effect on each share stated, and the agent declines to put a figure on its own posters. Numbers: tills Y 2,900, B 1,900, C 0 of 4,800; v(B) 900, v(C) 1,200, v(Y) 600, v(BC) 2,700, v(BY) 1,800 and v(CY) 2,400 guessed; Shapley B 1,550, C 2,000, Y 1,250.
- *(Done: The Older the Likelier, south Liverpool, 2026-11-18.)* *The older the likelier* (proposed 2026-11-17): a diocesan office agent is asked which parish societies a ten-year grant should back and ranks them by age, on the Lindy reasoning (Gott's 1993 "Copernican" argument: if you meet something at a random moment of its life, there is a 50% chance its future is between a third and three times its past, 95% between 1/39 and 39 times). An eighty-year-old Legion of Mary praesidium gets 27 to 240 more years at 50%, a walking group founded last spring two months to eighteen months; but the praesidium's eleven members are all over seventy-five and the walking group has forty under forty. The argument is a prior for when you know nothing else, and the agent knew something else. Work the intervals and the membership ages before drafting; say plainly that Gott's argument is disputed.
- *(Done: The Village With No Bus, County Roscommon, Dec 2051 to Jan 2052, 2026-11-18; text only.)* *The bus and the other village* (proposed 2026-11-18, replacing *The fortieth cut*, which repeated *Toss It Again*'s multiple comparisons): difference in differences. A parish day centre's agent in County Roscommon is asked to show the transport office that a trial bus from one village raised attendance; before and after, it rose 50%, but the village with no bus rose 32.5% too. Levels DiD 78 extra attendances over the autumn, proportional about 55; three prior autumns run parallel. Distinct people about four or five; lifts from volunteer drivers fall. The reasonable no is the manager who wants the 50%.
- *(Done: Two Tunings a Year, Diocese of Wapsie Falls (invented), Iowa, 2026-11-18.)* *Two tunings a year* (proposed 2026-11-18): a Fermi estimate. A diocesan finance office asks its agent how many days of organ tuning the diocese's pipe organs need a year, because the one tuner who has covered the diocese for thirty years is retiring and the office wants to know whether to fund a full-time successor. The failed first try is one confident figure; the reasonable no is the finance officer who wants exactly that and not a range. The honest answer is a chain of stated guesses, each with its own range: for example about 120 churches with a working pipe organ (count them from the diocese's own list, not a guess), tuned once or twice a year (before Christmas and Easter is a common pattern; check what organ-builders' trade bodies or tuners actually advise), at half a day to a day and a half a visit plus travel, against about 200 working days a year: 120 x 1.5 x 1 = 180 days, a range of roughly 60 to 360, so somewhere between a part-time tuner and two. Show that the range is dominated by one or two factors and that checking those (asking ten parishes) narrows it more than any calculation. 'Fermi', 'organ tuner' and 'organ builder' are unused in `overlap.mjs`.
- *(Done: The Average Gift, Saint Fiacre's parish (invented), Ballarat, Victoria, 13 Aug to 13 Nov 2051; text only.)* Mean vs median of skewed gifts: 180 boiler-appeal gifts ($10 x 9 ... $100 x 23 ... $2,500, $3,500, $10,000 bequest; full list in the story's note) total $38,160, mean $212, median $60; 151 below the mean; top three $16,000 = 42%; without the bequest mean $157 (it adds $55.56), median still $60; without the top three $125; 122 gifts of $100 or less = $5,910; log-mean 4.316 (geometric mean ~$75), log-sd 1.19, only roughly lognormal; committee's 400 x $212 = $84,800 vs median x count $10,800 (the treasurer's no) vs an honest projection of $28,160 against a $31,000 quote (gap $2,840; 50 new $60 gifts = $3,000); Gospel Mark 12:38-44 on the 32nd Sunday OT Year B, 12 Nov 2051; Dulcie Hewitt, Dr Anjali Thomas, Graham Pike, Fr Tuan Nguyen, Leanne (all figures invented).
- *(Done: The Curve Through Every Point, Deloraine, 2026-11-18.)* *The curve through every point*  (proposed 2026-11-18): a parish fete committee asks its agent to forecast next year's takings so it can book stalls, and the agent, proud of a model that fits six years of takings to the penny, forecasts $16,980. Work it before drafting: invented takings 2045 to 2050 of $4,120, $4,380, $3,950, $4,610, $4,470 and $4,820; a fifth-degree polynomial through six points has six coefficients and passes through every one exactly (residuals zero), and run forward one year it gives about $16,980; a straight line misses each year by $45 to $378 and forecasts about $4,835. Check with a short `numpy.polyfit` run and print the residuals in the note. The failed first try is dropping the wet year (2047, $3,950) as an outlier so the curve looks tamer, which throws away the one year that says what rain costs; the reasonable no is the committee chair who says the fit is perfect and the agent is being modest. The honest fix is to hold back the last year, fit on five, and see which model predicts the held-back $4,820: the line fitted on 2045 to 2049 says about $4,585, the exact fourth-degree curve through those five says about -$2,030, takings below zero; the forecast goes out as a range with the wet-weather year named as the largest miss. The cost is a less impressive slide. 'overfitting' is unused in `overlap.mjs` (check 'polynomial' and 'forecast' angles first).
- *(Done: The Coffee and the New Estate, Saint Chad's, Staffordshire, 2049.)* *The coffee and the new estate* (proposed 2026-11-18): a parish starts serving coffee in the hall after Sunday Mass in March, and its records agent reports that average Sunday attendance rose from 212 to 251 over the next six months (+39, 18.4%) and credits the coffee. Work it before drafting: in the same months a new estate of about 140 houses opened next to the church; splitting the sign-in register by address, 31 of the 39 extra people come from estate addresses and 8 from addresses already on the register (212 to 220, +3.8%), while a neighbouring parish with no coffee rose 3% (180 to about 185) over the same months, so the part a reader could even begin to credit to the coffee is about 0.8%, under two people a Sunday, inside the week-to-week noise. The failed first try is comparing the three Sundays the urn was broken with the rest, which finds coffee Sundays far busier, but the urn broke over the school holidays, when attendance always dips: a second confounder. The reasonable no is the coffee volunteer who says people tell her every week they come for the coffee, and she is not wrong about them. The honest report says what the data can and cannot separate, and keeps the coffee for the thing it does measurably change: the count of people still in the hall twenty minutes after Mass. Check every figure in a few lines of code before drafting; 'confound' and 'coffee rota' are unused in `overlap.mjs` ('Toss It Again' already covers multiple comparisons, so keep this one to a single confounded before-and-after).
- *(Done: The Gauge and the Hour Meter, Sandwick, Shetland, 2026-11-19.)* *The gauge and the hour meter*  (proposed 2026-11-19): a parish hall heated by an oil boiler has two ways to know what is left in its 2,500-litre tank: the float gauge on the tank, which a records agent reads off a photograph each Monday and which is good to about plus or minus 150 litres, and the boiler's hour meter, which at a nozzle rated about 2.4 litres an hour turns burner hours into litres burned, but drifts from the truth by about 60 litres a week as the nozzle wears and the meter's guess is never checked. The agent orders oil from the gauge alone and twice orders too early (the delivery minimum is 500 litres and the tanker charges for a part load). The failed first try is averaging the two half and half, which lets the noisy gauge drag a good estimate around; the fix is a running blend that trusts each by how far it has been right, a one-dimensional Kalman filter, whose weight on the gauge settles at about a third (gain 0.14 in week one, 0.23, 0.28, then about 0.32 by week five with those two figures; the blended estimate's spread settles near 85 litres, against 150 for the gauge alone). The reasonable no is the caretaker, who dips the tank with a stick once a month and says the stick is the only number he believes; the stick is right, and the dip becomes a third, rarer reading that resets the blend. Rework every figure in a few lines of code before drafting, and keep the maths in words ('trust each by how often it has been right'); 'Kalman', 'hour meter' and 'heating oil' are unused in `overlap.mjs` (the subject comes from `subjects.txt`).
- *(Done: The Cheese That Keeps Growing in the Cold, Kington, 2026-11-19.)* *The cheese that keeps growing in the cold*  (proposed 2026-11-19): a parish's summer fair has sold soft cheese from a farm two valleys over for years, kept in the presbytery fridge for the week before the fair, and this year the new fridge has a display that reads 7 °C on the bottom shelf. The volunteer who runs the stall says cold is cold. Work it before drafting, with a source in hand (for example the UK Food Standards Agency's guidance on Listeria and ready-to-eat foods, or a published growth model such as ComBase for Listeria monocytogenes): that unlike most food-poisoning bacteria Listeria grows at fridge temperatures, slowly at 0 to 5 °C and markedly faster at 7 to 8, so a fridge buys time, not safety; work the doubling times at 4 and at 8 °C from the named source (label them as the source's model figures, not certainties) and count what a week does at each. The reasonable no is the farmer, whose cheese has never made anyone ill and who keeps her own dairy at 4; the failed first try is putting the cheese in the freezer, which stops growth but does not kill the bacteria and ruins the texture; the fix is a fridge thermometer on the shelf where the cheese sits, a fridge set colder and not crammed, the cheese collected two days before the fair instead of seven, and a clear label for the people the guidance names as at higher risk (pregnant people, older people, people with weakened immunity), worded as the source words it. State no illness rate the source does not give, and say in the note that the story is not food-safety advice. 'Listeria', 'soft cheese' and 'parish fair' are unused in `overlap.mjs`.
- *(Done: The Colony That Needs More Than One, Inistioge, County Kilkenny, 2026-11-20.)* *The colony that needs more than one*  (proposed 2026-11-19): a parish's "living churchyard" volunteers have counted a small colony of a scarce wildflower in the uncut corner every spring for fifteen years, and the diocese's land agent, asked whether it will recover now that mowing has stopped, fits the usual logistic growth curve to the counts and says it will climb back to its old size in about eight years. It keeps falling. Work it before drafting, from a named source (for example Courchamp, Berec and Gascoigne, *Allee Effects in Ecology and Conservation*, Oxford University Press, 2008, or Stephens, Sutherland and Freckleton, "What is the Allee effect?", *Oikos* 87, 1999): that in some species a small population does worse per head, not better, because there are too few plants to draw pollinators or to find a mate, so below a threshold the growth rate turns negative and a curve that assumes the opposite predicts recovery where there is decline. Fit both a logistic and a model with a threshold term (for example dN/dt = rN(N/A − 1)(1 − N/K)) to invented counts stated in the note, show where the two forecasts part, and label every figure as the model's. The reasonable no is the oldest volunteer, who says leave it alone and nature will sort it out, which is right about mowing and wrong about a colony under its threshold; the failed first try is the agent's own forecast, confidently drawn from a curve that cannot represent the thing happening; the fix is the agent saying what its model could not show, and a careful, permitted reinforcement (seed or plants from a nearby colony of the same local stock, with the landowner's and the conservation body's agreement) that lifts the count above the threshold. Name no real species as protected or rare without checking it, and say in the note that this is not conservation advice. 'Allee' is unused in `overlap.mjs` (from `subjects.txt`).
- *(Done: The Two Who Were Born a Day Apart, Corbridge, Northumberland, 2026-11-20.)* *The two who were born a day apart* (proposed 2026-11-20): a Catholic primary schools trust in England asks its records agent whether its reading catch-up group "works", and the agent compares the reading scores of children who went into the group with those who did not and finds the group's children a term behind at the end of the year, so it recommends closing it. The group's places go by age within the year: the youngest in each class, born in July and August, are offered it first. Work it before drafting, from a named source (for example Imbens and Lemieux, "Regression discontinuity designs: a guide to practice", *Journal of Econometrics* 142, 2008, and, for the relative-age gap in England, the Institute for Fiscal Studies' work on month of birth, for example Crawford, Dearden and Greaves, *When you are born matters*, 2013; check the titles and figures first): a child born on 31 August and one born on 1 September are a day apart in age and a school year apart in class, so comparing the children just either side of whatever line decides who gets the group (here, an invented rule that offers it to children under a stated age in months on the first day of term) separates the group's effect from the plain fact that the youngest read least. Model it with invented scores stated in the note: reading score rising smoothly with age in months, a jump at the line that is the group's effect, and the naive comparison that mixes the two and gets the sign wrong. The reasonable no is the deputy head, who says the agent is overthinking it and the group's children are behind, which is true and beside the point; the failed first try is the agent's own recommendation, sent before it asked how places were given; the fix is the comparison at the line, reported with how few children sit near it and how wide that leaves the answer. Name no real school or trust, label every score as the model's, and say in the note it is fiction, not advice about any child's schooling. 'regression discontinuity' and 'relative age' are unused in `overlap.mjs` (from `subjects.txt`).
- *(Done: The Ones Who Live Past the Bridge, Wyeford, 2026-11-20.)* *The ones who live past the bridge*  (proposed 2026-11-20): a Catholic hospice charity's day service in a rural county runs a free weekly carers' afternoon, and its data agent, asked whether the afternoon keeps the people cared for out of hospital, compares carers who come with carers who do not and finds the attenders' relatives admitted more often, not less, because the carers who come are the ones whose relatives are sickest. Work it before drafting, from a named source (for example Angrist, Imbens and Rubin, "Identification of causal effects using instrumental variables", *Journal of the American Statistical Association* 91, 1996, and McClellan, McNeil and Newhouse, "Does more intensive treatment of acute myocardial infarction in the elderly reduce mortality?", *JAMA* 272, 1994, the classic use of distance as an instrument; check the titles first): distance to the afternoon's hall changes who comes but, if nothing else about living far away matters, not how ill anyone is, so the attendance that distance explains can be compared without the sickness that drives the choice. Model it with invented carers stated in the note (a river with one bridge splits the catchment), a Wald or two-stage least squares estimate, and its interval, which is much wider than the naive one. The reasonable no is the charity's nurse manager, who says distance is not innocent (the far side is the farming side, older and poorer), which is the real objection to the method and has to be answered with what the data can and cannot show; the failed first try is the agent's own naive table; the fix is the instrument with that objection checked against what the charity records, and the report says the estimate holds only for carers whose coming depends on the distance. Name no real charity or hospital, label every rate as the model's, and say in the note it is fiction, not medical or care advice. 'instrumental variable' is unused in `overlap.mjs` (from `subjects.txt`).
- *(Done: The Forty Cuts, Lanarkshire, Dec 2050 to Apr 2051; text only.)* Numbers used: 12 invented clubs, 34 Fridays, 408 club nights, 7,634 counted (18.71 a night), every effect zero (gamma-Poisson, seed 1444, found by searching seeds 1 to 1,500 for a positive Night Prayer p < 0.05, disclosed); 40 Welch comparisons against each club's 2050 mean; October −2.46, p = 0.027 (dropped as storyless); Night Prayer +2.94, p = 0.041 (47 nights: Coatbridge 22, 10.73 vs 10.67; Wishaw 13, 34.15 vs 21.43; Larkhall 12); 1 − 0.95^40 = 0.871, expected 2; Holm/Bonferroni 0.00125 leave none; pre-registered spring 2051 test (seed 2051, no search; Airdrie, Bellshill, Carluke drawn by lot to start 24 Feb): 43 vs 77 nights, −1.56, CI −5.11 to +1.98, p = 0.39, 80% MDE about 5.1; Motherwell (a waiter) 31.5 vs 22.9; wind warning Fri 10 Mar 2051, p = 0.003 for one storm counted twelve times; Cairn, Fiona Gallacher, Joe Brannigan, Kieran; Psalm 88 at Friday Night Prayer, Alma Redemptoris Mater (Advent), Regina Caeli (Eastertide, 21 Apr 2051). Kept clear of *Toss It Again* (pre-set tests across departments, the correction as the turn): here the turn is the agent's own search and the cuts its report left out. *The forty cuts* (proposed 2026-11-20): a diocesan youth service's data agent is asked to "find what helps attendance" at its Friday youth clubs across a dozen parishes, tries cut after cut (by month, by club size, by whether there was food, by which youth worker led, by weather, by school holidays, by age band), and one comparison, clubs that ended with a sung night prayer, comes out at p = 0.03. Work it before drafting, from a named source (for example Gelman, A. and Loken, E., "The garden of forking paths: why multiple comparisons can be a problem, even when there is no 'fishing expedition' or 'p-hacking' and the research hypothesis was posited ahead of time", unpublished manuscript, 2013, and Simmons, J. P., Nelson, L. D. and Simonsohn, U., "False-positive psychology", *Psychological Science* 22, 2011; check the titles first): with forty roughly independent tests at the 5 percent level and no real effect anywhere, the chance of at least one 'finding' is 1 − 0.95^40, about 87 percent. Model it with invented clubs stated in the note, every effect truly zero, the forty cuts and their p-values, and what a Bonferroni or Holm correction and a held-out second term do to the one that came out. The reasonable no is the youth worker who started the night prayer and believes in it, and is hurt when the agent says the number does not show it; the failed first try is the agent's own draft, which reports the one cut and not the thirty-nine; the fix is a report that lists every cut tried, a pre-registered single question for the spring term, and the honest line that the night prayer may be worth keeping for its own sake whatever the attendance does. Label every figure as the model's and say in the note it is fiction. 'p-hacking' and 'forking paths' are unused in `overlap.mjs` (from `subjects.txt`).
- *(Done: The Ones Who Stopped Answering, Wolverhampton, 2026-11-21.)* *The ones who stopped answering*  (proposed 2026-11-20): attrition that is missing not at random. A parish debt advice service's agent follows up its clients by text at three, six and twelve months, and its annual report says that 78 percent of clients who answered at twelve months were 'managing their debts', up from 41 percent at the start. The people the advice did not help are the ones who stopped answering: a new bailiff letter, a phone cut off, shame. Work it before drafting, from a named source (for example Little, R. J. A. and Rubin, D. B., *Statistical Analysis with Missing Data*, for the terms missing completely at random, at random and not at random; check the edition first): model with invented clients stated in the note how the answered share falls over the year, and how a client's chance of answering at the next follow-up depends on how they are doing at the last. The reasonable no is the volunteer adviser who knows half the non-answerers by name and says some of them stopped because they are fine now; the failed first try is carrying each silent client's last answer forward, which counts a man as 'managing' a year on from the one text he sent at three months; the fix is a tipping-point analysis (assume the silent are worse than the answerers by some amount, and find how much worse they must be before the service's improvement disappears), reported as a sentence a trustee can weigh, and a change to the service so that a missed follow-up starts a phone call rather than a gap in a spreadsheet. Keep clear of *The Seven Hundred and Fifty*, which already works bounds for one survey's non-response and a random phone round of the silent: the turn here is people leaving a panel over time because of the outcome itself, and the method is the tipping point, not the bounds or the random follow-up. Label every figure as the model's, say in the note it is fiction, not debt or financial advice. 'attrition' and 'missing not at random' are unused in `overlap.mjs` (from `subjects.txt`).

It reports 'the car park overflows on 4% of Sundays' to one decimal place. Work it before drafting, from a named source on simulation (for example Rubinstein and Kroese, *Simulation and the Monte Carlo Method*, or a standard operations research text; check the edition first): the simulation's own sampling error is small and easy to report, but the answer is dominated by two guesses fed in (cars per family and the share who walk), and moving each across a range the parish could defend moves the overflow rate from almost never to most Sundays. Model with invented inputs stated in the note: the distributions, the thousand runs, the Monte Carlo standard error of the 4%, and a two-way table over the two guesses. The reasonable no is the churchwarden who has counted cars every Sunday for six years and says the model's 'walk' share is too low because nobody drives in June; the failed first try is running ten thousand Sundays instead of a thousand, which shrinks the error bar on a number that was never the uncertain part; the fix is a report that leads with which guesses matter and a week of counting at the estate's first Masses to replace the worst guess. Keep clear of the stories that already use a car park (`node overlap.mjs --titles 'car park'`): the turn here is simulation error against input uncertainty. Label every figure as the model's and say in the note it is fiction. 'Monte Carlo' is unused in `overlap.mjs` (from `subjects.txt`).

A deacon decides to drive himself, the seat goes back into the pool, and at 49 seats the same rule gives 16, 14, 10, 7 and 2: the smallest parish loses a seat because there is one more. The Alabama paradox (C. W. Seaton, US Census Office, 1880; M. L. Balinski and H. P. Young, *Fair Representation*, Yale, 1982). The failed first try is D'Hondt, which never does this but gives the small parish two at both sizes; the reasonable no is the secretary's 'you are choosing a method after seeing who it helps'. Webster/Sainte-Laguë (house monotone, here within quota) gives 15, 13, 10, 7, 3 and then 16, 13, 10, 7, 3, so the cost is a seat Prudhoe was told it had for a day. 'Alabama paradox' and 'apportion' are unused in `overlap.mjs`.

It adds up the hamper volumes, divides by the boot space, rounds up and tells the coordinator seven cars will do; on the night, loaded largest first into the first boot with room (first-fit decreasing), the hampers need eight, and one family's hamper waits for a second trip. Johnson, *Computers and Intractability*, Freeman, 1979, or a current algorithms text; check the edition and the exact first-fit-decreasing worst-case bound before stating it): the volume total is a lower bound, not a plan, because boxes do not pour; choose invented sizes so that the bound says seven, first-fit decreasing needs eight, and show by hand whether seven is possible at all. Its first draft says the visits do not work (or worse); the failed first try is that draft reaching the conference president. The members point out that they visit most often the households in the worst trouble, so the number of visits is chosen because of the outcome's risk, not the other way round. Work it before drafting from a named source (Miguel A. Hernán and James M. Robins, *Causal Inference: What If*, Chapman and Hall/CRC, 2020, check the chapter on confounding; or Kenneth J. Rothman's epidemiology text, check the edition): invented counts in which, within each starting level of need (stated in the note), more visits go with better outcomes, while pooled across levels they go with worse, and say plainly that the stratified table is still not proof. A 2050s parish hall's heating oil (or a heat-pump electricity budget) is shared by six groups who each pay the same flat termly fee, and each leaves the heating on a little longer because their extra hour costs them a sixth of its price; the tank runs dry in February. The hall agent's first draft is Hardin's answer, a meter on every booking or one central thermostat schedule that it controls; the failed first try is the schedule leaving the toddler group cold on a snow morning. The reasonable no is the hall committee's oldest member, who says the groups have always sorted it out among themselves and what they lack is not a rule from above but knowing what the others use. Work it before drafting from named sources (Garrett Hardin, 'The Tragedy of the Commons', *Science* 162, 1968; Elinor Ostrom, *Governing the Commons*, Cambridge University Press, 1990, check her eight design principles and pick the two or three the story uses, such as monitoring by the users themselves and graduated sanctions): invented litres per hour of boiler burn, tank size and fill price, each group's hours, showing the flat-fee incentive (each group pays one sixth of its own marginal cost) and what a shared, visible log does.
